Figure 1.

Uniqueness and non-uniqueness regimes for probability solutions in

C_{t}L^{q}
to the three-dimensional Keller–Segel equation with external drift

v\in C_{t}L^{r}
.

The red region corresponds to the expected well-posedness regime: for
r>3

and
\frac{1}{q}\leqslant\frac{1}{3}+\frac{1}{r}
, uniqueness is expected in the corresponding
subcritical class.

The green region corresponds to the non-uniqueness regime
obtained in this paper: for
1<r<3
and

\frac{1}{q}\geqslant 1-\frac{1}{r}
, there exists a divergence-free drift

v\in C_{t}L^{r}
for which the equation admits multiple probability solutions.

The point
(\frac{1}{3},\frac{2}{3})
marks the sharp threshold separating these
two regimes.

2.5.
The 2D vorticity Navier–Stokes equation

We next consider the two-dimensional case
d=2
. Let

V\in L^{2,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{2})
and take

\sigma=\operatorname{Id}
. Then (
1.3
) becomes

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho-\Delta\rho+\operatorname{div}\bigl((V*\rho)\rho\bigr)+\operatorname{div}(v\rho)=0.

The corresponding DDSDE is

\displaystyle{\mathord{{\rm d}}}X_{t}=v(t,X_{t})\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}t+(V*\rho_{t})(X_{t})\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}t+\sqrt{2}\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}W_{t}.

In the case
v\equiv 0
, the existence and conditional uniqueness of the
corresponding SDE and Fokker–Planck equation have been studied in

[
Kry20a
,
Kry20b
,
RZZ25
]
.

In particular, if we choose the Biot–Savart kernel

\displaystyle V=\nabla^{\perp}G\in L^{2,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{2}),

where
G
is the Green function on the torus, then the equation reduces to the
two-dimensional vorticity Navier–Stokes equation with an additional drift:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho-\Delta\rho+\operatorname{div}\bigl((\nabla^{\perp}G*\rho)\rho\bigr)+\operatorname{div}(v\rho)=0.

The two-dimensional vorticity Navier–Stokes equation is a fundamental model in
fluid mechanics and turbulence theory, describing the evolution of the
vorticity of an incompressible fluid. The well-posedness theory for the
two-dimensional vorticity Navier–Stokes equation in the class
C_{t}L^{1}
is now
classical; see, for instance,
[
MB03
,
BA94
,
GG05
]
. The probabilistic formulation of the 2D vorticity
Navier–Stokes equation in terms of nonlinear McKean–Vlasov SDEs has been
studied, for instance, in
[
BRZ25
,
RZZ25
]
.

On the other hand, by Theorem
1.3
, there exists a
divergence-free drift field

v\in C_{t}L^{2-}

such that the above vorticity equation with the additional drift admits
infinitely many probability solutions in the class
C_{t}L^{1}
, all starting from
the stationary density
\rho_{0}\equiv 1
.

2.6.
Landau-type equation on
\mathbb{T}^{d}
,
d\geqslant 3

We next consider a Landau-type equation on the torus. Let

\sigma(\rho)=\sqrt{a*\rho},V=2\mathord{{\rm div}}a,

where the matrix-valued kernel
a
has the Coulomb-type singularity

\displaystyle a(x)=\left(\operatorname{Id}-\frac{x\otimes x}{|x|^{2}}\right)|x|^{2-d}

near the origin. More precisely,
a
is understood as a periodic
matrix-valued function on
\mathbb{T}^{d}
which coincides with the above
kernel in a neighborhood of the origin.

With this choice, equation (
1.3
) becomes the following
Landau-type equation:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho-\operatorname{div}\left[(a*\rho)\nabla\rho-(a*\nabla\rho)\rho\right]+\operatorname{div}(v\rho)=0.

The corresponding DDSDE is formally given by

\displaystyle{\mathord{{\rm d}}}X_{t}=v(t,X_{t})\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}t+2(a*\nabla\rho_{t})(X_{t})\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}t+\sqrt{2(a*\rho_{t})(X_{t})}\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}W_{t}.

The Landau equation is a fundamental kinetic model describing the evolution of
dilute plasmas under Coulomb-type interactions, where the dynamics are
dominated by the accumulation of grazing collisions. Classical references
include
[
Lan36
,
Vil98
,
Vil02
]
. In the genuine kinetic setting, the variable

x
here should be interpreted as the velocity variable. From this viewpoint,
it is not physically natural to impose periodicity in the velocity variable.
However, due to the limitations of our current method, we restrict ourselves
to the periodic setting on the torus.

Since
a\in L^{\frac{d}{d-2},\infty}
, the assumptions of Theorem
1.4
and
Theorem
1.5
are satisfied with
\gamma=2
, provided

1<\frac{d}{2}<d_{0}^{\prime},
i.e.
d_{0}<\frac{d}{d-2}
.
Consequently, there exists a divergence-free drift field

v\in C_{t}L^{d_{0}}

in the above range such that the corresponding Landau-type
equation admits non-unique weak solutions. Moreover, by applying
Theorem
1.5
, one can also construct a time-independent
divergence-free drift
for which the equation admits non-unique
stationary probability solutions.

2.7.
Further discussion

Our approach is flexible and is expected to be adaptable to a much broader
class of diffusion mechanisms than those treated in the present paper. With
suitable modifications, one may hope to extend the construction to nonlinear
diffusion operators such as the
p
-Laplacian

\operatorname{div}\bigl(|\nabla\rho|^{p-2}\nabla\rho\bigr),

nonlocal diffusion operators such as the fractional Laplacian

(-\Delta)^{\alpha}
, and genuinely degenerate porous-medium diffusions such as

\Delta\rho^{m}.

These operators arise naturally in a variety of physical models, including
non-Newtonian fluids, transport in heterogeneous media, and anomalous
diffusion. We do not pursue these extensions here, since they would require
additional estimates adapted to the specific structure of each diffusion
operator.

In this paper, we restrict our attention to the torus and whole-space settings.
Nevertheless, the method is expected to be adaptable to more general domains
equipped with suitable boundary conditions. A main additional ingredient would
be the use of Bogovskii operators
[
Bog79
]
, which provide a convenient
tool for solving divergence equations while taking boundary conditions into
account. The presence of boundaries, however, also introduces new technical
difficulties.

3.
Notations and Preliminaries

Let
T>0
,
{\mathbb{N}}_{0}:={\mathbb{N}}\cup\{0\}
. Throughout the manuscript, we write
{\mathbb{T}}^{d}={\mathbb{R}}^{d}/{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
for the
d
-dimensional flat torus.
We employ the notation
a\lesssim b
if there exists a constant
c>0
such that
a\leqslant cb
. Given a Banach space
E
with a norm
\|\cdot\|_{E}
, we write
C_{t}E=C([0,T];E)
for the space of continuous functions from
[0,T]
to
E
, equipped with the supremum norm. For
p\in[1,\infty]
we write
L^{p}_{t}E=L^{p}([0,T];E)
for the space of
L^{p}
-integrable functions from
[0,T]
to
E
, equipped with the usual
L^{p}
-norm.
For
\alpha\in(0,1)
we define
C^{\alpha}_{t}E
as the space of
\alpha
-Hölder continuous functions from
[0,T]
to
E
, endowed with the norm
\|f\|_{C^{\alpha}_{t}E}=\sup_{s,t\in[0,T],s\neq t}\frac{\|f(s)-f(t)\|_{E}}{|t-s|^{\alpha}}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|f(t)\|_{E},
and write
C_{t}^{\alpha}
in the case when
E=\mathbb{R}
.
We use
L^{p}
to denote the set of standard
L^{p}
-integrable functions on
\mathbb{T}^{d}
.
For
s>0
,
p>1
we set
W^{s,p}:=\{f\in L^{p};\|(I-\Delta)^{\frac{s}{2}}f\|_{L^{p}}<\infty\}
with the norm
\|f\|_{W^{s,p}}=\|(I-\Delta)^{\frac{s}{2}}f\|_{L^{p}}
. We use
L^{p,\infty}
to denote the Lorentz space

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{p,\infty}}:=\sup_{\lambda>0}\lambda\,|\{x\in\mathbb{T}^{d}:\ |f(x)|>\lambda\}|^{1/p}.

For
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
C_{x}^{N}
denotes the space of
N
-times differentiable functions equipped with the norm

\|f\|_{C_{x}^{N}}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha|\leqslant N,\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d}_{0}\end{subarray}}\|D^{\alpha}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}.

Similarly, if the norm is taken in space-time, we use
C^{N}_{t,x}
.

For a matrix-valued function

A=(A^{ij})_{1\leqslant i,j\leqslant d},

we define

(\mathord{{\rm div}}A)_{i}:=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\partial_{j}A^{ij}.

We define the projection
\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}f:=f-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}f{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x
.
We denote by
{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x
the Lebesgue measure on the torus.
We denote by
\mathcal{L}
the law of random variable.

3.1.
Some technical tools

We collect some technical tools used in the construction of convex integration schemes.

We define
\mathcal{R}_{1}:=\nabla\Delta^{-1}
as a right
inverse of the
\mathord{{\rm div}}
operator, i.e.
\mathord{{\rm div}}(\mathcal{R}_{1}v)=v
for scalars
v
with
\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}v{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x=0
. Here we use the notation
\mathcal{R}_{1}v:=\mathcal{R}_{1}(v-\int v{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x)
for a general scalar function
v
. Then since
\mathcal{R}_{1}
is a Calderón-Zygmund operator, we have

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
1\leqslant p\leqslant\infty
. For any vector field

f\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
,
\sigma\in\mathbb{N}
,

\|\mathcal{R}_{1}f(\sigma\cdot)\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim\sigma^{-1}\|f\|_{L^{p}}.

We introduce the bilinear version
\mathcal{B}_{1}:C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})\times C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})\to C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})
by

\mathcal{B}_{1}(v,f)=v\mathcal{R}_{1}f-\mathcal{R}_{1}{\Big(}\nabla v\cdot\mathcal{R}_{1}f+\int vf{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x{\Big)}.

Lemma 3.2
.

(
[
BCDL21
, Lemma 3.3]
)

Let
1\leqslant p\leqslant\infty
. For any
v\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
and
f\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
, we have
\mathord{{\rm div}}(\mathcal{B}_{1}(v,f))=vf-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}vf{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x,
and for
\sigma\in\mathbb{N}
,

\|\mathcal{B}_{1}(v,f(\sigma\cdot))\|_{W^{k,p}}\lesssim\sigma^{k-1}\|v\|_{C^{k+1}}\|f\|_{W^{k,p}}.

Then we introduce the improved Hölder’s inequality by using the additional decorrelation between frequencies.

Lemma 3.3
.

(
[
CL22a
, Theorem B.1]
)

Let
d\geqslant 2,p\in[1,\infty]
and
a,f:\mathbb{T}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
be smooth functions. Then for any
\sigma\in\mathbb{N}
,

|\|af(\sigma\cdot)\|_{L^{p}}-\|a\|_{L^{p}}\|f\|_{L^{p}}|\lesssim\sigma^{-1/p}\|a\|_{C^{1}}\|f\|_{L^{p}}.

Finally, we record the following weak Young inequality.

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
d\geqslant 2
and let
1<p,q,r<\infty
satisfy

\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{r}-1>0.

Then, for all
f\in L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
and

g\in L^{r,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, one has

(3.1)

\displaystyle\|f*g\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\|g\|_{L^{r,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}.

4.
Infinitely many evolutions from a stationary state

In this section, we prove our main result, Theorem
1.3
.
We first establish the first statement at the PDE level.
For any fixed diffusion coefficient
\sigma
satisfying Assumption
1.2
and (
1.4
), we construct a distribution-independent divergence-free drift
v\in L^{r}_{t}L^{p}\cap C_{t}L^{s}

such that the corresponding nonlinear Fokker–Planck equation (
1.3
) admits infinitely many distinct positive solutions
\rho^{i}\in C_{t}L^{1}
, satisfying moreover
v\rho^{i}\in L^{1}_{t}L^{1}.

To achieve this, we apply a convex integration scheme to the Fokker–Planck equation (
1.3
).
The overall iterative procedure follows the framework developed in previous works such as
[
LRZ25
,
LR25
]
.
However, the present setting requires handling general diffusion operators together with the construction of infinitely many distinct solutions.
Accordingly, in the sequel we mainly focus on the new ideas and techniques specific to this setting, while only briefly discussing those parts of the argument that are analogous to earlier works.

4.1.
Preparations

From now on, we write

A(\rho):=\sigma\sigma^{T}(\rho).

The following lemma collects several estimates that will be used later.

Lemma 4.1
.

Under Assumption
1.2
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|A(\rho)

\displaystyle-A(\tilde{\rho})\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\lesssim\|\rho-\tilde{\rho}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}+\|\rho-\tilde{\rho}\|_{C_{t,x}^{0}}(\|\rho\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}+\|\tilde{\rho}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}),

\displaystyle\|A(\rho)\|_{C_{t,x}^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1,\ \ \|A(\rho)\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\lesssim 1+\|\rho\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}},\ \ \|A(\rho)\|_{C_{t,x}^{2}}\lesssim 1+\|\rho\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}^{2}+\|\rho\|_{C_{t,x}^{2}}.

Proof.

We first consider case (1). Since

\sigma(t,x,\rho)=\sigma_{1}(t,x,\rho_{t}(x))

and

\sigma_{1}\in C_{b}^{2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}),

we have for
0\leqslant i\leqslant d
(
\partial_{0}
is regarded as the derivative to time
t
),

\displaystyle|\partial_{i}\sigma(t,x,\rho)

\displaystyle-\partial_{i}\sigma(t,x,\tilde{\rho})|

\displaystyle\lesssim|(\partial_{i}\sigma_{1})(t,x,\rho)-(\partial_{i}\sigma_{1})(t,x,\tilde{\rho})|+|(\partial_{\rho}\sigma_{1})(t,x,\rho)\partial_{i}\rho-(\partial_{\rho}\sigma_{1})(t,x,\tilde{\rho})\partial_{i}\tilde{\rho}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\rho-\tilde{\rho}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}+\|\rho-\tilde{\rho}\|_{C_{t,x}^{0}}\|\rho\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}},

\displaystyle\|\sigma(t,x,\rho)\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\|\rho\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}},

\displaystyle\|\sigma(t,x,\rho)\|_{C_{t,x}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\|\rho\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}^{2}+\|\rho\|_{C_{t,x}^{2}}.

Next, in case (2), it suffices to consider the case that
\sigma(\rho)=\sigma_{2}*\rho
. Young’s inequality yields

\displaystyle\|\sigma(t,x,\rho)-\sigma(t,x,\tilde{\rho})\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\sigma_{2}\|_{C_{t}^{1}C_{x}^{0}}\|\rho_{t}-\tilde{\rho}_{t}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}},

\displaystyle\|\sigma(t,x,\rho)\|_{C_{t,x}^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\sigma_{2}\|_{C_{t,x}^{0}}\|\rho_{t}\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim 1,

\displaystyle\|\sigma(t,x,\rho)\|_{C_{t,x}^{i}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\|\rho\|_{C_{t,x}^{i}},\ i=1,2.

Case (3) follows directly by combining the estimates from the previous two cases.
The corresponding estimates for
A
then follow immediately.
∎

4.2.
The iteration procedure

Let
0<\epsilon_{0}\leqslant 1
be given. Without loss of generality, we may replace
\epsilon_{0}
by

\epsilon_{0}\wedge\frac{1}{4}.
The iteration is indexed by a parameter

q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}.

We consider an increasing sequence

\{\lambda_{q}\}_{q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\subset\mathbb{N}

and a sequence

\{\delta_{q}\}_{q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\subset(0,1]

defined by

\displaystyle\lambda_{q}=a^{b^{q}},\ q\geqslant 0,\ \delta_{q}=(\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2})^{d+1}\lambda_{1}^{2\beta}\lambda_{q}^{-2\beta},\ q\geqslant 1,\ \delta_{0}=1,

where
\beta>0
will be chosen sufficiently small, while
a,b
will be chosen sufficiently large.
Moreover, by imposing

(4.1)

\displaystyle a^{2(b-1)\beta/(d+1)}>2,

we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{q\geqslant 1}\delta_{q}^{1/(d+1)}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2}\sum_{q\geqslant 1}a^{(1-q)2(b-1)\beta/(d+1)}\leqslant\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2}\cdot\frac{1}{1-a^{-2(b-1)\beta/(d+1)}}<\epsilon_{0}.

Without loss of generality, we assume
T=1
from now on.
At each step
q
, we construct a family

(v_{q},\rho_{q}^{i},M_{q}^{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}

solving the system

(4.2)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho_{q}^{i}-\mathord{{\rm div}}\mathord{{\rm div}}\bigl(A(\rho_{q}^{i})\rho_{q}^{i}\bigr)+\mathord{{\rm div}}(v_{q}\rho_{q}^{i})+\mathord{{\rm div}}((V*\rho_{q}^{i})\rho_{q}^{i})

\displaystyle=-\mathord{{\rm div}}M_{q}^{i},

\displaystyle\mathord{{\rm div}}v_{q}

\displaystyle=0.

The sequence
\{\rho_{q}^{i}\}_{q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
converges to a limit
\rho^{i}
, while the stress fields
\{M_{q}^{i}\}_{q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
converge to
0
in a suitable topology as
q\to\infty
.

To incorporate the initial condition, we require that

\rho_{q}^{i}=1
on
[0,T_{q}]
, where

T_{q}:=\frac{1}{3}-\sum_{1\leqslant r\leqslant q}\delta_{r}^{1/2}.

By (
4.1
), we have

0<T_{q}\leqslant\frac{1}{3}.

Here and throughout the paper, we use the convention

\sum_{1\leqslant r\leqslant 0}:=0.

Let
\alpha\in(0,1)
be fixed later.
For each
q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, we define

N_{q}:={\mathord{{\rm min}}}\bigl\{i\in\mathbb{N}:2^{i}\geqslant\lambda_{q}^{\alpha}\bigr\},

which satisfies

\lambda_{q}^{\alpha}\leqslant 2^{N_{q}}<2\lambda_{q}^{\alpha}.

At the iteration step
q+1
, we construct new perturbations to eliminate the first
N_{q+1}
stress terms

M_{q}^{i}
,
1\leqslant i\leqslant N_{q+1}
,
while ensuring that the remaining stress terms remain sufficiently small.

We initialize the iteration by setting

\displaystyle\rho_{0}^{i}(t,x)

\displaystyle=1+\frac{\sin 2\pi x_{1}}{4^{i}}\chi_{0}(t),\ v_{0}=\overline{v},

\displaystyle M_{0}^{i}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}\chi_{0}(t)\frac{\cos 2\pi x_{1}}{4^{i}\cdot 2\pi}(1,0,\dots,0)-\overline{v}(\rho_{0}^{i}-1)

\displaystyle\ +\mathord{{\rm div}}(A(t,x,\rho_{0}^{i})\rho_{0}^{i}-A(t,x,1))-(V*\rho_{0}^{i})\rho_{0}^{i}+V*1,

where
x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})
and
\chi_{0}
is a smooth function satisfying

\chi_{0}(t)=0
on
[0,\frac{1}{3}],\chi_{0}(t)=1
on
[\frac{2}{3},1].
It is easy to verify that
(v_{0},\rho^{i}_{0},M^{i}_{0})_{i\in{\mathbb{N}}}
is a solution to (
4.2
) by using the facts that
\mathord{{\rm div}}\overline{v}=0
,
\mathord{{\rm div}}\mathord{{\rm div}}(A(t,x,1))=0
and
\mathord{{\rm div}}(V*1)=0
. By definition, we know that
\rho_{0}^{i}=1
on
[0,T_{0}]
, and then
M_{0}^{i}=0
on
[0,T_{0}]
.

Then we have by weak Young’s inequality

\displaystyle\|\mathord{{\rm div}}(A(t,x,\rho_{0}^{i})\rho_{0}^{i}-A(t,x,1))\|_{C_{t,x}^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|A(t,x,\rho_{0}^{i})-A(t,x,1)\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}+\|A(t,x,\rho_{0}^{i})(\rho_{0}^{i}-1)\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\rho_{0}^{i}-1\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\lesssim 4^{-i},

\displaystyle\|(V*\rho_{0}^{i})\rho_{0}^{i}-V*1\|_{L_{t}^{1}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(V*\rho_{0}^{i}-V*1)\rho_{0}^{i}\|_{L_{t}^{1}L^{1}}+\|(V*1)(\rho_{0}^{i}-1)\|_{L_{t}^{1}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\rho_{0}^{i}-1\|_{C_{t,x}^{0}}\lesssim 4^{-i}.

By choosing
C_{0}>0
sufficiently large (depending on
\epsilon_{0}
), we obtain by Lemma
4.1

(4.3)

\displaystyle\|\rho_{0}^{i}-1\|_{C_{t,x}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant 4^{-i}C_{0}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}},\ \ \|v_{0}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\leqslant C_{0}^{1/d_{0}},\ \ \|M_{0}^{i}\|_{L_{t}^{1}L^{1}}\lesssim 4^{-i}\leqslant(\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2})^{d+1}4^{-i}C_{0}.

With the above assumptions in hand, our main iteration relies on the first step of iteration and reads as follows:

Proposition 4.2
.

Under the assumption of Theorem
1.3
, there exist
d+1>d_{0}>2>d_{0}^{\prime}>1
with
\frac{1}{d_{0}}+\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}=1
and a choice of parameters
a,b,\alpha,\beta
such
that the following holds: Let
(v_{q},\rho^{i}_{q},M^{i}_{q})_{i\in{\mathbb{N}}}
be a solution to the system (
4.2
) satisfying
\int\rho^{i}_{q}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x=1
,

(4.4)

\displaystyle\|v_{q}\|_{L^{d_{0}}_{t}L^{d_{0}}}\leqslant C_{v}C_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\sum_{m=0}^{q}\delta_{m}^{1/d_{0}},

for some universal constant
C_{v}\geqslant 1
, and

(4.5)

\displaystyle\|v_{q}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\leqslant C_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\lambda_{q}^{d+4},\ \|\rho^{i}_{q}-1\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}+\lambda_{q}^{-2}\|\rho^{i}_{q}-1\|_{C_{t,x}^{2}}\leqslant 4^{-i}C_{0}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\lambda_{q}^{d+4},

(4.6)

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{q}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{1}}\leqslant C_{0}2^{-i}\delta_{q+1},

(4.7)

\displaystyle M^{i}_{q}=M^{i}_{0}-(v_{q}-v_{0})(\rho^{i}_{0}-1),\ \rho_{q}^{i}=\rho_{0}^{i},\ {\rm for\ }i>N_{q},

(4.8)

\displaystyle\rho^{i}_{q}(t)=1,M^{i}_{q}(t)=0\ {\rm on}\ [0,T_{q}].

Then there exists

(v_{q+1},\rho^{i}_{q+1},M^{i}_{q+1})_{i\in{\mathbb{N}}}
which solves (
4.2
) and satisfies (
4.4
)-(
4.8
) at the level
q+1
and

(4.9)

\displaystyle\|v_{q+1}-v_{q}\|_{L^{d_{0}}_{t}L^{d_{0}}}\leqslant C_{v}C_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}},\ \ \|\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}_{t}L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\leqslant C_{v}C_{0}^{1/{d_{0}^{\prime}}}2^{-i/d_{0}^{\prime}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/{d_{0}^{\prime}}}.

Moreover, we have for some
\epsilon>0
small enough

(4.10)

\displaystyle\|v_{q+1}-v_{q}\|_{L^{r}_{t}L^{p}}+\|v_{q+1}-v_{q}\|_{C_{t}L^{s}}\leqslant\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}},

(4.11)

\displaystyle\|\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{C_{t}L^{1+\epsilon}}+\|\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{L_{t}^{1}W^{1,1+\epsilon}}\leqslant 4^{-i}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}},\ \ \inf_{t\in[0,1]}(\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q})\geqslant-\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}.

The proof of our main iteration Proposition
4.2
will be stated in the following section.

4.3.
Proof of Theorem
1.3

We intend to start the iteration from

(v_{0},\rho^{i}_{0},M^{i}_{0})_{i\in{\mathbb{N}}}
which are defined as above.
By (
4.3
), (
4.4
)-(
4.8
) are satisfied as
\delta_{0}=1,\delta_{1}=(\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2})^{d+1}
.

Next, we use Proposition
4.2
to build inductively
(v_{q},\rho^{i}_{q},M^{i}_{q})_{i\in{\mathbb{N}}}
for every
q\geqslant 1
. By (
4.1
) and (
4.9
)-(
4.11
), the sequence
\{(v_{q},\rho^{i}_{q})_{i\in{\mathbb{N}}}\}_{q\in\mathbb{N}}
is
Cauchy in

\displaystyle{\Big(}L^{r}([0,1];L^{p})\cap L^{d_{0}}([0,1]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d})\cap C([0,1];L^{s}){\Big)}\times{\Big(}L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}([0,1]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d})\cap C([0,1];L^{1+\epsilon})\cap L^{1}([0,1];W^{1,1+\epsilon}){\Big)}^{{\mathbb{N}}}

and we denote by
(v,\rho^{i})
its limit.
By (
4.6
), (
4.8
) and a similar calculation as (
5.21
), it is easy to verify
(\rho^{i},v)
solves (
1.3
). Then we know that
\int\rho^{i}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x=1
.

By (
4.1
), (
4.9
) and (
4.11
) we have

(4.12)

\displaystyle\left|\|\rho^{i}-1\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}-\|\rho^{i}_{0}-1\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}\right|\leqslant\sum_{q=0}^{\infty}\|\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}\leqslant 4^{-i}\sum_{q=0}^{\infty}\delta_{q+1}^{1/{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\leqslant 4^{-i-1},

\displaystyle\inf_{t\in[0,1]}\rho^{i}\geqslant\inf_{t\in[0,1]}\rho_{0}^{i}+\sum_{q=0}^{\infty}\inf_{t\in[0,1]}(\rho_{q+1}^{i}-\rho_{q}^{i})\geqslant 1-\frac{1}{4}-\sum_{q=0}^{\infty}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\geqslant\frac{1}{2},

at which point, together with the fact that
\|\rho^{i}_{0}-1\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}=\frac{2}{\pi 4^{i}}
, we obtain
\rho^{i}\to 1
in
C_{t}L^{1}
as
i\to\infty
, and

\rho^{i}
is nonnegative on
\mathbb{T}^{d}
. Moreover, for
i>j
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|\rho^{i}_{0}-\rho^{j}_{0}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}\geqslant\frac{3}{2\pi 4^{j}},

which together with (
4.12
) implies that
\rho^{i}

do not coincide with each other.

By (
4.1
) and (
4.10
) we obtain that

\displaystyle\|v-\overline{v}\|_{L^{r}_{t}L^{p}}+\|v-\overline{v}\|_{C_{t}L^{s}}\leqslant\sum_{q\geqslant 0}(\|v_{q+1}-v_{q}\|_{L^{r}_{t}L^{p}}+\|v_{q+1}-v_{q}\|_{C_{t}L^{s}})\leqslant\sum_{q\geqslant 0}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}}\leqslant\epsilon_{0}.

We finish the proof of the first statement.

For the second statement,
by (
4.5
), (
4.9
) and interpolation, we have
|v|\in L_{t}^{d_{0}(1+\epsilon)}L^{d_{0}(1+\epsilon)}
for some
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small. Since
\rho^{i}\in L_{t}^{d_{0}^{\prime}}L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}
, we deduce that
|v|^{1+\epsilon}\rho^{i}\in L_{t}^{1}L^{1}
.
Moreover, since

t\mapsto\rho^{i}(t)

is continuous on
[0,1]
, we may apply the superposition principle to the linear diffusion operator

\displaystyle L_{\rho^{i}}:=\sigma\sigma^{T}(\rho^{i}):\nabla^{2}+(v+V*\rho^{i})\cdot\nabla,

where the density
\rho^{i}
is regarded as fixed.

More precisely, let
C([0,1];\mathbb{T}^{d})
denote the space of continuous paths, equipped with its Borel
\sigma
-algebra and the natural filtration generated by the canonical process

\Pi_{t}
,
t\in[0,1],

defined by

\Pi_{t}(\omega):=\omega(t),\ \omega\in C([0,1];\mathbb{T}^{d}).

By the superposition principle
[
Tre14
, Section 7.2]
, there exists a probability measure
\mathbf{Q}^{i}
on
C([0,1];\mathbb{T}^{d})
which is a martingale solution associated with
L_{\rho^{i}}
in the sense that, for every smooth function
f
on
\mathbb{T}^{d}
, the process

\displaystyle f(\Pi_{t})-f(\Pi_{0})-\int_{0}^{t}L_{\rho^{i}}f(\Pi_{s})\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s

is a
\mathbf{Q}^{i}
-martingale. Moreover,

{\mathord{{\rm d}}}\mathbf{Q}^{i}\circ\Pi_{t}^{-1}=\rho^{i}(t)\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x,\ t\in[0,1].

Since
\{\rho^{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
is a family of distinct solutions to (
1.3
), it follows that
\{\mathbf{Q}^{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
is a family of distinct martingale solutions.

5.
Proof of Proposition
4.2

In this section, we extend the convex integration scheme to a system of infinitely many equations in order to prove Proposition
4.2
.
At step
q+1
, we focus on the first
N_{q+1}
Fokker–Planck equations in (
1.3
), and construct new perturbations to eliminate the corresponding stress terms.
At the same time, the remaining equations with indices larger than
N_{q+1}
are left unchanged and shown to remain sufficiently small so as to satisfy the required estimates at level
q+1
.

5.1.
Mollification

For a sufficiently small parameter
\alpha\in(0,1)
to be chosen later, we define

l:=\lambda_{q+1}^{-7\alpha/4}\lambda_{q}^{-3d/2-6}.

Then

(5.1)

\displaystyle l^{-1}\leqslant\lambda_{q+1}^{2\alpha},\ \ l\lambda_{q}^{3d+12}\leqslant\lambda_{q+1}^{-3\alpha/2},\ \ \lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha/2}\ll\delta_{q+2},

provided that

(5.2)

\displaystyle\alpha b>6d+24,\ \ \alpha>4\beta b.

To avoid the loss of derivatives, we first mollify the first
N_{q+1}
equations.
Let

\phi_{l}:=\frac{1}{l^{d}}\phi\bigl(\tfrac{\cdot}{l}\bigr)

be a family of standard radial mollifiers on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and let

\varphi_{l}:=\frac{1}{l}\varphi\bigl(\tfrac{\cdot}{l}\bigr)

be a family of standard mollifiers supported in
(0,1)
.
We define

\displaystyle v_{l}=(v_{q}*_{x}\phi_{l})*_{t}\varphi_{l},\ \ \rho_{l}^{i}=(\rho_{q}^{i}*_{x}\phi_{l})*_{t}\varphi_{l},\ \ M_{l}^{i}=(M_{q}^{i}*_{x}\phi_{l})*_{t}\varphi_{l}.

For the mollification around
t=0
, since
\rho_{q}^{i}
and
M_{q}
are constants around
t=0
, we can directly extend these definitions to
t\leqslant 0
by their values at
t=0
. For
v_{q}
, we also extend the definition to
t\leqslant 0
by their values at
t=0
.
Since
\rho_{q}^{i}
and
M_{q}^{i}
are constant near
t=0
, this causes no problem.
Moreover, we know that
\rho_{l}^{i}=1
and
M_{l}^{i}=0
on
[0,T_{q+1}]
.

By straightforward calculations we obtain

(5.3)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho_{l}^{i}-\mathord{{\rm div}}\mathord{{\rm div}}(A(\rho_{l}^{i})\rho_{l}^{i})+\mathord{{\rm div}}(v_{l}\rho_{l}^{i})+\mathord{{\rm div}}((V*\rho_{l}^{i})\rho_{l}^{i})

\displaystyle=-\mathord{{\rm div}}(M^{i}_{l}+M^{i}_{com}),\ \ \mathord{{\rm div}}v_{l}=0,

where

\displaystyle M_{com}^{i}

\displaystyle:=-v_{l}\rho^{i}_{l}+(v_{q}\rho^{i}_{q})*_{x}\phi_{l}*_{t}\varphi_{l}+\mathord{{\rm div}}(A(\rho_{l}^{i})\rho_{l}^{i})-(\mathord{{\rm div}}(A(\rho_{q}^{i})\rho_{q}^{i}))*_{x}\phi_{l}*_{t}\varphi_{l}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ -(V*\rho_{l}^{i})\rho_{l}^{i}+((V*\rho_{q}^{i})\rho_{q}^{i})*_{x}\phi_{l}*_{t}\varphi_{l}.

Moreover, we know that
M_{com}^{i}=0
on
[0,T_{q+1}]
.

Finally, by the standard mollification estimates, the space-time Sobolev embedding

W^{d+\frac{4}{3},1}\hookrightarrow L^{\infty},

and (
4.6
), we obtain for every
N\geqslant 0
,

(5.4)

\displaystyle\|M_{l}^{i}\|_{C_{t,x}^{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-d-\frac{4}{3}-N}\|M_{q}^{i}\|_{L_{t}^{1}L^{1}}\lesssim C_{0}2^{-i}l^{-d-\frac{4}{3}-N}.

5.2.
Construction of
v_{q+1}

As discussed above, we apply the convex integration scheme only to the first
N_{q+1}
equations.
To construct the perturbations for
v_{q}
and
\rho_{q}^{i}
,
1\leqslant i\leqslant N_{q+1}
, we employ the
L^{d_{0}}
-based building blocks introduced in Appendix
A
, for some exponent
d_{0}>1
to be specified later.
We also recall the parameters

\lambda,\ r_{\perp},\ r_{\parallel},\ \eta,\ \mu,\ \sigma

introduced in Appendix
A
. Their precise choice will be specified subsequently.

Let

\chi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(-\frac{3}{4},\frac{3}{4})

be a nonnegative function such that

\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\chi(t-n)=1

for every
t\in\mathbb{R}
.
Let

\tilde{\chi}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(-\frac{4}{5},\frac{4}{5})

be a nonnegative function satisfying

\tilde{\chi}=1

on

[-\frac{3}{4},\frac{3}{4}]

and

\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\tilde{\chi}(t-n)\leqslant 2.

For
1\leqslant i\leqslant N_{q+1}
, we fix the parameters

\zeta^{i}:=20\cdot 2^{i}\delta_{q+2}^{-1},

and consider the two disjoint sets

\Lambda^{1},\Lambda^{2}

defined in Appendix
A
.
We abuse the notation

\Lambda^{i}=\Lambda^{1}

for odd
i
, and

\Lambda^{i}=\Lambda^{2}

for even
i
.

We take

K=N_{q+1}

in the construction of Appendix
A
, so that

(5.5)

\displaystyle\eta N_{q+1}\ll 1.

We then introduce a family of pairwise disjoint functions

g_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}

and

g_{(\xi,i,d_{0}^{\prime})}

for

\xi\in\Lambda

and

1\leqslant i\leqslant N_{q+1}.

With these preparations, we define the rescaled building blocks by

\displaystyle W_{(\xi,n,i)}(x,t)

\displaystyle:=W_{(\xi,d_{0})}\Bigl(x,\Bigl(\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Bigr)^{1/d_{0}}H_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}(t)\Bigr),

\displaystyle\Theta_{(\xi,n,i)}(x,t)

\displaystyle:=\Theta_{(\xi,d_{0}^{\prime})}\Bigl(x,\Bigl(\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Bigr)^{1/d_{0}}H_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}(t)\Bigr).

Similarly, we define

V_{(\xi,n,i)},\Phi_{(\xi,n,i)},

and all the other quantities appearing in Appendix
A
.
By (
A.2
) and (
A.8
), we have

(5.6)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Theta_{(\xi,n,i)}+\Bigl(\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Bigr)^{1/d_{0}}g_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}\mathord{{\rm div}}\bigl(W_{(\xi,n,i)}\Theta_{(\xi,n,i)}\bigr)=0.

We next define the perturbations for the drift term.
For
1\leqslant i\leqslant N_{q+1}
, let

\displaystyle w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}:=\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\tilde{\chi}(\zeta^{i}|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\Bigl(\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Bigr)^{1/d_{0}}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}W_{(\xi,n,i)}g_{(\xi,i,d_{0})},

and

\displaystyle w_{q+1}^{(c,i)}:=\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\Bigl(

\displaystyle-\tilde{\chi}(\zeta^{i}|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\Bigl(\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Bigr)^{1/d_{0}}\frac{1}{(n_{*}\lambda_{q+1})^{2}}\nabla\Phi_{(\xi,n,i)}\,\xi\cdot\nabla\psi_{(\xi,n,i)}

\displaystyle\quad+\nabla\bigl(\tilde{\chi}(\zeta^{i}|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\bigr)\Bigl(\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Bigr)^{1/d_{0}}:V_{(\xi,n,i)}\Bigr)g_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}.

By (
A.3
), we obtain

(5.7)

\displaystyle w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}+w_{q+1}^{(c,i)}=\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\mathord{{\rm div}}\Bigl(\tilde{\chi}(\zeta^{i}|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\Bigl(\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Bigr)^{1/d_{0}}V_{(\xi,n,i)}\Bigr)g_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}.

Since
V_{(\xi,n,i)}
is skew-symmetric, it follows that

\mathord{{\rm div}}\bigl(w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}+w_{q+1}^{(c,i)}\bigr)=0.

Finally, we define the total perturbation and the new velocity field by

\displaystyle w_{q+1}:=\sum_{i=1}^{N_{q+1}}\bigl(w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}+w_{q+1}^{(c,i)}\bigr),\qquad v_{q+1}:=v_{l}+w_{q+1}.

Then
v_{q+1}
is mean-zero and divergence-free.
Moreover, since
M_{l}^{i}(t)=0
on
[0,T_{q+1}],
the perturbation
w_{q+1}
vanishes on
[0,T_{q+1}]
.

5.3.
Construction of
\rho^{i}_{q+1}

We next define the perturbations for the density functions.
For
1\leqslant i\leqslant N_{q+1}
, we set

\displaystyle\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}:=

\displaystyle\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\chi(\zeta^{i}|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\Bigl(\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Bigr)^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\Gamma_{\xi}\Bigl(\frac{M_{l}^{i}}{|M_{l}^{i}|}\Bigr)\Theta_{(\xi,n,i)}g_{(\xi,i,d_{0}^{\prime})},

\displaystyle\theta_{q+1}^{(c,i)}:=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x,

\displaystyle\theta_{q+1}^{(o,i)}:=

\displaystyle-\sigma^{-1}\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}h_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}\mathord{{\rm div}}\Bigl(\chi(\zeta^{i}|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Gamma_{\xi}\Bigl(\frac{M_{l}^{i}}{|M_{l}^{i}|}\Bigr)\xi\Bigr).

By an argument analogous to that in
[
LRZ25
, (5.5)]
, we obtain

\displaystyle w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}=

\displaystyle\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\chi(\zeta^{i}|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Gamma_{\xi}\Bigl(\frac{M_{l}^{i}}{|M_{l}^{i}|}\Bigr)\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}\bigl(W_{(\xi,n,i)}\Theta_{(\xi,n,i)}\bigr)g_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}g_{(\xi,i,d_{0}^{\prime})}

\displaystyle+\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\chi(\zeta^{i}|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Gamma_{\xi}\Bigl(\frac{M_{l}^{i}}{|M_{l}^{i}|}\Bigr)\xi\bigl(g_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}g_{(\xi,i,d_{0}^{\prime})}-1\bigr)

\displaystyle+\chi(\zeta^{i}|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\frac{M_{l}^{i}}{|M_{l}^{i}|}.

Combining this identity with (
5.6
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}+\mathord{{\rm div}}\bigl(w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}-M_{l}^{i}\bigr)+\partial_{t}\theta_{q+1}^{(o,i)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\partial_{t}\Bigl[\chi(\zeta^{i}|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\Bigl(\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Bigr)^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\Gamma_{\xi}\Bigl(\frac{M_{l}^{i}}{|M_{l}^{i}|}\Bigr)g_{(\xi,i,d_{0}^{\prime})}\Bigr]\Theta_{(\xi,n,i)}

\displaystyle+\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\nabla\Bigl[\chi(\zeta^{i}|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Gamma_{\xi}\Bigl(\frac{M_{l}^{i}}{|M_{l}^{i}|}\Bigr)\Bigr]g_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}g_{(\xi,i,d_{0}^{\prime})}\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}\bigl(W_{(\xi,n,i)}\Theta_{(\xi,n,i)}\bigr)

\displaystyle+\mathord{{\rm div}}\Bigl(\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\chi(\zeta^{i}|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\frac{M_{l}^{i}}{|M_{l}^{i}|}-M_{l}^{i}\Bigr)

(5.8)

\displaystyle-\sigma^{-1}\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}h_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}\partial_{t}\mathord{{\rm div}}\Bigl(\chi(\zeta^{i}|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Gamma_{\xi}\Bigl(\frac{M_{l}^{i}}{|M_{l}^{i}|}\Bigr)\xi\Bigr).

We now define, for every
1\leqslant i\leqslant N_{q+1}
,

\displaystyle\theta_{q+1}^{i}:=\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}+\theta_{q+1}^{(c,i)}+\theta_{q+1}^{(o,i)},\qquad\rho_{q+1}^{i}:=\rho_{l}^{i}+\theta_{q+1}^{i}.

For
i>N_{q+1}
, we simply set

\rho_{q+1}^{i}:=\rho_{q}^{i}.

By construction,

\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\rho_{q+1}^{i}\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x=1

for every
i\in\mathbb{N}
.
Since

M_{l}^{i}(t)=0

on

[0,T_{q+1}],

the perturbation
\theta_{q+1}^{i}(t)
vanishes there for all

1\leqslant i\leqslant N_{q+1}.

Hence

\rho_{q+1}^{i}(t)=1

on

[0,T_{q+1}]

for all

1\leqslant i\leqslant N_{q+1}.

For

i>N_{q+1},

we already have

\rho_{q+1}^{i}(t)=\rho_{q}^{i}(t)=1

on

[0,T_{q+1}].

Moreover, since the functions

g_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}

have disjoint supports for distinct
i
, and since

\theta_{q+1}^{(c,i)}

depends only on
t
, we obtain for

1\leqslant i\leqslant N_{q+1}

that

(5.9)

\displaystyle\mathord{{\rm div}}\bigl(w_{q+1}\theta_{q+1}^{i}\bigr)=\mathord{{\rm div}}\Bigl((w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}+w_{q+1}^{(c,i)})\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}+w_{q+1}\theta_{q+1}^{(o,i)}\Bigr).

5.4.
Construction of the stress terms
M^{i}_{q+1}

5.4.1.
The case
1\leqslant i\leqslant N_{q+1}

From (
5.9
), and the definition of the perturbations
we obtain

\displaystyle-\mathord{{\rm div}}M^{i}_{q+1}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}\theta_{q+1}^{i}+\mathord{{\rm div}}(w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}-M^{i}_{l})(:=\mathord{{\rm div}}M^{i}_{osc})

\displaystyle\quad-\mathord{{\rm div}}[\mathord{{\rm div}}(A(\rho^{i}_{q+1})\rho^{i}_{q+1}-A(\rho^{i}_{l})\rho^{i}_{l})-(V*\rho_{q+1}^{i})\rho_{q+1}^{i}+(V*\rho_{l}^{i})\rho_{l}^{i}](:=\mathord{{\rm div}}M^{i}_{nonlin})

\displaystyle\quad+\mathord{{\rm div}}(v_{l}\theta^{i}_{q+1}+w_{q+1}(\rho^{i}_{l}+\theta_{q+1}^{(o,i)})+w_{q+1}^{(c,i)}\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)})(:=\mathord{{\rm div}}M^{i}_{lin})

\displaystyle\quad-\mathord{{\rm div}}M_{com}^{i},

where we define the nonlinear error and the linear error respectively, by

\displaystyle M^{i}_{nonlin}:

\displaystyle=-\mathord{{\rm div}}(A(\rho^{i}_{q+1})\rho^{i}_{q+1}-A(\rho^{i}_{l})\rho^{i}_{l})-(V*\rho_{q+1}^{i})\rho_{q+1}^{i}+(V*\rho_{l}^{i})\rho_{l}^{i},

\displaystyle M^{i}_{lin}:

\displaystyle=v_{l}\theta^{i}_{q+1}+w_{q+1}(\rho^{i}_{l}+\theta_{q+1}^{(o,i)})+w_{q+1}^{(c,i)}\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}.

To define the oscillation error, using the inverse divergence operators
\mathcal{R}_{1},\mathcal{B}_{1}
introduced in Section
3.1
and (
5.8
), we define
M^{i}_{osc}:=M^{i}_{osc,t}+M^{i}_{osc,x}+M^{i}_{osc,c}+M^{i}_{osc,o}
as

\displaystyle M^{i}_{osc,t}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\mathcal{R}_{1}{\Big(}\partial_{t}[\chi(\zeta^{i}|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}g_{(\xi,i,d_{0}^{\prime})}]\Theta_{(\xi,n,i)}{\Big)},

\displaystyle M^{i}_{osc,x}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\mathcal{B}_{1}{\Big(}\nabla[\chi(\zeta^{i}|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}],\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}(W_{(\xi,n,i)}\Theta_{(\xi,n,i)}){\Big)}g_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}g_{(\xi,i,d_{0}^{\prime})},

\displaystyle M^{i}_{osc,c}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\chi(\zeta^{i}|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}-M^{i}_{l},

\displaystyle M^{i}_{osc,o}

\displaystyle:=-\sigma^{-1}\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}h_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}\partial_{t}{\Big(}\chi(\zeta|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M_{l}^{i}}{|M_{l}^{i}|}{\Big)}\xi{\Big)}.

Then we define

-{M}^{i}_{q+1}:=M^{i}_{osc}+M^{i}_{nonlin}+M^{i}_{lin}-M_{com}^{i}.

Since
M^{i}_{l}(t)=w_{q+1}(t)=\theta^{i}_{q+1}(t)=0
on
[0,T_{q+1}]
, we have
M_{q+1}^{i}(t)=0
on
[0,T_{q+1}]
, which implies (
4.8
) for
M_{q+1}^{i},1\leqslant i\leqslant N_{q+1}
.

5.4.2.
The case
i>N_{q+1}

In this case, since

\mathord{{\rm div}}(v_{q+1}-v_{q})=0

and

\rho_{q+1}^{i}=\rho_{q}^{i}=\rho_{0}^{i},

we define

\displaystyle M_{q+1}^{i}:=M_{q}^{i}-(v_{q+1}-v_{q})(\rho_{0}^{i}-1)=M_{0}^{i}-(v_{q+1}-v_{0})(\rho_{0}^{i}-1).

It is straightforward to verify that

(v_{q+1},\rho_{q+1}^{i},M_{q+1}^{i})_{i>N_{q+1}}

satisfies (
4.2
) at level
q+1
.

Moreover, since

\rho_{0}^{i}(t)=1

and

M_{0}^{i}(t)=0

on

[0,T_{0}],

it follows that

M_{q+1}^{i}(t)=0

on

[0,T_{q+1}],

which proves (
4.8
) for

M_{q+1}^{i},\ i>N_{q+1}.

5.5.
Proof of Proposition
4.2

In this section, we verify that the perturbations and the new stress terms constructed above satisfy the properties stated in Proposition
4.2
.
Most of the estimates are analogous to the basic convex integration estimates, so we mainly focus on the new features arising from the choice of
N_{q+1}
and on the estimates for
M_{q+1}^{i}
when
i>N_{q+1}
.

5.5.1.
Choice of parameters

Regarding the parameters of the building blocks, we define

\displaystyle\lambda=\lambda_{q+1},\ r_{\perp}=\lambda_{q+1}^{-1+\frac{1}{N}},\ r_{\parallel}=\lambda_{q+1}^{-1+\frac{2}{N}},\ \eta=\lambda_{q+1}^{-1},\ \mu=r_{\perp}^{-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}}r_{\parallel}^{-\frac{1}{d_{0}}},\ \sigma=\lambda_{q+1}^{\frac{1}{2N}},

where
N>4d
is a sufficiently large integer satisfying
\frac{d}{p}+\frac{1}{r}>1+\frac{4d}{N},\frac{d}{s}>1+\frac{4d}{N}
. We further define
d_{0}:=d+1-\frac{4d}{N}\in(d,d+1),d_{0}^{\prime}:=\frac{d_{0}}{d_{0}-1}\in(1,2)
. It is straightforward to verify (
5.5
).
With these choices, we have

(5.10)

\displaystyle r_{\perp}^{d-1-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}r_{\parallel}^{1-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\eta^{-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}},\ r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{p}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}}r_{\parallel}^{\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{d_{0}}}\eta^{\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{d_{0}}},\ r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{s}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}}r_{\parallel}^{\frac{1}{s}-\frac{1}{d_{0}}}\eta^{-\frac{1}{d_{0}}}\leqslant\lambda^{-\frac{1}{N}}.

In fact, by a direct calculation, it holds that

\displaystyle\lambda r_{\perp}^{d-1-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}r_{\parallel}^{1-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\eta^{1-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=r_{\perp}^{d-1-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}r_{\parallel}^{1-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\eta^{-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\leqslant r_{\parallel}^{\frac{d}{d_{0}}}\eta^{\frac{1}{d_{0}}-1}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{\frac{2d}{Nd_{0}}+\frac{d_{0}-d-1}{d_{0}}}=\lambda^{-\frac{2d}{Nd_{0}}}\leqslant\lambda^{-\frac{1}{N}},

\displaystyle r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{p}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}}r_{\parallel}^{\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{d_{0}}}\eta^{\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{d_{0}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\lambda^{(\frac{d+1}{N}-d)(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{d_{0}})-\frac{1}{r}+\frac{1}{d_{0}}}\leqslant\lambda^{-\frac{d}{p}-\frac{1}{r}+\frac{d+1}{d_{0}}+\frac{d+1}{N}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\lambda^{-1-\frac{4d}{N}+1+\frac{2d}{N}+\frac{d+1}{N}}\leqslant\lambda^{-\frac{1}{N}},

\displaystyle r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{s}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}}r_{\parallel}^{\frac{1}{s}-\frac{1}{d_{0}}}\eta^{-\frac{1}{d_{0}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\lambda^{(\frac{d+1}{N}-d)(\frac{1}{s}-\frac{1}{d_{0}})+\frac{1}{d_{0}}}\leqslant\lambda^{-\frac{d}{s}+\frac{d+1}{d_{0}}+\frac{d+1}{N}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\lambda^{-1-\frac{4d}{N}+1+\frac{2d}{N}+\frac{d+1}{N}}\leqslant\lambda^{-\frac{1}{N}}.

In the sequel, we shall also require (
5.2
) together with

(5.11)

\displaystyle\lambda_{q}^{d+4}\leqslant\lambda_{q+1}^{\alpha},\ \ (12d+44)\alpha<\frac{1}{2N}.

These conditions can be achieved as follows:
first choose
\alpha>0
sufficiently small such that

(12d+44)\alpha<\frac{1}{2N},

then choose

b\in 2N\mathbb{N}

sufficiently large so that

b>\frac{6d+24}{\alpha},

and finally choose
\beta>0
sufficiently small such that

\alpha>4\beta b.

At the end, we choose
a
sufficiently large in order to absorb all implicit and universal constants arising in the subsequent estimates, and to guarantee the validity of (
4.1
).

Finally, we record the following estimate for the amplitude functions.

Lemma 5.1
.

(
[
LRZ25
, Proposition 5.2]
)
.
For
M,N\in\mathbb{N}_{0},1\leqslant i\leqslant N_{q+1}
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{n\geqslant 3}{\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}{\Big)}^{M}{\Big(}\|\chi(\zeta^{i}|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\|_{C_{t,x}^{N}}+\|\tilde{\chi}(\zeta^{i}|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\|_{C_{t,x}^{N}}{\Big)}

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-(d+4)N-(d+2)(M+1)},

\displaystyle\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}{\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}{\Big)}^{M}\left\|\chi(\zeta^{i}|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}\right\|_{C_{t,x}^{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-(2d+8)N-(d+2)(M+1)}.

5.5.2.
Proof of (
4.9
) for
v_{q+1}-v_{q}
.

From this section onward, we establish the desired estimates for the perturbation
w_{q+1}
.

We first estimate the principal perturbations
w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}
for
1\leqslant i\leqslant N_{q+1}
in the
L_{t}^{d_{0}}L^{d_{0}}
-norm. By Cauchy’s inequality and the fact that
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\tilde{\chi}(t-n)\leqslant 2
we have

\displaystyle|w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}|^{d_{0}}

\displaystyle\leqslant{\Big(}\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\tilde{\chi}(\zeta^{i}|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big)}^{d_{0}-1}\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\tilde{\chi}(\zeta^{i}|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Bigg|\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}W_{(\xi,n,i)}g_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}\bigg|^{d_{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\tilde{\chi}(\zeta^{i}|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\left|W_{(\xi,n,i)}g_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}\right|^{d_{0}}.

By applying the generalized Hölder inequality of
Theorem
3.3
in spatial direction, together with the estimates for the building blocks in (
A.6
) and Lemma
5.1
we deduce

\displaystyle\|w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}(t)\|^{d_{0}}_{L^{d_{0}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\left\|\tilde{\chi}(\zeta^{i}|M^{i}_{l}(t)|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\right\|_{L^{1}}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\|W_{(\xi,n,i)}\|_{C_{t}L^{d_{0}}}^{d_{0}}g_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}^{d_{0}}(t)

\displaystyle\quad+(r_{\perp}\lambda_{q+1})^{-1}\left\|\tilde{\chi}(\zeta^{i}|M^{i}_{l}(t)|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\right\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\|W_{(\xi,n,i)}\|_{C_{t}L^{d_{0}}}^{d_{0}}g_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}^{d_{0}}(t)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\left\|\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\tilde{\chi}(\zeta^{i}|M^{i}_{l}(t)|-n)(M^{i}_{l}(t)+|\zeta^{i}|^{-1})\right\|_{L^{1}}+l^{-3d-8}\lambda_{q+1}^{-\frac{1}{N}}\right)\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda}g_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}^{d_{0}}(t)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|M^{i}_{l}(t)\|_{L^{1}}+2^{-i}\delta_{q+1})\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda}g_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}^{d_{0}}(t),

where we used the fact that
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\tilde{\chi}(t-n)\leqslant 2
, and used conditions on the parameters to have
(6d+17)\alpha-\frac{1}{N}<-\alpha<-2\beta,2^{i}\leqslant 2^{N_{q+1}}\leqslant\lambda_{q+1}^{\alpha}
. Here we recall the notation
\Lambda=\Lambda^{1}\cup\Lambda^{2}
.
Then we apply the generalized Hölder inequality of Theorem
3.3
in time direction, the bounds (
A.9
) and (
5.4
) to deduce for some
{C}_{v}\geqslant 1

\displaystyle\|w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|_{L^{d_{0}}_{t}L^{d_{0}}}^{d_{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|M^{i}_{l}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{1}}+2^{-i}\delta_{q+1}+\sigma^{-1}\|M^{i}_{l}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}})\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda}\left\|g_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}\right\|^{d_{0}}_{L_{t}^{d_{0}}}

(5.12)

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}2^{-i}(\delta_{q+1}+\lambda_{q+1}^{(2d+6)\alpha-\frac{1}{2N}})\leqslant(\frac{1}{4}C_{v}(2^{\frac{1}{d+1}}-1))^{d_{0}}C_{0}2^{-i}\delta_{q+1},

where we used conditions on the parameters to have
(2d+6)\alpha-\frac{1}{2N}<-\alpha<-2\beta
.

For the general
L^{u}_{t}L^{m}
-norm with
u,m\in[1,\infty]
, by the estimates for the building blocks in (
A.4
)-(
A.6
), (
A.9
)
and the estimate for the amplitude function in Lemma
5.1
we obtain

\displaystyle\|w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|_{L^{u}_{t}L^{m}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\left\|\tilde{\chi}(\zeta^{i}|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}}\right\|_{C_{t,x}^{0}}\|W_{(\xi,n,i)}\|_{C_{t}L^{m}}\|g_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}\|_{L_{t}^{u}}

(5.13)

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-2d-4}r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{m}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}}r_{\parallel}^{\frac{1}{m}-\frac{1}{d_{0}}}\eta^{\frac{1}{u}-\frac{1}{d_{0}}},

\displaystyle\|w_{q+1}^{(c,i)}\|_{L^{u}_{t}L^{m}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\left\|\tilde{\chi}(\zeta^{i}|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}}\right\|_{C^{1}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times{\Big(}\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}^{2}}\|\nabla\Phi_{(\xi,n,i)}\xi\cdot\nabla\psi_{(\xi,n,i)}\|_{L^{m}}+\|V_{(\xi,n,i)}\|_{L^{m}}{\Big)}\|g_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}\|_{L_{t}^{u}}

(5.14)

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-3d-8}r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{m}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}}r_{\parallel}^{\frac{1}{m}-\frac{1}{d_{0}}}\frac{r_{\perp}}{r_{\parallel}}\eta^{\frac{1}{u}-\frac{1}{d_{0}}}.

With these estimates, combining with the choice of parameters in (
5.1
) and the bound (
5.12
) we obtain

\displaystyle\|w_{q+1}\|_{L^{d_{0}}_{t}L^{d_{0}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{i=1}^{N_{q+1}}\frac{C_{v}}{4}2^{-i/d_{0}}C_{0}^{1/d_{0}}(2^{\frac{1}{d+1}}-1)\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}}+N_{q+1}Cl^{-3d-8}\frac{r_{\perp}}{r_{\parallel}}

(5.15)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C_{v}}{4}C_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}}+C\lambda_{q+1}^{(6d+17)\alpha-\frac{1}{N}}\leqslant\frac{C_{v}}{2}C_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}},

where we used conditions on the parameters to have

{(6d+18)\alpha<\frac{1}{N}},N_{q+1}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{\alpha}
and chose
a
large enough to absorb the universal constant.
The above inequality yields that (
4.9
) holds for
v_{q+1}-v_{q}
and then (
4.4
) holds for
v_{q+1}
:

\displaystyle\|v_{q+1}-v_{q}\|_{L^{d_{0}}_{t}L^{d_{0}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|w_{q+1}\|_{L^{d_{0}}_{t}L^{d_{0}}}+\|v_{l}-v_{q}\|_{L^{d_{0}}_{t}L^{d_{0}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{2}C_{v}C_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}}+lC_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\lambda_{q}^{d+4}\leqslant C_{v}C_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}}.

5.5.3.
Proof of (
4.10
)

Combining the bounds (
5.13
) and (
5.14
) above we obtain

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle v_{q+1}-v_{q}\|_{L^{r}_{t}L^{p}}+\|v_{q+1}-v_{q}\|_{C_{t}L^{s}}\lesssim\|w_{q+1}\|_{C_{t}L^{s}}+\|w_{q+1}\|_{L^{r}_{t}L^{p}}+l\|v_{q}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{q+1}l^{-3d-8}(r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{s}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}}r_{\parallel}^{\frac{1}{s}-\frac{1}{d_{0}}}\eta^{-\frac{1}{d_{0}}}+r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{p}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}}r_{\parallel}^{\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{d_{0}}}\eta^{\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{d_{0}}})+l\lambda_{q}^{d+4}

(5.16)

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{(6d+17)\alpha-\frac{1}{N}}+\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}\leqslant\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}}.

Here we used (
5.1
), (
5.10
) and conditions on the parameters to have

{(6d+18)\alpha<\frac{1}{N}},N_{q+1}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{\alpha}
. Then we chose
a
large enough to absorb the universal constant.

5.5.4.
Proof of (
4.5
) for
v_{q+1}
.

By the estimates for the building blocks in (
A.6
), (
A.9
), (
5.7
) and the estimates for the amplitude functions in Lemma
5.1
we have for
d_{0}\geqslant 2

\displaystyle\|w_{q+1}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{N_{q+1}}\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\left\|\tilde{\chi}(\zeta^{i}|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}}\right\|_{C_{t,x}^{2}}\|\nabla V_{(\xi,n,i)}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\|g_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}\|_{C_{t}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{q+1}l^{-4d-12}\lambda_{q+1}\mu r_{\parallel}^{-\frac{1}{d_{0}}}r_{\perp}^{-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}}\sigma\eta^{-1-\frac{2}{d_{0}}}\lesssim N_{q+1}\lambda_{q+1}^{(8d+24)\alpha+d+\frac{7}{2}}.

Thus, by
N_{q+1}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{\alpha},(8d+25)\alpha<\frac{1}{2}
we obtain the following

\displaystyle\|v_{q+1}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\leqslant\|v_{l}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}+\|w_{q+1}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\leqslant C_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\lambda_{q}^{d+4}+\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{q+1}^{d+4}\leqslant C_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\lambda_{q+1}^{d+4},

where we chose
a
large enough to absorb the universal constant.

5.5.5.
Proof of (
4.9
) for
\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho_{q}^{i}

Similarly as before, we first estimate the principal perturbations
\theta^{(p,i)}_{q+1}
in
L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}_{t}L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}
. By Hölder inequality and the fact that
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}{\chi}(t-n)=1
, and
\Gamma_{\xi}
are uniformly bounded we have

\displaystyle|\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}|^{d_{0}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\Big(}\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\chi(\zeta^{i}|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big)}^{d_{0}^{\prime}-1}\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\chi(\zeta^{i}|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\left|\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}\Theta_{(\xi,n,i)}g_{(\xi,i,d_{0}^{\prime})}\right|^{d_{0}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\chi(\zeta^{i}|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\left|\Theta_{(\xi,n,i)}g_{(\xi,i,d_{0}^{\prime})}\right|^{d_{0}^{\prime}}.

By the same argument as in (
5.12
), we have

\displaystyle\|\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|_{L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}_{t}L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}}^{d_{0}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\Big(}\|M^{i}_{l}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{1}}+2^{-i}\delta_{q+1}+\sigma^{-1}\|M^{i}_{l}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}{\Big)}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda}\left\|g_{(\xi,i,d_{0}^{\prime})}\right\|^{d_{0}^{\prime}}_{L_{t}^{d_{0}^{\prime}}}

(5.17)

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-i}C_{0}(\delta_{q+1}+\lambda_{q+1}^{(2d+6)\alpha-\frac{1}{2N}})\leqslant 2^{-i}(\frac{1}{2}C_{v})^{d_{0}^{\prime}}C_{0}\delta_{q+1},

where we used conditions on the parameters to have
(2d+6)\alpha-\frac{1}{2N}<-\alpha<-2\beta
.

For general
L_{t}^{u}L^{m}
-norm with
u,m\in[1,\infty]
, by the estimates for the building blocks in (
A.7
), (
A.9
) and Lemma
5.1
we obtain

\displaystyle\|\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|_{L^{u}_{t}L^{m}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\left\|\chi(\zeta^{i}|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}\right\|_{C_{t,x}^{0}}\|\Theta_{(\xi,n,i)}\|_{C_{t}L^{m}}\|g_{(\xi,i,d_{0}^{\prime})}\|_{L_{t}^{u}}

(5.18)

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-2d-4}r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{m}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}r_{\parallel}^{\frac{1}{m}-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\eta^{\frac{1}{u}-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}.

Moreover, by the bounds (
A.9
) and (
5.10
) we have for some
\epsilon>0
small enough

(5.19)

\displaystyle\|\theta_{q+1}^{(c,i)}\|_{C_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|_{C_{t}L^{1+\epsilon}}\lesssim l^{-2d-4}r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{1+\epsilon}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}r_{\parallel}^{\frac{1}{1+\epsilon}-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\eta^{-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{(4d+8)\alpha-\frac{1}{N}+d\epsilon}\lesssim 4^{-i}\lambda_{q+1}^{-2\alpha},

(5.20)

\displaystyle\|\theta_{q+1}^{(o,i)}\|_{C_{t}C^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma^{-1}l^{-6d-20}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{(12d+40)\alpha-\frac{1}{2N}}\lesssim 4^{-i}\lambda_{q+1}^{-2\alpha},

where we used conditions on the parameters to have
(12d+44)\alpha<\frac{1}{2N},2^{i}\leqslant 2^{N_{q+1}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{\alpha}
and chose
a
large enough to absorb the universal constant. We also chose
\epsilon>0
small such that
d\epsilon<\alpha
.
Then, combining the above estimates together, we obtain by (
4.5
)

\displaystyle\|\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}_{t}L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\theta^{i}_{q+1}\|_{L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}_{t}L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}}+l\|\rho_{q}^{i}-1\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{2}C_{v}C_{0}^{1/{d_{0}^{\prime}}}2^{-i/d_{0}^{\prime}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/{d_{0}^{\prime}}}+4^{-i}\lambda_{q+1}^{-2\alpha}+l2^{-i}C_{0}^{1/{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\lambda_{q}^{d+4}\leqslant C_{v}C_{0}^{1/{d_{0}^{\prime}}}2^{-i/d_{0}^{\prime}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/{d_{0}^{\prime}}},

which implies (
4.9
) for
\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho_{q}^{i},1\leqslant i\leqslant N_{q+1}
. Here we chose
a
large enough to absorb the universal constant. For
i>N_{q+1}
, we have

\rho_{q+1}^{i}-\rho_{q}^{i}=0.

Hence, the second estimate in (
4.9
) also holds for
i>N_{q+1}
.

5.5.6.
Proof of (
4.11
)

We first estimate
\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}
in
W^{1,1+\epsilon}
-norm for some
\epsilon>0
small enough. By the bounds for the building blocks in (
A.7
), (
A.9
) and the bounds for the amplitude functions in Lemma

we have

\displaystyle\|\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|_{L^{1}_{t}W^{1,1+\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\left\|\chi(\zeta^{i}|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}\right\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\|\Theta_{(\xi,n,i)}\|_{C_{t}W^{1,1+\epsilon}}\|g_{(\xi,i,d_{0}^{\prime})}\|_{L_{t}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-4d-12}\lambda_{q+1}r_{\parallel}^{\frac{1}{1+\epsilon}-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{1+\epsilon}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\eta^{1-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{(8d+24)\alpha-\frac{1}{N}+d\epsilon}\lesssim 2^{-i}\lambda_{q+1}^{-2\alpha},

where we used the choice of parameters in (
5.10
) and chose
\epsilon>0
small enough such that
d\epsilon<\alpha
. We also used conditions on the parameters to have
(8d+28)\alpha<\frac{1}{N},2^{i}\leqslant 2^{N_{q+1}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{\alpha}
.

Similarly, by the fact that
\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}
is non-negative, (
4.5
) and the choice of parameters in (
5.1
), (
5.10
) we have

\displaystyle\|\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{C_{t}L^{1+\epsilon}}

\displaystyle+\|\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{L_{t}^{1}W^{1,1+\epsilon}}\leqslant\|\theta^{i}_{q+1}\|_{C_{t}L^{1+\epsilon}}+\|\theta^{i}_{q+1}\|_{L_{t}^{1}W^{1,1+\epsilon}}+l\|\rho_{q}^{i}-1\|_{C_{t,x}^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\lesssim 4^{-i}\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}\leqslant 4^{-i}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\inf_{t\in[0,1]}(\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q})

\displaystyle\geqslant-\|\theta_{q+1}^{(c,i)}+\theta_{q+1}^{(o,i)}\|_{C_{t,x}^{0}}-l\|\rho_{q}^{i}-1\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\geqslant-C\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}\geqslant-\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}},

which yields (
4.11
) for
1\leqslant i\leqslant N_{q+1}
. Here we chose
a
large enough to absorb the universal constant. Since

\rho_{q+1}^{i}-\rho_{q}^{i}=0

for

i>N_{q+1},

the estimate (
4.11
) also holds in this case.

5.5.7.
Proof of (
4.5
) for
\rho^{i}_{q+1}

By (
A.7
), (
A.9
) and Lemma
5.1
we have

\displaystyle\|\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\left\|\chi(\zeta^{i}|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}\right\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\|\Theta_{(\xi,n,i)}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\|g_{(\xi,i,d_{0}^{\prime})}\|_{C_{t}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-4d-12}\lambda_{q+1}\mu r_{\parallel}^{-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}r_{\perp}^{-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\sigma\eta^{-1-\frac{1}{d_{0}}-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{(8d+24)\alpha+d+\frac{7}{2}},

\displaystyle\|\theta_{q+1}^{(c,i)}\|_{C_{t}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{(8d+24)\alpha+d+\frac{7}{2}},

\displaystyle\|\theta_{q+1}^{(o,i)}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma^{-1}\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\|h_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}\|_{C_{t}^{1}}\left\|\mathord{{\rm div}}{\Big(}\chi(\zeta^{i}|M_{l}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M_{l}^{i}}{|M_{l}^{i}|}{\Big)}\xi{\Big)}\right\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma^{-1}\eta^{-1}l^{-6d-20}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{(12d+40)\alpha+2}.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\|\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|_{C_{t,x}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\left\|\chi(\zeta^{i}|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}\right\|_{C_{t,x}^{2}}\|\Theta_{(\xi,n,i)}g_{(\xi,i,d_{0}^{\prime})}\|_{C_{t,x}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-6d-20}\lambda_{q+1}\mu r_{\parallel}^{-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}r_{\perp}^{-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\sigma\eta^{-2}(\lambda_{q+1}\mu+\sigma\eta^{-1})\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{(12d+40)\alpha+d+\frac{11}{2}},

\displaystyle\|\theta_{q+1}^{(o,i)}\|_{C_{t,x}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma^{-1}\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\|h_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}\|_{C_{t}^{2}}\left\|\chi(\zeta^{i}|M_{l}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M_{l}^{i}}{|M_{l}^{i}|}{\Big)}\xi\right\|_{C_{t,x}^{3}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{(16d+56)\alpha+4}.

By choosing
(12d+42)\alpha<\frac{1}{2}
,
2^{i}\leqslant 2^{N_{q+1}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{\alpha}
we deduce

\displaystyle 4^{i}\|\rho^{i}_{q+1}-1\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}+\lambda_{q+1}^{-2}\|\rho^{i}_{q+1}\|_{C_{t,x}^{2}}\leqslant C_{0}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\lambda_{q}^{d+4}+\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{q+1}^{d+4}\leqslant C_{0}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\lambda_{q+1}^{d+4},

which implies (
4.5
) for
\rho^{i}_{q+1},1\leqslant i\leqslant N_{q+1}
. For
i>N_{q+1}
, the estimate (
4.5
) also holds, since

\rho_{q+1}^{i}-\rho_{q}^{i}=0

and

\lambda_{q}\leqslant\lambda_{q+1}.

5.5.8.
Proof of (
4.6
) for
1\leqslant i\leqslant N_{q+1}

We now estimate each term in the definition of
M_{q+1}^{i}
separately.

Oscillation error
M^{i}_{osc}
.
By Lemma
3.1
, the estimates for the amplitude functions and for the building blocks in Lemma
5.1
, (
A.7
) and (
A.9
) respectively we obtain

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{osc,t}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\left\|\chi(\zeta^{i}|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}\right\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\|g_{(\xi,i,d_{0}^{\prime})}\|_{W_{t}^{1,1}}\|\Theta_{(\xi,n,i)}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-4d-12}r_{\perp}^{d-1-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}r_{\parallel}^{1-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\sigma\eta^{-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{(8d+24)\alpha-\frac{1}{2N}}\lesssim 2^{-i}\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha},

where we used the choice of parameters in
(
5.1
), (
5.10
) and conditions for the parameters to have
(8d+26)\alpha<\frac{1}{2N},2^{i}\leqslant 2^{N_{q+1}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{\alpha}
.

We observe that
W_{(\xi,n,i)}\Theta_{(\xi,n,i)}
is
(\mathbb{T}/r_{\perp}\lambda_{q+1})^{d}
-periodic. So by Theorem
3.2
, the estimates for the amplitude functions and for the building blocks in Lemma
5.1
, (
A.6
), (
A.9
) and (
A.7
) respectively we have

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{osc,x}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\left\|\chi(\zeta^{i}|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}\right\|_{C_{t,x}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times(r_{\perp}\lambda_{q+1})^{-1}\|W_{(\xi,n,i)}\Theta_{(\xi,n,i)}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}\|g_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}g_{(\xi,i,d_{0}^{\prime})}\|_{L_{t}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-6d-20}(r_{\perp}\lambda_{q+1})^{-1}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{(12d+40)\alpha-\frac{1}{N}}\lesssim 2^{-i}\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha},

where we used the choice of parameters in (
5.1
), (
5.10
) and conditions on the parameters to have
(12d+42)\alpha<\frac{1}{N},2^{i}\leqslant 2^{N_{q+1}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{\alpha}
.

For the stress term
M^{i}_{osc,c}
, it holds

\displaystyle\left|M_{osc,c}^{i}\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left|\sum_{n=-1}^{2}\chi(\zeta^{i}|M_{l}^{i}|-n)M_{l}^{i}\right|+\left|\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\chi(\zeta^{i}|M_{l}^{i}|-n)(\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\frac{M_{l}^{i}}{|M_{l}^{i}|}-M_{l}^{i})\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{3}{\zeta^{i}}+\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\chi(\zeta^{i}|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\left|\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}-|M_{l}^{i}|\right|\leqslant\frac{3}{20}2^{-i}\delta_{q+2}+\frac{1}{20}2^{-i}\delta_{q+2}\leqslant\frac{1}{5}C_{0}2^{-i}\delta_{q+2}.

By the bounds (
A.9
), (
4.6
) and (
5.1
) we have

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{osc,o}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma^{-1}\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\|h_{(\xi,d_{0})}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}}\left\|\partial_{t}[\chi(\zeta|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M_{l}^{i}}{|M_{l}^{i}|}{\Big)}\xi]\right\|_{C_{t,x}^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma^{-1}l^{-4d-12}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{(8d+24)\alpha-\frac{1}{2N}}\lesssim 2^{-i}\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha},

where we used conditions on the parameters to have
(8d+26)\alpha<\frac{1}{2N},2^{i}\leqslant 2^{N_{q+1}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{\alpha}
.

In summary, we have

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{osc}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{1}}\leqslant C2^{-i}\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}+\frac{1}{5}C_{0}2^{-i}\delta_{q+2}\leqslant\frac{2}{5}C_{0}2^{-i}\delta_{q+2},

where we chose
a
large to absorb the universal constant.

Nonlinear error
M_{nonlin}^{i}
.

We first consider case Assumption
1.2
(1). Using the notation
\overline{\sigma}(\rho)=\sigma(\rho)\sigma^{T}(\rho)\rho
, we have

\displaystyle|\partial_{i}[\overline{\sigma}(\rho_{q+1}^{i})-\overline{\sigma}(\rho_{l}^{i})]|

\displaystyle\lesssim|(\partial_{i}\overline{\sigma})(\rho_{q+1}^{i})-(\partial_{i}\overline{\sigma})(\rho_{l}^{i})|+|(\partial_{\rho}\overline{\sigma})(\rho_{q+1}^{i})\partial_{i}\rho_{q+1}^{i}-(\partial_{\rho}\overline{\sigma})(\rho_{l}^{i})\partial_{i}\rho_{l}^{i}]|

\displaystyle\lesssim|\rho_{q+1}^{i}-\rho_{l}^{i}|(1+|\rho_{l}^{i}|+|\partial_{i}\rho_{l}^{i}|)+|\partial_{i}\rho_{q+1}^{i}-\partial_{i}\rho_{l}^{i}|,

which implies

\displaystyle\|\mathord{{\rm div}}[A(\rho_{q+1}^{i})\rho_{q+1}^{i}-A({\rho_{l}^{i}}){\rho_{l}^{i}}]\|_{L_{t}^{1}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{1}}(1+\|\rho_{q}^{i}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}})+\|\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{L^{1}_{t}W^{1,1}}.

Next, in case Assumption
1.2
(2), Young’s inequality yields

\displaystyle\|\mathord{{\rm div}}[A(\rho_{q+1}^{i})\rho_{q+1}^{i}

\displaystyle-A({\rho_{l}^{i}}){\rho_{l}^{i}}]\|_{L_{t}^{1}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|A(\rho_{q+1}^{i})-A(\rho_{l}^{i})\|_{C_{t,x}^{0}}\|\nabla\rho_{q+1}^{i}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{1}}+\|A(\rho_{l}^{i})\|_{C_{t,x}^{0}}\|\nabla\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{1}}

\displaystyle+\|(\mathord{{\rm div}}A(\rho_{q+1}^{i}-\rho_{l}^{i}))\rho_{l}^{i}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{1}}+\|\mathord{{\rm div}}A(\rho_{q+1}^{i})\|_{L_{t}^{1}C_{x}^{0}}\|\rho_{q+1}^{i}-\rho_{l}^{i}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}(\|\rho_{q}^{i}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}+\|\nabla\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{1}})+\|\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{L^{1}_{t}W^{1,1}}\|\rho_{q}^{i}\|_{C_{t,x}^{0}}.

Case Assumption
1.2
(3) follows directly by combining the estimates from the previous two cases.

By weak Young’s inequality and Sobolev embedding, we obtain

\displaystyle\|(V*\theta_{q+1}^{i})\rho_{l}^{i}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{1}}

\displaystyle+\|(V*\rho_{q+1}^{i})\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|V*\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{L_{t}^{1}L^{1+1/\epsilon}}\|\rho_{l}^{i}\|_{C_{t}L^{1+\epsilon}}+\|V*\rho_{q+1}^{i}\|_{L_{t}^{1}L^{1+1/\epsilon}}\|\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{C_{t}L^{1+\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{d/d-1}}\|\rho_{l}^{i}\|_{C_{t}L^{1+\epsilon}}+\|\rho_{q+1}^{i}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{d/d-1}}\|\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{C_{t}L^{1+\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{C_{t}L^{1+\epsilon}}+\|\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{d/d-1}})(\|\rho_{l}^{i}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}+\|\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{d/d-1}})

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{C_{t}L^{1+\epsilon}}+\|\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{L^{1}_{t}W^{1,1+\epsilon}})(\|\rho_{l}^{i}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}+\|\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{L^{1}_{t}W^{1,1+\epsilon}}).

Then, by the bounds in (
5.19
) and (
5.20
) we have

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{nonlin}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|\theta^{i}_{q+1}\|_{C_{t}L^{1+\epsilon}}+\|\theta^{i}_{q+1}\|_{L^{1}_{t}W^{1,1+\epsilon}})(1+\|\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}+\|\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{L^{1}_{t}W^{1,1+\epsilon}})

(5.21)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\lambda_{q}^{d+4}+1)2^{-i}\lambda_{q+1}^{-2\alpha}\lesssim 2^{-i}C_{0}\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}\leqslant\frac{1}{5}C_{0}2^{-i}\delta_{q+2},

where we used (
5.1
). We also chose

large to absorb the universal constant.

M_{com}^{i}

), (

) and Lemma

we obtain

\displaystyle\|M_{com}^{i}\|_{L_{t}^{1}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim l\|v_{q}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\|\rho_{q}^{i}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}+\|(A(\rho_{l}^{i})-A(\rho_{q}^{i}))\rho_{l}^{i}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}

\displaystyle\quad+\|A(\rho_{q}^{i})(\rho_{l}^{i}-\rho_{q}^{i})\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}+l\|A(\rho_{q}^{i})\rho_{q}^{i}\|_{C_{t,x}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\|(V*(\rho_{l}^{i}-\rho_{q}^{i}))\rho_{l}^{i}\|_{L_{t}^{1}L^{1}}+\|(V*\rho_{q}^{i})(\rho_{l}^{i}-\rho_{q}^{i})\|_{L_{t}^{1}L^{1}}+l\|(\partial_{t}+\nabla)(V*\rho_{q}^{i})\rho_{q}^{i}\|_{L_{t}^{1}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim l\|v_{q}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\|\rho_{q}^{i}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}+l(1+\|\rho_{q}^{i}\|_{C_{t,x}^{2}}+\|\rho_{q}^{i}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}^{2})\|\rho_{q}^{i}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}

(5.22)

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}l\lambda_{q}^{3d+12}\lesssim 2^{-i}C_{0}\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha/2}\leqslant\frac{1}{5}C_{0}2^{-i}\delta_{q+2},

where we used
2^{i}\leqslant 2^{N_{q+1}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{\alpha}
, and chose

large to absorb the universal constant.

Summarizing all the estimates above we obtain (
4.6
).
