6.
More singular interaction case

In this section, we follow the strategy developed in Section
4
to treat the singular interaction case.
The main idea is to remove the temporal building blocks in order to obtain stronger regularity estimates for
\rho
, which in turn allows us to justify the well-posedness of the convolutions involving singular interaction kernels. The price to pay is that we only obtain estimates in the supremum-in-time norm, and spatial integrability estimates up to
L^{d-}
.

Let
N>0,0<\epsilon_{0}\leqslant\frac{1}{4}
and
1<d_{0}<d
be given. We recall
d_{0}^{\prime}=\frac{d_{0}}{d_{0}-1}
. The iteration is again indexed by a parameter

q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}.

We consider an increasing sequence

\{\lambda_{q}\}_{q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\subset\mathbb{N}

and a sequence

\{\delta_{q}\}_{q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\subset(0,1]

defined by

\displaystyle\lambda_{q}=a^{b^{q}},\ q\geqslant 0,\ \delta_{q}=(\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2})^{d_{0}+d_{0}^{\prime}}\lambda_{1}^{2\beta}\lambda_{q}^{-2\beta},\ q\geqslant 1,\ \delta_{0}=1.

By imposing

(6.1)

\displaystyle a^{2(b-1)\beta/(d_{0}+d_{0}^{\prime})}>2,

we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{q\geqslant 1}\delta_{q}^{1/(d_{0}+d_{0}^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2}\sum_{q\geqslant 1}a^{(1-q)2(b-1)\beta/(d_{0}+d_{0}^{\prime})}\leqslant\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2}\cdot\frac{1}{1-a^{-2(b-1)\beta/(d_{0}+d_{0}^{\prime})}}<\epsilon_{0}.

Without loss of generality, we assume
T=1
from now on.
At each step
q
, we construct a family

(v_{q},\rho_{q}^{i},M_{q}^{i})_{1\leqslant i\leqslant{N}}

solving the system

(6.2)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho_{q}^{i}-\mathord{{\rm div}}\mathord{{\rm div}}\bigl(A(\rho_{q}^{i})\rho_{q}^{i}\bigr)+\mathord{{\rm div}}(v_{q}\rho_{q}^{i})+\mathord{{\rm div}}((V*\rho_{q}^{i})\rho_{q}^{i})

\displaystyle=-\mathord{{\rm div}}M_{q}^{i},

\displaystyle\mathord{{\rm div}}v_{q}

\displaystyle=0.

We define

T_{q}:=\frac{1}{3}-\sum_{1\leqslant r\leqslant q}\delta_{r}^{1/2}.

We initialize the iteration by setting

\displaystyle\rho_{0}^{i}(t,x)

\displaystyle=1+\frac{\sin 2\pi x_{1}}{4^{i}}\chi_{0}(t),\ v_{0}=\overline{v},

\displaystyle M_{0}^{i}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}\chi_{0}(t)\frac{\cos 2\pi x_{1}}{4^{i}\cdot 2\pi}(1,0,\dots,0)-\overline{v}(\rho_{0}^{i}-1)+\mathord{{\rm div}}(A(t,x,\rho_{0}^{i})\rho_{0}^{i})-(V*\rho_{0}^{i})\rho_{0}^{i},

where
x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})
and
\chi_{0}
is a smooth function satisfying

\chi_{0}(t)=0
on
[0,\frac{1}{3}],\chi_{0}(t)=1
on
[\frac{2}{3},1].
It is easy to verify that
(v_{0},\rho^{i}_{0},M^{i}_{0})_{1\leqslant i\leqslant N}
is a solution to (
4.2
). By definition, we know that
\rho_{0}^{i}=1
on
[0,T_{0}]
, and then
M_{0}^{i}=0
on
[0,T_{0}]
by using the facts that
\mathord{{\rm div}}\overline{v}=0
,
\mathord{{\rm div}}(a*1)=0
.

Then by (
3.1
) we have

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{0}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\|(a*\nabla\rho_{0}^{i})\rho_{0}^{i}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}+\|(a*\rho^{i}_{0})\nabla\rho^{i}_{0}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}+\|(b*\nabla\rho^{i}_{0})\rho^{i}_{0}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\|a*\nabla\rho_{0}^{i}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}+\|a*\rho^{i}_{0}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}+\|b*\nabla\rho^{i}_{0}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}

(6.3)

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\|\rho^{i}_{0}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\leqslant(\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2})^{d_{0}+d_{0}^{\prime}}C_{0}.

With the above assumptions in hand, our main iteration relies on the first step of iteration and reads as follows:

Proposition 6.1
.

Under the assumption of Theorem
1.4
, there exists a choice of parameters
a,b,\beta
such
that the following holds: Let
(v_{q},\rho^{i}_{q},M^{i}_{q})_{1\leqslant i\leqslant N}
be a solution to the system (
6.2
) satisfying
\int\rho^{i}_{q}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x=1
,
and

(6.4)

\displaystyle\|v_{q}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\leqslant C_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\lambda_{q}^{2d+2},\ \|\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}+\lambda_{q}^{-d-1}\|\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{C_{t,x}^{2}}\leqslant C_{0}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\lambda_{q}^{2d+2},

(6.5)

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{q}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}\leqslant C_{0}\delta_{q+1},

(6.6)

\displaystyle\rho^{i}_{q}(t)=1,M^{i}_{q}(t)=0\ {\rm on}\ [0,T_{q}].

Then there exists

(v_{q+1},\rho^{i}_{q+1},M^{i}_{q+1})_{1\leqslant i\leqslant N}
which solves (
6.2
) and satisfies (
6.4
)-(
6.6
) at the level
q+1
and for some universal constant
C_{v}\geqslant 1

(6.7)

\displaystyle\|v_{q+1}-v_{q}\|_{C_{t}L^{d_{0}}}\leqslant C_{v}C_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}},\ \ \|\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{C_{t}L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\leqslant C_{v}C_{0}^{1/{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/{d_{0}^{\prime}}}.

Moreover, we have for some
\epsilon>0

(6.8)

\displaystyle\|v_{q+1}-v_{q}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}\leqslant\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}},

(6.9)

\displaystyle\|\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{C_{t}W^{1,1+\epsilon}}\leqslant 4^{-i}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}},\ \ \inf_{t\in[0,1]}(\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q})\geqslant-\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}.

Proof of Theorem
1.4
.

We intend to start the iteration from

(v_{0},\rho^{i}_{0},M^{i}_{0})_{1\leqslant i\leqslant N}
which are defined as above.
By (
6.3
), (
6.4
)-(
6.6
) are satisfied as
\delta_{1}=(\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2})^{d_{0}+d_{0}^{\prime}}
.
Next, we use Proposition
6.1
to build inductively
(v_{q},\rho^{i}_{q},M^{i}_{q})_{1\leqslant i\leqslant N}
. By (
6.1
) and (
6.7
)-(
6.9
), the sequence
\{(v_{q},\rho^{i}_{q})_{1\leqslant i\leqslant N}\}_{q\in\mathbb{N}}
is
Cauchy in
C([0,1];L^{d_{0}})\times(C([0,1];L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}\cap W^{1,1+\epsilon}))^{N}

and we denote by
(v,\rho^{i})
its limit.

Now we need to verify
(\rho^{i},v)
solves (
1.3
). By (
3.1
), we have for
\epsilon>0
small enough,
q^{\prime}=1+\epsilon,q=\frac{q^{\prime}}{q^{\prime}-1},\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{p}-\frac{\gamma}{d}
,
p_{1}=1+\epsilon,\frac{1}{q_{1}}=\frac{1}{p_{1}}-\frac{\gamma}{d},q_{1}^{\prime}=\frac{q_{1}}{q_{1}-1}
, we have

\displaystyle\|(a*\rho)\nabla\rho

\displaystyle-(a*\tilde{\rho})\nabla\tilde{\rho}\|_{L^{1}}+\|(a*\nabla\rho)\rho-(a*\nabla\tilde{\rho})\tilde{\rho}\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|a*(\rho-\tilde{\rho})\|_{L^{q}}\|\nabla\rho\|_{L^{q^{\prime}}}+\|a*\tilde{\rho}\|_{L^{q}}\|\nabla(\rho-\tilde{\rho})\|_{L^{q^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle+\|a*\nabla(\rho-\tilde{\rho})\|_{L^{q_{1}}}\|\rho\|_{L^{q_{1}^{\prime}}}+\|a*\nabla\tilde{\rho}\|_{L^{q_{1}}}\|\rho-\tilde{\rho}\|_{L^{q_{1}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\rho-\tilde{\rho}\|_{L^{p}}\|\nabla\rho\|_{L^{q^{\prime}}}+\|\tilde{\rho}\|_{L^{p}}\|\nabla(\rho-\tilde{\rho})\|_{L^{q^{\prime}}}

(6.10)

\displaystyle+\|\nabla(\rho-\tilde{\rho})\|_{L^{p_{1}}}\|\rho\|_{L^{q_{1}^{\prime}}}+\|\nabla\tilde{\rho}\|_{L^{p_{1}}}\|\rho-\tilde{\rho}\|_{L^{q_{1}^{\prime}}}.

The similar calculation also holds for
b
. Then we know that

\displaystyle\|(a*\rho^{i}_{q})\nabla\rho^{i}_{q}

\displaystyle-(a*{\rho^{i}})\nabla{\rho^{i}}\|_{L^{1}}+\|(a*\nabla\rho_{q}^{i})\rho^{i}_{q}-(a*\nabla{\rho^{i}}){\rho^{i}}\|_{L^{1}}\to 0

By (
6.5
), (
6.6
) we then obtain that
(\rho^{i},v)
solves (
1.3
).

By (
6.1
), (
6.7
) and (
6.9
) we have

(6.11)

\displaystyle\left|\|\rho^{i}-1\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}-\|\rho^{i}_{0}-1\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}\right|\leqslant\sum_{q=0}^{\infty}\|\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}\leqslant 4^{-i}\sum_{q=0}^{\infty}\delta_{q+1}^{1/{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\leqslant 4^{-i-1},

\displaystyle\inf_{t\in[0,1]}\rho^{i}\geqslant\inf_{t\in[0,1]}\rho_{0}^{i}+\sum_{q=0}^{\infty}\inf_{t\in[0,1]}(\rho_{q+1}^{i}-\rho_{q}^{i})\geqslant\frac{3}{4}-\sum_{q=0}^{\infty}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\geqslant\frac{1}{2},

at which point, by a similar argument as before, we obtain

\rho^{i}
is nonnegative on
\mathbb{T}^{d}
, and do not coincide with each other.

By (
6.1
) and (
6.8
) we obtain that
\|v-\overline{v}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}\leqslant\sum_{q\geqslant 0}\|v_{q+1}-v_{q}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}\leqslant\sum_{q\geqslant 0}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}}\leqslant\epsilon_{0}.

By (
6.4
), (
6.7
) and interpolation, we have
|v|\in C_{t}L^{d_{0}(1+\epsilon)}
for some
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small. Since
\rho^{i}\in C_{t}L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}
, we deduce that
|v|^{1+\epsilon}\rho^{i}\in C_{t}L^{1}
. We conclude the proof by using the superposition principle.
∎

7.
Proof of Proposition
6.1

The construction and the corresponding estimates are essentially the same as those in Section
5
, after formally regarding the temporal building blocks
g_{(\xi)}
as identically equal to
1
.
We shall choose different parameters in order to obtain higher regularity for
\rho
.

7.1.
Mollification

For a sufficiently small parameter
\alpha\in(0,1)
to be chosen later, we define

l:=\lambda_{q+1}^{-3\alpha/2}\lambda_{q}^{-5d/2-5/2}.

Then

(7.1)

\displaystyle l^{-1}\leqslant\lambda_{q+1}^{2\alpha},\ \ l\lambda_{q}^{5d+5}\leqslant\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}\ll\delta_{q+2},

provided that

(7.2)

\displaystyle\alpha b>5d+5,\ \ \alpha>2\beta b.

To avoid the loss of derivatives, we first mollify the stress term. As before,
we define

\displaystyle v_{l}=(v_{q}*_{x}\phi_{l})*_{t}\varphi_{l},\ \ \rho_{l}^{i}=(\rho_{q}^{i}*_{x}\phi_{l})*_{t}\varphi_{l},\ \ M_{l}^{i}=(M_{q}^{i}*_{x}\phi_{l})*_{t}\varphi_{l}.

Moreover, we know that
\rho_{l}^{i}=1
and
M_{l}^{i}=0
on
[0,T_{q+1}]
.

By straightforward calculations we obtain

(7.3)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho_{l}^{i}-\mathord{{\rm div}}\mathord{{\rm div}}(A(\rho_{l}^{i})\rho_{l}^{i})+\mathord{{\rm div}}(v_{l}\rho_{l}^{i})+\mathord{{\rm div}}((V*\rho_{l}^{i})\rho_{l}^{i})

\displaystyle=-\mathord{{\rm div}}(M^{i}_{l}+M^{i}_{com}),\ \ \mathord{{\rm div}}v_{l}=0,

where

\displaystyle M_{com}^{i}

\displaystyle:=-v_{l}\rho^{i}_{l}+(v_{q}\rho^{i}_{q})*_{x}\phi_{l}*_{t}\varphi_{l}+\mathord{{\rm div}}(A(\rho_{l}^{i})\rho_{l}^{i})-(\mathord{{\rm div}}(A(\rho_{q}^{i})\rho_{q}^{i}))*_{x}\phi_{l}*_{t}\varphi_{l}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ -(V*\rho_{l}^{i})\rho_{l}^{i}+((V*\rho_{q}^{i})\rho_{q}^{i})*_{x}\phi_{l}*_{t}\varphi_{l}.

Finally, by Sobolev embedding, we still have for every
N\geqslant 0
,

\displaystyle\|M_{l}^{i}\|_{C_{t,x}^{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-d-\frac{1}{3}-N}\|M_{q}^{i}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}\lesssim C_{0}l^{-d-\frac{4}{3}-N}.

Here we still write
l^{-\frac{4}{3}}
so that the estimate remains formally the same as before.

7.2.
Construction of
(v_{q+1},\rho_{q+1})

To construct the perturbations of
v_{l}
and
\rho^{i}_{l}
, we still consider the space-building blocks in Appendix
A
. We consider
2N
disjoint sets
\Lambda^{1,n},\Lambda^{2,n}
defined in Appendix
A
by taking suitable rational rotations of one fixed set. We use the notation
\Lambda^{j,n}=\Lambda^{1,n}
for
j
odd, and
\Lambda^{j,n}=\Lambda^{2,n}
for
j
even.
For a fixed exponent

d_{0}>1,
and
\xi\in\Lambda:=\cup_{i=1}^{N}(\Lambda^{1,i}\cup\Lambda^{2,i})

we recall the notation

\Phi_{(\xi,d_{0})},\phi_{(\xi,d_{0})},\phi^{\prime}_{(\xi,d_{0}^{\prime})}

introduced in Appendix
A
.
We recall the parameters

\lambda,\ r_{\perp},\ r_{\parallel},\ \mu,

introduced in Appendix
A
. Their precise choice will be specified subsequently.

Remark 7.1
.

Here constructing infinitely many solutions appears to be nontrivial.
Indeed, if one attempts to treat
N
equations simultaneously, many parameters arising in the spatial building blocks (such as
n_{*}
) necessarily depend on
N
.
It then becomes necessary to carefully control the growth of these parameters, which should increase at most polynomially in
N
. In the former case, this difficulty can be overcome by introducing suitable time building blocks.

To ensure that the building blocks have mutually disjoint supports, we introduce the following lemma.

Lemma 7.2
.

(
[
BMS21
, Lemma 3]
)

Let
d\geqslant 3
. Then there exist
\{\alpha_{\xi}\}_{\xi\in\Lambda}
and
a>0
such that

(B_{a}(\alpha_{\xi})+\{s\xi\}_{s\in\mathbb{R}}+(\mathbb{Z}/r_{\perp}\lambda)^{d})\cap(B_{a}(\alpha_{\xi^{\prime}})+\{s^{\prime}\xi^{\prime}\}_{s^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}}+(\mathbb{Z}/r_{\perp}\lambda)^{d})=\varnothing,

for all
\xi,\xi^{\prime}\in\Lambda,\xi\neq\xi^{\prime}
.

Let

\chi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(-\frac{3}{4},\frac{3}{4})

be a nonnegative function such that

\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\chi(t-n)=1

for every
t\in\mathbb{R}
.
Let

\tilde{\chi}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(-\frac{4}{5},\frac{4}{5})

be a nonnegative function satisfying

\tilde{\chi}=1

on

[-\frac{3}{4},\frac{3}{4}]

and

\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\tilde{\chi}(t-n)\leqslant 2.

For
1\leqslant i\leqslant N
, we fix the parameters

\zeta:=20\delta_{q+2}^{-1}.

With these preparations, we define the rescaled building blocks by

\displaystyle W_{(\xi,n)}(x,t)

\displaystyle:=W_{(\xi,d_{0})}\Bigl(x-\alpha_{\xi},\Bigl(\frac{n}{\zeta}\Bigr)^{1/d_{0}}t\Bigr),

\displaystyle\Theta_{(\xi,n)}(x,t)

\displaystyle:=\Theta_{(\xi,d_{0}^{\prime})}\Bigl(x-\alpha_{\xi},\Bigl(\frac{n}{\zeta}\Bigr)^{1/d_{0}}t\Bigr).

By the choice of the shifts
\alpha_{\xi}
in Lemma
7.2
, the building blocks
W_{(\xi)}
have mutually disjoint supports.
Similarly, we define

V_{(\xi,n)},\Phi_{(\xi,n)},

and all the other quantities appearing in Appendix
A
.
By (
A.2
) and (
A.8
), we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Theta_{(\xi,n)}+\Bigl(\frac{n}{\zeta}\Bigr)^{1/d_{0}}\mathord{{\rm div}}\bigl(W_{(\xi,n)}\Theta_{(\xi,n)}\bigr)=0.

We next define the perturbations for the drift term.
For
1\leqslant i\leqslant N
, let

\displaystyle w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\tilde{\chi}(\zeta|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\Bigl(\frac{n}{\zeta}\Bigr)^{1/d_{0}}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}W_{(\xi,n)},

\displaystyle w_{q+1}^{(c,i)}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}-\tilde{\chi}(\zeta|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\Bigl(\frac{n}{\zeta}\Bigr)^{1/d_{0}}\frac{1}{(n_{*}\lambda_{q+1})^{2}}\nabla\Phi_{(\xi,n)}\,\xi\cdot\nabla\psi_{(\xi,n)}

\displaystyle\quad+\nabla\bigl(\tilde{\chi}(\zeta|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\bigr)\Bigl(\frac{n}{\zeta}\Bigr)^{1/d_{0}}:V_{(\xi,n)}.

By (
A.3
), we obtain

(7.4)

\displaystyle w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}+w_{q+1}^{(c,i)}=\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\mathord{{\rm div}}\Bigl(\tilde{\chi}(\zeta|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\Bigl(\frac{n}{\zeta}\Bigr)^{1/d_{0}}V_{(\xi,n)}\Bigr).

Since
V_{(\xi,n)}
is skew-symmetric, it follows that

\mathord{{\rm div}}\bigl(w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}+w_{q+1}^{(c,i)}\bigr)=0.

Finally, we define the total perturbation and the new velocity field by

\displaystyle w_{q+1}:=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bigl(w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}+w_{q+1}^{(c,i)}\bigr),\qquad v_{q+1}:=v_{l}+w_{q+1}.

Then
v_{q+1}
is mean-zero and divergence-free.
Moreover, since
M_{l}^{i}(t)=0
on
[0,T_{q+1}],
the perturbation
w_{q+1}
vanishes on
[0,T_{q+1}]
.

We next define the perturbations for the density functions.
For
1\leqslant i\leqslant N
, we set

\displaystyle\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}:=

\displaystyle\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\chi(\zeta|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\Bigl(\frac{n}{\zeta}\Bigr)^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\Gamma_{\xi}\Bigl(\frac{M_{l}^{i}}{|M_{l}^{i}|}\Bigr)\Theta_{(\xi,n)},

\displaystyle\theta_{q+1}^{(c,i)}:=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x.

By an argument analogous to (
5.8
), we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}+\mathord{{\rm div}}\bigl(w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}-M_{l}^{i}\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\partial_{t}\Bigl[\chi(\zeta|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\Bigl(\frac{n}{\zeta}\Bigr)^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\Gamma_{\xi}\Bigl(\frac{M_{l}^{i}}{|M_{l}^{i}|}\Bigr)\Bigr]\Theta_{(\xi,n)}+\mathord{{\rm div}}\Bigl(\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\chi(\zeta|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta}\frac{M_{l}^{i}}{|M_{l}^{i}|}-M_{l}^{i}\Bigr)

(7.5)

\displaystyle+\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\nabla\Bigl[\chi(\zeta|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta}\Gamma_{\xi}\Bigl(\frac{M_{l}^{i}}{|M_{l}^{i}|}\Bigr)\Bigr]\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}\bigl(W_{(\xi,n)}\Theta_{(\xi,n)}\bigr).

We now define, for every
1\leqslant i\leqslant N
,

\displaystyle\theta_{q+1}^{i}:=\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}+\theta_{q+1}^{(c,i)},\qquad\rho_{q+1}^{i}:=\rho_{l}^{i}+\theta_{q+1}^{i}.

By construction,

\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\rho_{q+1}^{i}\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x=1
.
Since

M_{l}^{i}(t)=0

on

[0,T_{q+1}],

we know
\theta_{q+1}^{i}(t)=0,\rho_{q+1}^{i}(t)=1

on

[0,T_{q+1}]
.

Moreover, by Lemma
7.2
, we obtain for

1\leqslant i\leqslant N

that

\displaystyle\mathord{{\rm div}}\bigl(w_{q+1}\theta_{q+1}^{i}\bigr)=\mathord{{\rm div}}{\Big(}(w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}+w_{q+1}^{(c,i)})\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}{\Big)}.

7.3.
Construction of the stress terms
M^{i}_{q+1}

From the definition of the perturbations, as in Section
5.4
,
we define

-{M}^{i}_{q+1}:=M^{i}_{osc}+M^{i}_{nonlin}+M^{i}_{lin}-M_{com}^{i}.

Here, we define the nonlinear error and the linear error by

\displaystyle M^{i}_{nonlin}:

\displaystyle=-\mathord{{\rm div}}((a*\rho^{i}_{q+1})\rho^{i}_{q+1}-(a*\rho^{i}_{l})\rho^{i}_{l})-(b*\nabla\rho_{q+1}^{i})\rho_{q+1}^{i}+(b*\nabla\rho_{l}^{i})\rho_{l}^{i},

\displaystyle M^{i}_{lin}:

\displaystyle=v_{l}\theta^{i}_{q+1}+w_{q+1}\rho^{i}_{l}+w_{q+1}^{(c,i)}\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}.

We define the oscillation error
M^{i}_{osc}:=M^{i}_{osc,t}+M^{i}_{osc,x}+M^{i}_{osc,c}
as

\displaystyle M^{i}_{osc,t}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\mathcal{R}_{1}{\Big(}\partial_{t}[\chi(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}\Theta_{(\xi,n)}){\Big)},

\displaystyle M^{i}_{osc,x}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\mathcal{B}_{1}{\Big(}\nabla[\chi(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}],\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}(W_{(\xi,n)}\Theta_{(\xi,n)}){\Big)},

\displaystyle M^{i}_{osc,c}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\chi(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}-M^{i}_{l}.

Since
M^{i}_{l}(t)=0,w_{q+1}(t)=0,\theta^{i}_{q+1}(t)=0
on
[0,T_{q+1}]
, we have
M_{q+1}^{i}(t)=0
on
[0,T_{q+1}]
, which implies (
6.6
) for
M_{q+1}^{i}
.

7.4.
Proof of Proposition
6.1

7.4.1.
Choice of parameters

Regarding the parameters of the building blocks, we define

\displaystyle\lambda=\lambda_{q+1},\ r_{\perp}=\lambda_{q+1}^{-1+\frac{1}{M}},\ r_{\parallel}=\lambda_{q+1}^{-1+\frac{2}{M}},\ \mu=r_{\perp}^{-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}}r_{\parallel}^{-\frac{1}{d_{0}}},

where
M>0
is a sufficiently large integer satisfying
(-1+\frac{2}{M})d(\frac{\gamma}{d}-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}})<-\frac{1}{M},(-1+\frac{2}{M})\frac{d}{d_{0}^{\prime}}<-\frac{1}{M},1+(-1+\frac{2}{M})\frac{d}{d_{0}}<-\frac{1}{M}
.
With these choices, we obtain

(7.6)

\displaystyle r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{d/\gamma}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}r_{\parallel}^{\frac{1}{d/\gamma}-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}},\ r_{\perp}^{d-1-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}}r_{\parallel}^{1-\frac{1}{d_{0}}},\ \lambda r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{1}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}r_{\parallel}^{\frac{1}{1}-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\leqslant\lambda^{-\frac{1}{M}}.

In the sequel, we shall also require (
7.2
) together with

\displaystyle\lambda_{q}^{2d+2}\leqslant\lambda_{q+1}^{\alpha},\ \ (12d+41)\alpha<\frac{1}{M}.

These conditions can be achieved as follows:
first choose
\alpha>0
sufficiently small such that

(12d+41)\alpha<\frac{1}{M},

then choose

b\in 2N\mathbb{N}

sufficiently large so that

b>\frac{5d+5}{\alpha},

and finally choose
\beta>0
sufficiently small such that

\alpha>2\beta b.

At the end, we choose
a
sufficiently large in order to absorb all implicit and universal constants arising in the subsequent estimates, and to guarantee the validity of (
6.1
).

Finally, we record the following estimate for the amplitude functions.

Lemma 7.3
.

(
[
LRZ25
, Proposition 5.2]
)
.
For
M,K\in\mathbb{N}_{0},1\leqslant i\leqslant N
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{n\geqslant 3}{\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta}{\Big)}^{M}{\Big(}\|\chi(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\|_{C_{t,x}^{K}}+\|\tilde{\chi}(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\|_{C_{t,x}^{K}}{\Big)}

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-(d+4)K-(d+2)(M+1)},

\displaystyle\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}{\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta}{\Big)}^{M}\left\|\chi(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}\right\|_{C_{t,x}^{K}}

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-(2d+8)K-(d+2)(M+1)}.

7.4.2.
Proof of (
6.7
) for
v_{q+1}-v_{q}
.

We first estimate the principal perturbations
w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}
for
1\leqslant i\leqslant N
in the
C_{t}L^{d_{0}}
-norm. By Cauchy’s inequality we have

\displaystyle|w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}|^{d_{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\tilde{\chi}(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\left|W_{(\xi,n,i)}\right|^{d_{0}}.

By applying the generalized Hölder inequality of
Theorem
3.3
in spatial direction, together with the estimates for the building blocks in (
A.6
) and Lemma
7.3
we deduce

\displaystyle\|w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}(t)\|^{d_{0}}_{L^{d_{0}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\left\|\tilde{\chi}(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}(t)|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta}\right\|_{L^{1}}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\|W_{(\xi,n,i)}\|_{C_{t}L^{d_{0}}}^{d_{0}}

\displaystyle\quad+(r_{\perp}\lambda_{q+1})^{-1}\left\|\tilde{\chi}(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}(t)|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta}\right\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\|W_{(\xi,n,i)}\|_{C_{t}L^{d_{0}}}^{d_{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|M^{i}_{l}(t)+|\zeta|^{-1}\right\|_{L^{1}}+l^{-3d-8}\lambda_{q+1}^{-\frac{1}{M}}\lesssim\|M^{i}_{l}(t)\|_{L^{1}}+C_{0}\delta_{q+1}\lesssim C_{0}\delta_{q+1},

where we used the fact that
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\tilde{\chi}(t-n)\leqslant 2
, and used the conditions on the parameters to have
(6d+16)\alpha-\frac{1}{M}<-\alpha<-2\beta
.

For the general
C_{t}L^{m}
-norm with
m\in[1,\infty]
, by the estimates for the building blocks in (
A.4
)-(
A.6
)
and the estimate for the amplitude function in Lemma
7.3
we obtain

\displaystyle\|w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|_{C_{t}L^{m}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\left\|\tilde{\chi}(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}}\right\|_{C_{t,x}^{0}}\|W_{(\xi,n,i)}\|_{C_{t}L^{m}}

(7.7)

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-2d-4}r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{m}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}}r_{\parallel}^{\frac{1}{m}-\frac{1}{d_{0}}},

\displaystyle\|w_{q+1}^{(c,i)}\|_{C_{t}L^{m}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\left\|\tilde{\chi}(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}}\right\|_{C^{1}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times{\Big(}\frac{1}{\lambda_{q+1}^{2}}\|\nabla\Phi_{(\xi,n,i)}\xi\cdot\nabla\psi_{(\xi,n,i)}\|_{C_{t}L^{m}}+\|V_{(\xi,n,i)}\|_{C_{t}L^{m}}{\Big)}

(7.8)

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-3d-8}r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{m}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}}r_{\parallel}^{\frac{1}{m}-\frac{1}{d_{0}}}\frac{r_{\perp}}{r_{\parallel}}.

With these estimates, combining with the choice of parameters in (
7.1
) we obtain

(7.9)

\displaystyle\|w_{q+1}\|_{C_{t}L^{d_{0}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C_{v}}{4}C_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}}+C\lambda_{q+1}^{(6d+17)\alpha-\frac{1}{M}}\leqslant\frac{C_{v}}{2}C_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}},

where we used conditions on the parameters to have

{(6d+18)\alpha<\frac{1}{M}},N\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{\alpha}
and chose
a
large enough to absorb the universal constant.
The above inequality yields that (
6.7
) holds for
v_{q+1}-v_{q}
:

\displaystyle\|v_{q+1}-v_{q}\|_{C_{t}L^{d_{0}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|w_{q+1}\|_{C_{t}L^{d_{0}}}+\|v_{l}-v_{q}\|_{C_{t}L^{d_{0}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{2}C_{v}C_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}}+lC_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\lambda_{q}^{d+4}\leqslant C_{v}C_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}}.

7.4.3.
Proof of (
6.8
)

Combining the bounds (
7.7
) and (
7.8
) above we obtain

\displaystyle\|v_{q+1}-v_{q}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|w_{q+1}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}+l\|v_{q}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim Nl^{-3d-8}r_{\perp}^{d-1-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}}r_{\parallel}^{1-\frac{1}{d_{0}}}+l\lambda_{q}^{d+4}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{(6d+17)\alpha-\frac{1}{M}}+\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}\leqslant\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}}.

Here we used (
7.1
), (
7.6
) and conditions on the parameters to have

{(6d+18)\alpha<\frac{1}{M}},N\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{\alpha}
. Then we chose
a
large enough to absorb the universal constant.

7.4.4.
Proof of (
6.4
) for
v_{q+1}
.

By the estimates for the building blocks in (
A.6
), (
7.4
) and the estimates for the amplitude functions in Lemma
7.3
we have

\displaystyle\|w_{q+1}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\left\|\tilde{\chi}(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}}\right\|_{C_{t,x}^{2}}\|\nabla V_{(\xi,n,i)}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim Nl^{-4d-12}\lambda_{q+1}\mu r_{\parallel}^{-\frac{1}{d_{0}}}r_{\perp}^{-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}}\lesssim N_{q+1}\lambda_{q+1}^{(8d+24)\alpha+2d+1}.

Thus, by
(8d+25)\alpha<\frac{1}{2}
we obtain the following

\displaystyle\|v_{q+1}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\leqslant\|v_{l}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}+\|w_{q+1}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\leqslant C_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\lambda_{q}^{2d+2}+\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{q+1}^{2d+2}\leqslant C_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\lambda_{q+1}^{2d+2},

where we chose
a
large enough to absorb the universal constant.

7.4.5.
Proof of (
6.7
) for
\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho_{q}^{i}

Similarly as before, we first estimate the principal perturbations
\theta^{(p,i)}_{q+1}
in
C_{t}L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}
. By the same argument as in (
5.12
), we have

\displaystyle\|\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|_{C_{t}L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}}^{d_{0}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|M^{i}_{l}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}+C_{0}\delta_{q+1}\lesssim C_{0}\delta_{q+1}.

For general
C_{t}L^{m}
-norm with
m\in[1,\infty]
, by the estimates for the building blocks in (
A.7
) and Lemma
7.3
we obtain

\displaystyle\|\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|_{C_{t}L^{m}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\left\|\chi(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}\right\|_{C_{t,x}^{0}}\|\Theta_{(\xi,n,i)}\|_{C_{t}L^{m}}

(7.10)

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-2d-4}r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{m}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}r_{\parallel}^{\frac{1}{m}-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}.

Moreover, by (
7.6
) we have

(7.11)

\displaystyle\|\theta_{q+1}^{(c,i)}\|_{C_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|_{C_{t}L^{d/\gamma}}\lesssim l^{-2d-4}r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{d/\gamma}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}r_{\parallel}^{\frac{1}{d/\gamma}-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{(4d+8)\alpha-\frac{1}{M}}\lesssim 4^{-i}\lambda_{q+1}^{-2\alpha},

where we used conditions on the parameters to have
(4d+11)\alpha<\frac{1}{M},4^{i}\leqslant 4^{N}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{\alpha}
and chose
a
large enough to absorb the universal constant.
Then, combining the above estimates together, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{C_{t}L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\theta^{i}_{q+1}\|_{C_{t}L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}}+\|\rho^{i}_{l}-\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{C_{t}L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{2}C_{v}C_{0}^{1/{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/{d_{0}^{\prime}}}+C\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}+lC_{0}^{1/{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\lambda_{q}^{d+4}\leqslant C_{v}C_{0}^{1/{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/{d_{0}^{\prime}}},

which implies (
6.7
) for
\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho_{q}^{i}
. Here we chose
a
large enough to absorb the universal constant.

7.4.6.
Proof of (
6.9
)

We first estimate
\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}
in
W^{1,1+\epsilon}
-norm for some
\epsilon>0
small enough. By the bounds for the building blocks in (
A.7
) and the bounds for the amplitude functions in Lemma
7.3
we have

\displaystyle\|\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|_{C_{t}W^{1,1+\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\left\|\chi(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}\right\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\|\Theta_{(\xi,n,i)}\|_{C_{t}W^{1,1+\epsilon}}

(7.12)

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-4d-12}\lambda_{q+1}r_{\parallel}^{\frac{1}{1+\epsilon}-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{1+\epsilon}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{(8d+24)\alpha-\frac{1}{M}+d\epsilon}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{-2\alpha},

where we chose
\epsilon>0
small enough such that
d\epsilon<\alpha
. We also used conditions on the parameters to have
(8d+27)\alpha<\frac{1}{M}
.

By the fact that
\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}
is non-negative, and the choice of parameters in (
7.1
), (
7.6
) we have

\displaystyle\|\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{C_{t}W^{1+\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|_{C_{t}W^{1,1+\epsilon}}+l\|\rho_{q}^{i}\|_{C_{t,x}^{2}}\lesssim 4^{-i}\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}\leqslant 4^{-i}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}},

(7.13)

\displaystyle\inf_{t\in[0,1]}(\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q})

\displaystyle\geqslant-\|\theta_{q+1}^{(c,i)}\|_{C_{t,x}^{0}}-l\|\rho_{q}^{i}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\geqslant-C\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}\geqslant-\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}},

which yields (
6.9
). Here we chose
a
large enough to absorb the universal constant, including
4^{i}\leqslant 4^{N}
.

7.4.7.
Proof of (
6.4
) for
\rho^{i}_{q+1}

By (
A.7
) and Lemma
7.3
we have for
j=1,2

\displaystyle\|\theta_{q+1}^{(c,i)}\|_{C_{t}^{j}}\lesssim\|\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|_{C_{t,x}^{j}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\left\|\chi(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}\right\|_{C_{t,x}^{j}}\|\Theta_{(\xi,n,i)}\|_{C_{t,x}^{j}}

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-6d-20}(\lambda_{q+1}\mu)^{j}r_{\parallel}^{-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}r_{\perp}^{-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{(12d+40)\alpha+d+j(d+1)}.

By choosing
(12d+42)\alpha<\frac{1}{2}
we deduce

\displaystyle\|\rho^{i}_{q+1}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}+\lambda_{q+1}^{-d-1}\|\rho^{i}_{q+1}\|_{C_{t,x}^{2}}\leqslant C_{0}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\lambda_{q}^{2d+2}+\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{q+1}^{2d+2}\leqslant C_{0}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\lambda_{q+1}^{2d+2},

which implies (
6.4
) for
\rho^{i}_{q+1},1\leqslant i\leqslant N
.

7.4.8.
Proof of (
6.5
)

We now estimate each term in the definition of
M_{q+1}^{i}
separately.

Oscillation error
M^{i}_{osc}
.
By Lemma
3.1
, the estimates for the amplitude functions and for the building blocks in Lemma
7.3
, (
A.7
) we obtain

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{osc,t}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\left\|\chi(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}\right\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\|\Theta_{(\xi,n,i)}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-4d-12}r_{\perp}^{d-1-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}r_{\parallel}^{1-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{(8d+24)\alpha-\frac{1}{M}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha},

where we used the choice of parameters in
(
7.1
), (
7.6
) and conditions for the parameters to have
(8d+25)\alpha<\frac{1}{M}
.

We observe that
W_{(\xi,n,i)}\Theta_{(\xi,n,i)}
is
(\mathbb{T}/r_{\perp}\lambda_{q+1})^{d}
-periodic. So by Theorem
3.2
, the estimates for the amplitude functions and for the building blocks in Lemma
7.3
, (
A.6
) and (
A.7
) respectively we have

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{osc,x}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\left\|\chi(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}\right\|_{C_{t,x}^{2}}(r_{\perp}\lambda_{q+1})^{-1}\|W_{(\xi,n,i)}\Theta_{(\xi,n,i)}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-6d-20}(r_{\perp}\lambda_{q+1})^{-1}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{(12d+40)\alpha-\frac{1}{M}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha},

where we used the choice of parameters in (
7.1
), (
7.6
) and conditions on the parameters to have
(12d+41)\alpha<\frac{1}{M}
.

For the stress term
M^{i}_{osc,c}
, it holds

\displaystyle\left|M_{osc,c}^{i}\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{3}{\zeta}+\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\chi(\zeta|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\left|\frac{n}{\zeta}-|M_{l}^{i}|\right|\leqslant\frac{3}{20}\delta_{q+2}+\frac{1}{20}\delta_{q+2}\leqslant\frac{1}{5}C_{0}\delta_{q+2}.

In summary, we have

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{osc}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}\leqslant C\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}+\frac{1}{5}C_{0}\delta_{q+2}\leqslant\frac{2}{5}C_{0}\delta_{q+2},

where we chose
a
large to absorb the universal constant.

Nonlinear error
M_{nonlin}^{i}
.

By (
6.10
),
(
7.11
) and (
7.12
) we have

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{nonlin}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{C_{t}L^{d/\gamma}}\|\nabla\rho_{q+1}^{i}\|_{C_{t}L^{1+\epsilon}}+\|\rho_{l}^{i}\|_{C_{t}L^{d/\gamma}}\|\nabla\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{C_{t}L^{1+\epsilon}}

\displaystyle+\|\nabla\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{C_{t}L^{1+\epsilon}}\|\rho_{q+1}^{i}\|_{C_{t}L^{d/\gamma}}+\|\nabla\rho_{l}^{i}\|_{C_{t}L^{1+\epsilon}}\|\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{C_{t}L^{d/\gamma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{C_{t}L^{d/\gamma}}+\|\nabla\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{C_{t}L^{1+\epsilon}})(\|\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{C_{t}L^{d/\gamma}}+\|\nabla\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{C_{t}L^{1+\epsilon}}+\|\rho_{q}^{i}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}})

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}\lambda_{q+1}^{-2\alpha}(\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}+\lambda_{q}^{2d+2})\lesssim C_{0}\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}\leqslant\frac{1}{5}C_{0}\delta_{q+2}.

Linear error
M^{i}_{lin}
.

By the estimates
in (
6.4
), (
7.7
), (
7.8
), (
7.10
) respectively we obtain

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{lin}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|v_{l}\|_{C_{t,x}^{0}}\|\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}+\|\rho^{i}_{l}\|_{C_{t,x}^{0}}\|w_{q+1}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}+\|\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|_{C_{t}L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\|w_{q+1}^{(c,i)}\|_{C_{t}L^{d_{0}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{v}C_{0}(\lambda_{q}^{2d+2}+1)\lambda_{q+1}^{(6d+16)\alpha-\frac{1}{N}}\lesssim C_{0}\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}\leqslant\frac{1}{5}C_{0}\delta_{q+2},

where we used (
7.1
) and conditions on the parameters to have
(6d+18)\alpha<\frac{1}{N}
.

Commutator error
M_{com}^{i}
.
By the bounds in (
6.4
), (
6.10
) and (
7.1
) we obtain

(7.14)

\displaystyle\|M_{com}^{i}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim l\|v_{q}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\|\rho_{q}^{i}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}+l\|\rho_{q}^{i}\|_{C_{t,x}^{2}}\|\rho_{q}^{i}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\lesssim C_{0}l\lambda_{q}^{5d+5}\lesssim C_{0}\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}\leqslant\frac{1}{5}C_{0}\delta_{q+2}.

Summarizing all the estimates above we obtain (
6.5
).

8.
Non-uniqueness of stationary solutions

In this section, we prove Theorem
1.5
by constructing non-unique stationary solutions.
For any time-independent diffusion coefficient
\sigma
satisfying Assumption
1.2
, or the singular case, we apply the convex integration method to the stationary Fokker–Planck equation (
1.6
).
Compared with previous works, the main novelty of the argument lies in the treatment of the time-independent setting. Accordingly, we mainly emphasize the ideas and estimates specific to the stationary case.

Let
N>0,0<\epsilon_{0}\leqslant\frac{1}{4}
and
1<d_{0}<d-1
be given. We recall
d_{0}^{\prime}=\frac{d_{0}}{d_{0}-1}
. The iteration is again indexed by a parameter

q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}.

We consider an increasing sequence

\{\lambda_{q}\}_{q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\subset\mathbb{N}

and a sequence

\{\delta_{q}\}_{q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\subset(0,1]

defined by

\displaystyle\lambda_{q}=a^{b^{q}},\ q\geqslant 0,\ \delta_{q}=(\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2})^{d_{0}+d_{0}^{\prime}}\lambda_{1}^{2\beta}\lambda_{q}^{-2\beta},\ q\geqslant 1,\ \delta_{0}=1.

By imposing

(8.1)

\displaystyle a^{2(b-1)\beta/(d_{0}+d_{0}^{\prime})}>2,

we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{q\geqslant 1}\delta_{q}^{1/(d_{0}+d_{0}^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2}\sum_{q\geqslant 1}a^{(1-q)2(b-1)\beta/(d_{0}+d_{0}^{\prime})}\leqslant\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2}\cdot\frac{1}{1-a^{-2(b-1)\beta/(d_{0}+d_{0}^{\prime})}}<\epsilon_{0}.

At each step
q
, we construct

(v_{q},\rho^{i}_{q},M^{i}_{q})_{1\leqslant i\leqslant N}

solving the stationary system

(8.2)

\displaystyle-\mathord{{\rm div}}\mathord{{\rm div}}(A(\rho^{i}_{q})\rho^{i}_{q})+\mathord{{\rm div}}(v_{q}\rho^{i}_{q})+\mathord{{\rm div}}((V*\rho_{q}^{i})\rho_{q}^{i})

\displaystyle=-\mathord{{\rm div}}M^{i}_{q},\qquad\mathord{{\rm div}}v_{q}=0,

where we recall the notation

A(\rho)=(\sigma\sigma^{T})(\rho).

We initialize the iteration by defining

(v_{0},\rho^{i}_{0},M^{i}_{0})

through

\displaystyle\rho^{i}_{0}(x)=1+\frac{\sin 2\pi x_{1}}{4^{i}},\ v_{0}(x)=\overline{v}(x),\ M^{i}_{0}(x)=-(\overline{v}+V*\rho_{0}^{i})\rho^{i}_{0}(x)+\mathord{{\rm div}}(A(\rho^{i}_{0})\rho^{i}_{0})(x),

where

x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{d}).

Then, by Assumption
1.2
, there exists a sufficiently large constant
C_{0}>0
such that

(8.3)

\displaystyle\|\rho^{i}_{0}\|_{C_{x}^{2}}\leqslant C_{0}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}},\ \|v_{0}\|_{C_{x}^{1}}\leqslant C_{0}^{1/d_{0}},\ \|M^{i}_{0}\|_{L^{1}}\leqslant(\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2})^{d_{0}+d_{0}^{\prime}}C_{0}.

In the singular interaction case, by (
3.1
) it holds that

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{0}\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\|(a*\nabla\rho_{0}^{i})\rho_{0}^{i}\|_{L^{1}}+\|(a*\rho^{i}_{0})\nabla\rho^{i}_{0}\|_{L^{1}}+\|(b*\nabla\rho^{i}_{0})\rho^{i}_{0}\|_{L^{1}}

(8.4)

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\|a*\nabla\rho_{0}^{i}\|_{L^{1}}+\|a*\rho^{i}_{0}\|_{L^{1}}+\|b*\nabla\rho^{i}_{0}\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim 1+\|\rho^{i}_{0}\|_{C_{x}^{1}}\leqslant(\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2})^{d_{0}+d_{0}^{\prime}}C_{0}.

With these preparations, we are ready to state the iteration proposition, which forms the basis of the whole scheme.

Proposition 8.1
.

Under the assumption of Theorem
1.5
, there exists a choice of parameters
a,b,\beta
such
that the following holds: Let
(v_{q},\rho^{i}_{q},M^{i}_{q})_{1\leqslant i\leqslant N}
be a solution to the system (
8.2
) satisfying
\int\rho^{i}_{q}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x=1
,

(8.5)

\displaystyle\|v_{q}\|_{C_{x}^{1}}\leqslant C_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\lambda_{q}^{d+1},\ \|\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{C_{x}^{1}}+\lambda_{q}^{-1}\|\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{C_{x}^{2}}\leqslant C_{0}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\lambda_{q}^{d+1},

(8.6)

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{q}\|_{L^{1}}\leqslant C_{0}\delta_{q+1}.

Then there exists

(v_{q+1},\rho^{i}_{q+1},M^{i}_{q+1})_{1\leqslant i\leqslant N}
which solves (
8.2
) and satisfies (
8.5
)-(
8.6
) at the level
q+1
and

(8.7)

\displaystyle\|v_{q+1}-v_{q}\|_{L^{d_{0}}}\leqslant C_{v}C_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}},\ \ \|\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\leqslant C_{v}C_{0}^{1/{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/{d_{0}^{\prime}}},

for some universal constant
C_{v}\geqslant 1
.
Moreover, we have for some
\epsilon>0
small enough,

(8.8)

\displaystyle\|v_{q+1}-v_{q}\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}},

(8.9)

\displaystyle\|\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{W^{1,1+\epsilon}}\leqslant 4^{-i}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}},\ \rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q}

\displaystyle\geqslant-\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}.

The proof of the main iteration, Proposition
8.1
, will be given in the next section.
Assuming Proposition
8.1
, we now complete the proof of Theorem
1.5
.

Proof of Theorem
1.5
.

We initialize the iteration from the triple

(v_{0},\rho^{i}_{0},M^{i}_{0})

defined above.
By (
8.3
) and (
8.4
), the required estimates hold at level
q=0
, since

\delta_{1}=(\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2})^{d_{0}+d_{0}^{\prime}}.

Next, applying Proposition
8.1
inductively, we construct

(v_{q},\rho^{i}_{q},M^{i}_{q})

for every

q\geqslant 1.

By (
8.1
) together with
(
8.7
)–(
8.9
), the sequence

(v_{q},\rho^{i}_{q})

is Cauchy in

L^{d_{0}}\times(L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}\cap W^{1,1+\epsilon})^{N},

and we denote its limit by

(v,\rho^{i}).

Moreover, by (
8.6
), it is straightforward to verify that

(\rho^{i},v)

solves (
1.6
).

Furthermore, by (
8.1
),
(
8.7
), and
(
8.9
), we obtain

\displaystyle|\|\rho^{i}-1\|_{L^{1}}-\|\rho^{i}_{0}-1\|_{L^{1}}|

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{q=0}^{\infty}\|\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{L^{1}}\leqslant 4^{-i}\sum_{q=0}^{\infty}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\leqslant 4^{-i-1},

\displaystyle\rho^{i}

\displaystyle\geqslant\rho^{i}_{0}+\sum_{q=0}^{\infty}(\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q})\geqslant\frac{3}{4}-\sum_{q=0}^{\infty}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}>0.

Hence, by the same calculation as before,
\rho^{i}
is nonnegative, nontrivial, and
\rho^{i}

do not coincide with each other.
Since

\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\rho_{q}^{i}\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x=1,

it follows that
\rho^{i}
is a probability density.

Moreover, by (
8.8
) we have
\|v-\overline{v}\|_{L^{1}}\leqslant\sum_{q=0}\|v_{q+1}-v_{q}\|_{L^{1}}\leqslant\sum_{q=0}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}}\leqslant\epsilon_{0}.

By (
8.5
), (
8.7
), and interpolation, we obtain

v\in L^{d_{0}(1+\epsilon)}

for some sufficiently small

\epsilon>0.

Since

\rho^{i}\in L^{d_{0}^{\prime}},

it follows that

|v|^{1+\epsilon}\rho^{i}\in L^{1}.

Therefore, we apply the superposition principle

[
Tre14
, Section 7.2]

and there exists a probability measure

\mathbf{Q}^{i}

on

C([0,\infty);\mathbb{T}^{d})

which is a martingale solution associated with
L_{\rho^{i}}
and satisfies

\displaystyle{\mathord{{\rm d}}}\mathbf{Q}^{i}\circ\Pi_{t}^{-1}=\rho^{i}\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x,\ t\geqslant 0.

The resulting solution is stationary, since the density
\rho^{i}
is independent of time.
∎

9.
Proof of Proposition
8.1

In this section, we apply the convex integration method to prove Proposition
8.1
.
The overall strategy is similar to that in Section
5.4
.
However, in the present stationary setting, we introduce new building blocks adapted to the time-independent framework, which enable us to implement the convex integration scheme and derive the desired estimates.

9.1.
Mollification

For given sufficiently small
\alpha\in(0,1)
to be determined, we take
{l}:=\lambda_{q+1}^{-3\alpha/{2}}\lambda_{q}^{-3d/2-2}
and have

(9.1)

\displaystyle{l}^{-1}\leqslant\lambda_{q+1}^{2\alpha},\ \ l\lambda_{q}^{3d+4}\leqslant\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}\ll\delta_{q+2}.

Then, in this section, we also replace
(v_{q},\rho^{i}_{q},M^{i}_{q})
by a space-direction mollified field

\displaystyle v_{l}=v_{q}*\phi_{l},\ \ \rho^{i}_{l}=\rho^{i}_{q}*\phi_{l},\ M^{i}_{l}=M^{i}_{q}*\phi_{l},

where we recall that
\phi_{l}
is a standard radial mollifiers on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
By calculation we obtain that

\displaystyle-\mathord{{\rm div}}\mathord{{\rm div}}(A(\rho^{i}_{l})\rho^{i}_{l})+\mathord{{\rm div}}(v_{l}\rho^{i}_{l})+\mathord{{\rm div}}((V*\rho_{l}^{i})\rho_{l}^{i})

\displaystyle=-\mathord{{\rm div}}(M^{i}_{l}+M^{i}_{com}),\ \ \mathord{{\rm div}}v_{l}=0,

where

\displaystyle M^{i}_{com}:

\displaystyle=-v_{l}\rho^{i}_{l}+(v_{q}\rho^{i}_{q})*\phi_{l}+\mathord{{\rm div}}(A(\rho^{i}_{l})\rho^{i}_{l})-(\mathord{{\rm div}}(A(\rho^{i}_{q})\rho^{i}_{q}))*\phi_{l}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ -(V*\rho_{l}^{i})\rho_{l}^{i}+((V*\rho_{q}^{i})\rho_{q}^{i})*_{x}\phi_{l}.

By the mollification estimate (
8.6
)
and (
9.1
) we have for
N\geqslant 0
,

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{l}\|_{C_{x}^{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-d-\frac{1}{3}-N}\|M^{i}_{q}\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim C_{0}l^{-d-\frac{1}{3}-N}.

9.2.
Construction of the perturbations

To construct the perturbations of
v_{l}
and
\rho^{i}_{l}
, we introduce in this section a family of Mikado-type building blocks, which may be regarded as a generalization of those in Appendix
A
. We consider
2N
disjoint sets
\Lambda^{1,n},\Lambda^{2,n}
defined in Appendix
A
by taking suitable rational rotations of one fixed set. We use the notation
\Lambda^{j,n}=\Lambda^{1,n}
for
j
odd, and
\Lambda^{j,n}=\Lambda^{2,n}
for
j
even.
For a fixed exponent

d_{0}>1,
and
\xi\in\Lambda:=\cup_{i=1}^{N}(\Lambda^{1,i}\cup\Lambda^{2,i})

we recall the notation

\Phi_{(\xi,d_{0})},\phi_{(\xi,d_{0})},\phi^{\prime}_{(\xi,d_{0}^{\prime})}

introduced in Appendix
A
.

Remark 9.1
.

We recall that in
[
Luo19
]
, the construction of stationary solutions requires the spatial dimension to be larger than 3.
In contrast, in the present work, we are able to employ more general
L^{d_{0}}
-based building blocks, which allows us to construct stationary solutions also in dimension three.

We recall
\alpha_{\xi}
from Lemma
7.2
, and define

\displaystyle W_{(\xi)}(x):=\xi\phi_{(\xi,d_{0})}(x-\alpha_{\xi}),\ \Theta_{(\xi)}(x):=\phi^{\prime}_{(\xi,d_{0}^{\prime})}(x-\alpha_{\xi}),

and

\displaystyle V_{(\xi)}:=\frac{1}{(n_{*}\lambda)^{2}}(\xi\otimes\nabla\Phi_{(\xi,d_{0})}-\nabla\Phi_{(\xi,d_{0})}\otimes\xi)(x-\alpha_{\xi})

with

(9.2)

\displaystyle\mathord{{\rm div}}V_{(\xi)}=W_{(\xi)}.

In this case, by choosing
\lambda
large enough, by Lemma
7.2
we have

(9.3)

\displaystyle W_{(\xi)}\Theta_{(\xi^{\prime})}\equiv 0,\ {\rm for}\ \xi\neq\xi^{\prime}\in\Lambda,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}W_{(\xi)}\Theta_{(\xi)}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x=\xi,\ \ \mathord{{\rm div}}(W_{(\xi)}\Theta_{(\xi)})=0.

Finally, we obtain that for
N\geqslant 0
and
p\in[1,\infty]
, it holds

(9.4)

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{N}W_{(\xi)}\|_{L^{p}}+\lambda\|\nabla^{N}V_{(\xi)}\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{p}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}}\lambda^{n},\ \ \ \|\nabla^{N}\Theta_{(\xi)}\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{p}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\lambda^{n},

where the implicit constants may depend on
p,N
, but are independent of
\lambda,r_{\perp}
.

With these building blocks in hand, then, we fix a parameter
\zeta=20\delta_{q+2}^{-1}
. Let

\chi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(-\frac{3}{4},\frac{3}{4})

be a nonnegative function such that

\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\chi(t-n)=1

for every
t\in\mathbb{R}
.
Let

\tilde{\chi}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(-\frac{4}{5},\frac{4}{5})

be a nonnegative function satisfying

\tilde{\chi}=1

on

[-\frac{3}{4},\frac{3}{4}]

and

\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\tilde{\chi}(t-n)\leqslant 2.

Then we define the perturbations

\displaystyle w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}:

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\tilde{\chi}(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}}W_{(\xi)},

\displaystyle w_{q+1}^{(c,i)}:

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\nabla(\tilde{\chi}(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n)){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta}{\Big)}^{1/{d_{0}}}:V_{(\xi)}.

Finally, the total perturbation and new iteration are defined by

\displaystyle w_{q+1}:=\sum_{i=1}^{N}{\Big(}w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}+w_{q+1}^{(c,i)}{\Big)},\ \ v_{q+1}:=v_{l}+w_{q+1}.

Then
w_{q+1},v_{q+1}
are both mean-zero and divergence-free functions.

Now, we define the perturbations for the stationary densities functions as

\displaystyle\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}:

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\chi(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta}{\Big)}^{1/{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}\Theta_{(\xi)},

\displaystyle\theta_{q+1}^{(c,i)}:

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x.

Then, by (
9.3
) and a similar calculation as before, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathord{{\rm div}}(w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}-M^{i}_{l})

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\nabla{\Big[}\chi(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}{\Big]}{\mathbb{P}}_{\neq 0}(W_{(\xi)}\Theta_{(\xi)})

(9.5)

\displaystyle\ \ +\mathord{{\rm div}}{\Big(}\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\chi(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}-M^{i}_{l}{\Big)}.

Finally, we define

\displaystyle\theta_{q+1}^{i}:=\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}+\theta_{q+1}^{(c,i)},\ \ \rho^{i}_{q+1}:=\rho^{i}_{l}+\theta^{i}_{q+1}.

By definition, it holds that
\int\rho^{i}_{q+1}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x=1
.

Moreover, by Lemma
7.2
, we obtain for

1\leqslant i\leqslant N

that

(9.6)

\displaystyle\mathord{{\rm div}}\bigl(w_{q+1}\theta_{q+1}^{i}\bigr)=\mathord{{\rm div}}{\Big(}(w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}+w_{q+1}^{(c,i)})\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}{\Big)}.

9.3.
Construction of the stress term
M^{i}_{q+1}

From (
9.6
) and the definition of the perturbations
we obtain

\displaystyle-\mathord{{\rm div}}M^{i}_{q+1}

\displaystyle=\mathord{{\rm div}}(w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}-M^{i}_{l})(:=\mathord{{\rm div}}M^{i}_{osc})

\displaystyle\quad-\mathord{{\rm div}}[\mathord{{\rm div}}(A(\rho^{i}_{q+1})\rho^{i}_{q+1}-A(\rho^{i}_{l})\rho^{i}_{l})-(V*\rho_{q+1}^{i})\rho_{q+1}^{i}+(V*\rho_{l}^{i})\rho_{l}^{i}](:=\mathord{{\rm div}}M^{i}_{nonlin})

\displaystyle\quad+\mathord{{\rm div}}(v_{l}\theta_{q+1}^{i}+w_{q+1}\rho^{i}_{l}+w_{q+1}^{(c,i)}\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)})(:=\mathord{{\rm div}}M^{i}_{lin})

\displaystyle\quad-\mathord{{\rm div}}M^{i}_{com},

where we define the nonlinear error
M^{i}_{nonlin}
and linear error
M^{i}_{lin}
respectively by

\displaystyle M^{i}_{nonlin}:

\displaystyle=-\mathord{{\rm div}}(A(\rho^{i}_{q+1})\rho^{i}_{q+1}-A(\rho^{i}_{l})\rho^{i}_{l})+(V*\rho_{q+1}^{i})\rho_{q+1}^{i}-(V*\rho_{l}^{i})\rho_{l}^{i},

\displaystyle M^{i}_{lin}:

\displaystyle=v_{l}\theta^{i}_{q+1}+w_{q+1}\rho^{i}_{l}+w_{q+1}^{(c,i)}\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}.

Using (
9.5
) and the inverse divergence operator
\mathcal{B}_{1}
, we define the oscillation error
M^{i}_{osc}:=M^{i}_{osc,x}+M^{i}_{osc,c}
by

\displaystyle M^{i}_{osc,x}:

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\mathcal{B}_{1}{\Big(}\nabla{\Big[}\chi(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}{\Big]},\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}(W_{(\xi)}\Theta_{(\xi)}){\Big)},

\displaystyle M^{i}_{osc,c}:

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\chi(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}-M^{i}_{l}.

Then we define

-{M}^{i}_{q+1}:=M^{i}_{osc}+M^{i}_{nonlin}+M^{i}_{lin}-M^{i}_{com}.

9.4.
Proof of Proposition
8.1

9.4.1.
Choice of parameters

Regarding the parameters of the building blocks, we define

\displaystyle\lambda=\lambda_{q+1},\ r_{\perp}=\lambda_{q+1}^{-1+\frac{1}{M}},

where we introduce a integer
M>0
large enough satisfying
0<\frac{1}{M}(\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}+1)<\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}-1,(1-\frac{1}{M})\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}>\frac{1}{M}
and
(1-\frac{1}{M})(d-1)(\frac{\gamma}{d}-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}})>\frac{1}{M}
. Then, it holds that

(9.7)

\displaystyle\lambda_{q+1}r_{\perp}^{d-1-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{-\frac{1}{M}},\ \ r_{\perp}^{d-1-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{-\frac{1}{M}},\ \ r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{d/\gamma}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{-\frac{1}{M}}.

In the sequel, we also need (
9.1
) and

(9.8)

\displaystyle\lambda_{q}^{d+1}\leqslant\lambda_{q+1}^{\alpha},\ \ (12d+41)\alpha<\frac{1}{M}.

The above conditions can be achieved as follows.
First, choose
\alpha>0
sufficiently small such that
(12d+41)\alpha<\frac{1}{M}.
Next, choose
b\in M\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large so that

b>\frac{3d+4}{\alpha}.
We then select

\beta>0
sufficiently small such that

\alpha>2\beta b.
Finally, we choose

a
sufficiently large in order to absorb all implicit and universal constants arising in the subsequent estimates and to guarantee the validity of (
8.1
).

In the end, we establish the estimate of the amplitude functions as in Lemma
5.1
.

Lemma 9.2
.

For
M,K\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
1\leqslant i\leqslant N
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{n\geqslant 3}{\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta}{\Big)}^{M}{\Big(}\|\chi(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\|_{C_{x}^{K}}+\|\tilde{\chi}(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\|_{C_{x}^{K}}{\Big)}

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-(d+4)K-(d+2)(M+1)},

\displaystyle\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}{\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta}{\Big)}^{M}\left\|\chi(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}\right\|_{C_{x}^{K}}

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-(2d+8)K-(d+2)(M+1)}.

9.4.2.
Proof of (
8.7
) for
v_{q+1}-v_{q}
.

We first estimate the principal perturbations
{w}^{(p,i)}_{q+1}
. By Cauchy’s inequality we have

\displaystyle|w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}|^{d_{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\tilde{\chi}(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\left|W_{(\xi)}\right|^{d_{0}}.

By applying the generalized Hölder inequality of
Theorem
3.3
in spatial direction, together with the estimates for the building blocks in (
9.4
) and Lemma
9.2
we deduce

\displaystyle\|w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|^{d_{0}}_{L^{d_{0}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\left\|\tilde{\chi}(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta}\right\|_{L^{1}}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\|W_{(\xi)}\|_{L^{d_{0}}}^{d_{0}}

\displaystyle\quad+(r_{\perp}\lambda_{q+1})^{-1}\left\|\tilde{\chi}(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta}\right\|_{C_{x}^{1}}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\|W_{(\xi)}\|_{L^{d_{0}}}^{d_{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|M^{i}_{l}+\zeta^{-1}\right\|_{L^{1}}+l^{-3d-8}\lambda_{q+1}^{-\frac{1}{M}}\lesssim\|M^{i}_{l}\|_{L^{1}}+\delta_{q+1}\lesssim C_{0}\delta_{q+1},

where we used the condition on the parameters to have
(6d+16)\alpha-\frac{1}{M}<-\alpha<-2\beta
.

In the next step, we estimate in general
L^{m}
-norm with
m\in[1,\infty]
. By (
9.4
) and Lemma
9.2
we obtain

(9.9)

\displaystyle\|w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|_{L^{m}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\left\|\tilde{\chi}(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}}\right\|_{C_{x}^{0}}\|W_{(\xi)}\|_{L^{m}}\lesssim l^{-2d-4}r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{m}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}},

(9.10)

\displaystyle\|w_{q+1}^{(c,i)}\|_{L^{m}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\left\|\tilde{\chi}(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}}\right\|_{C^{1}_{x}}\|V_{(\xi)}\|_{L^{m}}\lesssim l^{-3d-8}r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{m}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}.

We then have

(9.11)

\displaystyle\|w_{q+1}\|_{L^{d_{0}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim NC_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}}+N\lambda_{q+1}^{(6d+16)\alpha-1}\leqslant\frac{C_{v}}{2}C_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}},

where we used conditions on the parameters to have

(6d+16)\alpha-1<-\alpha<-2\beta
.
The above inequality together with (
8.5
) and (
9.1
) implies (
8.7
) for
v_{q+1}
:

\displaystyle\|v_{q+1}-v_{q}\|_{L^{d_{0}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|w_{q+1}\|_{L^{d_{0}}}+l\|v_{q}\|_{C_{x}^{1}}\leqslant\frac{{C}_{v}}{2}C_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}}+lC_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\lambda_{q}^{d+1}\leqslant C_{v}C_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}}.

9.4.3.
Proof of (
8.5
) for
v_{q+1}
.

By the estimates for the building blocks in (
9.4
) and Lemma
9.2
we have

\displaystyle\|w_{q+1}\|_{C_{x}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\left\|\tilde{\chi}(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}}\right\|_{C_{x}^{2}}\|V_{(\xi)}\|_{C_{x}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim Nl^{-4d-12}\lambda_{q+1}r_{\perp}^{-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{(8d+24)\alpha+d}.

Thus, by
(8d+24)\alpha<1
we obtain

\displaystyle\|v_{q+1}\|_{C_{x}^{1}}\leqslant\|v_{l}\|_{C_{x}^{1}}+\|w_{q+1}\|_{C_{x}^{1}}\leqslant C_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\lambda_{q}^{d+1}+\lambda_{q+1}^{d+1}\leqslant C_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\lambda_{q+1}^{d+1},

where we chose
a
large enough to absorb the universal constant.

9.4.4.
Proof of (
8.8
)

By (
9.7
), (
9.9
) and (
9.10
), it holds that

\displaystyle\|w_{q+1}\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim Nl^{-2d-4}r_{\perp}^{{d-1}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}}\lesssim C_{0}\lambda_{q+1}^{(4d+8)\alpha-\frac{1}{M}},

which together with (
8.1
), (
8.5
) implies

\displaystyle\|v_{q+1}-v_{q}\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim C_{0}\lambda_{q+1}^{(4d+8)\alpha-\frac{1}{M}}+lC_{0}\lambda_{q}^{d+1}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}\leqslant\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}},

where we used the conditions on the parameters to have
(4d+9)\alpha<\frac{1}{M}
. We also chose
a
large to absorb the universal constant.

9.4.5.
Proof of (

) for
\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q}

We first estimate the principal perturbations
\theta^{(p,i)}_{q+1}
. By the same argument as in (
5.12
),

\displaystyle\|\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|^{d_{0}^{\prime}}_{L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|M^{i}_{l}\|_{L^{1}}+\delta_{q+1}\lesssim C_{0}\delta_{q+1}.

Then we estimate in general
L^{m}
-norm with
m\in[1,\infty]
, by the estimates for the building blocks in (
9.4
) and Lemma
9.2
we obtain

(9.12)

\displaystyle\|\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|_{L^{m}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\left\|\chi(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}\right\|_{C_{x}^{0}}\|\Theta_{(\xi)}\|_{L^{m}}\lesssim l^{-2d-4}r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{m}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}},

which implies that by (
9.7
)

(9.13)

\displaystyle|\theta_{q+1}^{(c,i)}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|_{L^{d/\gamma}}\lesssim l^{-2d-4}r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{d/\gamma}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{(4d+8)\alpha-\frac{1}{M}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{-2\alpha},

where we used conditions on the parameters to have
(4d+10)\alpha<\frac{1}{M}
and chose
a
large enough to absorb the universal constant.
Then together with (
9.1
) we imply

\displaystyle\|\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\theta^{i}_{q+1}\|_{L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}}+l\|\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{C_{x}^{1}}\lesssim C_{0}^{1/{d_{0}^{\prime}}}(\delta_{q+1}^{1/{d_{0}^{\prime}}}+\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha})\leqslant C_{v}C_{0}^{1/{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/{d_{0}^{\prime}}}.

9.4.6.
Proof of (
8.9
)

We first estimate
\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}
in

-norm for some

. By (

) and Lemma

we have

\displaystyle\|\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|_{W^{1,1+\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\left\|\chi(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}\right\|_{C_{x}^{1}}\|\Theta_{(\xi)}\|_{W^{1,1+\epsilon}}

(9.14)

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-4d-12}\lambda_{q+1}r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{1+\epsilon}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{(8d+24)\alpha-\frac{1}{M}+d\epsilon}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{-2\alpha},

where we chose
\epsilon>0
small enough to ensure
d\epsilon<\alpha
, and used (
9.7
) and (
9.8
). We also used conditions on the parameters to have
(8d+27)\alpha<\frac{1}{M}
.

Since
\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}
is non-negative, by the choice of parameters in (
9.1
) and (
9.13
) we have

\displaystyle\|\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{W^{1,1+\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\theta^{i}_{q+1}\|_{W^{1,1+\epsilon}}+l\|\rho_{q}^{i}\|_{C_{x}^{2}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}+l\lambda_{q}^{d+1}\leqslant 4^{-i}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q}

\displaystyle\geqslant-|\theta_{q+1}^{(c,i)}|-l\|\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{C_{x}^{1}}\geqslant-C\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}\geqslant-\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}},

which yields (
8.9
). Here we chose
a
large enough to absorb the universal constant.

9.4.7.
Proof of (
8.5
) for
\rho^{i}_{q+1}

By (

) and Lemma
9.2
we have for

\displaystyle\|\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|_{C_{x}^{j}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\left\|\chi(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}\right\|_{C_{x}^{j}}\|\Theta_{(\xi)}\|_{C_{x}^{j}}

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-6d-20}\lambda_{q+1}^{j}r_{\perp}^{-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{(12d+40)\alpha+d+j-1}.

By choosing
