we deduce

\displaystyle\|\rho^{i}_{q+1}\|_{C_{x}^{1}}+\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\|\rho^{i}_{q+1}\|_{C_{x}^{2}}\leqslant C_{0}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\lambda_{q}^{d+1}+\lambda_{q+1}^{d+1}\leqslant C_{0}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\lambda_{q+1}^{d+1},

which implies (
8.5
). Here we chose
a
large enough to absorb the universal constant.

9.4.8.
Proof of (
8.6
)

We estimate each terms in the definition of
M^{i}_{q+1}
separately.

Oscillation error
M^{i}_{osc}
.

We observe that
W_{(\xi)}\Theta_{(\xi)}
is
(\mathbb{T}/r_{\perp}\lambda_{q+1})^{d}
-periodic, so by Theorem
3.2
, Lemma
9.2
and (
9.4
) we have

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{osc,x}\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\left\|\chi(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}\right\|_{C_{x}^{2}}(r_{\perp}\lambda_{q+1})^{-1}\|W_{(\xi)}\Theta_{(\xi)}\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-6d-20}(r_{\perp}\lambda_{q+1})^{-1}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{(12d+40)\alpha-\frac{1}{M}}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}\leqslant\frac{1}{5}C_{0}\delta_{q+2},

where we used the conditions on the parameters to have
(12d+41)\alpha<\frac{1}{M}
.

The estimate on the stress term
M^{i}_{osc,c}
is directly from Section
9.4.8
:

\displaystyle\left|M^{i}_{osc,c}\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{3}{\zeta}+\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\chi(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\left|\frac{n}{\zeta}-|M^{i}_{l}|\right|\leqslant\frac{1}{5}C_{0}\delta_{q+2}.

Nonlinear error
M^{i}_{nonlin}
.
Under Assumption
1.2
, by the calculation in (
5.21
), the bounds in (
8.5
), (
9.1
) and (
9.14
) we have

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{nonlin}\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\theta^{i}_{q+1}\|_{W^{1,1+\epsilon}}(1+\|\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{C_{x}^{1}}+\|\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{W^{1,1+\epsilon}})

\displaystyle\lesssim(\lambda_{q}^{d+1}+1)\lambda_{q+1}^{-2\alpha}\lesssim C_{0}\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}\leqslant\frac{1}{5}C_{0}\delta_{q+2}.

For the singular interaction case, by (
6.10
), we have

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{nonlin}\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{L^{d/\gamma}}+\|\nabla\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{L^{1+\epsilon}})(\|\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{L^{d/\gamma}}+\|\nabla\theta_{q+1}^{i}\|_{L^{1+\epsilon}}+\|\rho_{q}^{i}\|_{C_{x}^{1}})

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}\lambda_{q+1}^{-2\alpha}(1+\lambda_{q}^{d+1})\lesssim C_{0}\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}\leqslant\frac{1}{5}C_{0}\delta_{q+2}.

Linear error
M^{i}_{lin}
.

Together with the estimates
in (
8.5
), (
9.1
), (
9.9
), (
9.10
) and (
9.13
) we obtain

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{lin}\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|v_{l}\|_{C_{x}^{0}}\|\theta^{i}_{q+1}\|_{L^{1}}+\|\rho^{i}_{l}\|_{C_{x}^{0}}\|w_{q+1}\|_{L^{1}}+\|\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|_{L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\|w_{q+1}^{(c,i)}\|_{L^{d_{0}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{v}C_{0}(\lambda_{q}^{d+1}+1)\lambda_{q+1}^{(4d+8)\alpha-\frac{1}{M}}\lesssim C_{0}\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}\leqslant\frac{1}{5}C_{0}\delta_{q+2},

where we used the conditions on the parameters to have
(4d+10)\alpha<\frac{1}{M}
.

Commutator error
M^{i}_{com}
.
By the calculation as in (
5.22
) or (
7.14
), the bounds in (
8.5
), (
9.1
) and Lemma
4.1
we obtain

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{com}\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim l\|v_{q}\|_{C_{x}^{1}}\|\rho_{q}^{i}\|_{C_{x}^{1}}+l(1+\|\rho_{q}^{i}\|_{C_{x}^{2}}+\|\rho_{q}^{i}\|_{C_{x}^{1}}^{2})\|\rho_{q}^{i}\|_{C_{x}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}l\lambda_{q}^{3d+4}\lesssim C_{0}\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}\leqslant\frac{1}{5}C_{0}\delta_{q+2},

where we also chose
a
large to absorb the universal constant.

Summarizing all the estimates above we obtain (
8.6
) at the level
q+1
.

10.
Application to the whole space case

In this section, we consider the whole-space setting and prove Theorem
1.7
and Theorem
1.6
. The non-uniqueness solutions to linear Fokker–Planck equations on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
was established in
[
LR25
]
. A key ingredient in the whole-space construction is the Bogovskii operator, which replaces the inverse divergence operator used in the periodic setting.
As a inverse of the divergence operator, the Bogovskii operator preserves compact support: when applied to a compactly supported function, the resulting vector field remains compactly supported. This property allows us to localize the entire convex integration construction to a fixed domain, say
[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d}.

The overall construction and the corresponding estimates are largely analogous to those developed in the torus setting. For this reason, we omit many repetitive computations and focus only on the modifications required in the whole-space case.
Moreover, the solutions constructed here may be viewed as small perturbations of a stationary solution
\rho^{st}
, which plays a role analogous to that of the uniform distribution in the periodic setting.

10.1.
Bogovskii operator

Let
K\subset\mathbb{R}^{d},d\geqslant 2
be a bounded and star shaped domain with respect to a ball
K^{\prime}\subset\subset K
.
We choose a non-negative
\omega\in C_{c}^{\infty}(K^{\prime})
with
\int_{K^{\prime}}\omega{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x=1
and define for

g\in C_{c}^{\infty}(K)

\mathcal{B}_{\omega}g(x):=\int_{K}g(y)\,\frac{x-y}{\lvert x-y\rvert^{n}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\omega\!\left(x+r\,\frac{x-y}{\lvert x-y\rvert}\right)(|x-y|+r)^{n-1}\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}r\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}y.

By rewriting

\mathcal{B}_{\omega}g(x):=\int_{K}g(y)(x-y)\int_{1}^{\infty}\omega\!\left(y+r(x-y)\right)r^{n-1}\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}r\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}y,

the following Lemma is well-known (see c.f.
[
Bog79
, Theorem 1]
).

Proposition 10.1
.

Let
k\geqslant 0,1<p<\infty
. Let
f\in W_{0}^{k,p}(K)
. Then it holds that
\mathcal{B}_{\omega}(C_{c}^{\infty}(K))\subset C_{c}^{\infty}(K)^{d},
and

\displaystyle\mathord{{\rm div}}\mathcal{B}_{\omega}f=f-\omega\int_{K}f{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x.

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{B}_{\omega}f\|_{W^{k+1,p}(K)}\lesssim_{\omega}\|f\|_{W^{k,p}(K)}.

Then, we introduce the the bilinear version
\mathcal{R}_{\omega}:C^{\infty}_{c}(K;\mathbb{R})\times C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})\to C_{c}^{\infty}(K;\mathbb{R}^{d})
by

\mathcal{R}_{\omega}(v,f):=v\nabla\Delta^{-1}f-\mathcal{B}_{\omega}{\Big(}\nabla v\cdot\nabla\Delta^{-1}f{\Big)}.

Here
C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
denotes the space of smooth
\mathbb{T}^{d}
-periodic functions on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with zero-mean.

Proposition 10.2
.

Let
k\geqslant 0,1<p<\infty
. For any
v\in C^{\infty}_{c}(K;\mathbb{R})
and
f\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathbb{R})
, we have

\mathord{{\rm div}}(\mathcal{R}_{\omega}(v,f))=vf-\omega\int_{K}vf{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x,

and for
\sigma\in\mathbb{N}
,

\|\mathcal{R}_{\omega}(v,f(\sigma\cdot))\|_{W^{k,p}(K)}\lesssim\sigma^{k-1}\|v\|_{C^{k+1}(K)}\|f\|_{W^{k,p}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}.

10.2.
Some estimates on
\rho^{st}

We collect below several estimates that will be used throughout the remainder of this section.

Lemma 10.3
.

The stationary solution satisfies that

(10.1)

\displaystyle\|\rho^{st}\|_{W^{2,\infty}}<\infty.

Proof.

By rewriting
g(\rho^{st}(x))=-\Phi(x)+\mu^{st}
, taking derivative and using the monotony of
g^{-1}
, it holds that

\displaystyle|\rho^{st}(x)|\leqslant g^{-1}(\mu^{st})<\infty.

\displaystyle|\nabla\rho^{st}(x)|\leqslant|g^{\prime}(\rho^{st}(x))|^{-1}|\nabla\Phi|\lesssim|\rho^{st}(x)|\Phi^{m}\lesssim e^{-c\Phi}\Phi^{m}<\infty,

where we also used that
\rho^{st}=g^{-1}(-\Phi+\mu^{st})\lesssim e^{c(-\Phi+\mu^{st})}
.

By calculation, we know that

\displaystyle|g^{\prime\prime}(x)|\leqslant\frac{|\beta^{\prime\prime}(x)xb(x)|+|\beta^{\prime}(x)(b(x)+xb^{\prime}(x))|}{(xb(x))^{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{x}+\frac{1}{x^{2}},

which implies that

\displaystyle|g^{\prime\prime}(\rho^{st}(x))(\nabla\rho^{st}(x))^{2}|\lesssim|g^{\prime\prime}(\rho^{st}(x))||\rho^{st}(x)|^{2}\Phi^{2m}\lesssim(1+|\rho^{st}(x)|)\Phi^{2m}\lesssim\Phi^{2m}.

Then

\displaystyle|\nabla^{2}\rho^{st}|\leqslant|g^{\prime}(\rho^{st}(x))|^{-1}(|g^{\prime\prime}(\rho^{st}(x))(\nabla\rho^{st}(x))^{2}|+|\nabla^{2}\Phi|)\lesssim\rho^{st}\Phi^{2m}\lesssim e^{-c\Phi}\Phi^{2m}<\infty.

∎

Moreover, by definition,
\rho^{st}
is a strictly positive function. Since
[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d}
is compact and
\rho^{st}
is continuous, there exists a constant
c_{\mathrm{in}}>0
such that

(10.2)

\displaystyle\rho^{st}\geqslant 1_{[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d}}c_{in}.

10.3.
The main iteration used in the proof of Theorem
1.7

Let
N>0,0<\epsilon_{0}\leqslant{\mathord{{\rm min}}}\{\frac{1}{8},\frac{1}{2^{d+10}}c_{in}\}
and
1<d_{0}<d-1
be given. The iteration is again indexed by a parameter

q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}.

We consider the sequences

\displaystyle\lambda_{q}=a^{b^{q}},\ q\geqslant 0,\ \delta_{q}=(\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2})^{d_{0}+d_{0}^{\prime}}\lambda_{1}^{2\beta}\lambda_{q}^{-2\beta},\ q\geqslant 1,\ \delta_{0}=1.

By (
8.1
)
we obtain
\sum_{q\geqslant 1}\delta_{q}^{1/(d_{0}+d_{0}^{\prime})}<\epsilon_{0}.

At each step
q
, we construct

(v_{q},\rho^{i}_{q},M^{i}_{q})_{1\leqslant i\leqslant N}

solving the stationary system on the whole space:

\displaystyle-\mathord{{\rm div}}(\beta^{\prime}(\rho_{q}^{i}+\rho^{st})\nabla(\rho_{q}^{i}+\rho^{st}))+\mathord{{\rm div}}(v_{q}(\rho_{q}^{i}+\rho^{st}))+\mathord{{\rm div}}(B(\rho_{q}^{i}+\rho^{st}))

\displaystyle=-\mathord{{\rm div}}M^{i}_{q},

(10.3)

\displaystyle\mathord{{\rm div}}v_{q}

\displaystyle=0,

where we rewrite the notation

B(\rho)=Eb(\rho)\rho.

In particular, we also need the family
(v_{q},\rho^{i}_{q},M^{i}_{q})_{1\leqslant i\leqslant N}
has compact support on
\Omega_{q}\subset[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
, where

\displaystyle\Omega_{q}:={\Big[}-\frac{1}{3}-\sum_{1\leqslant r\leqslant q}{\delta}_{r}^{1/2},\frac{1}{3}+\sum_{1\leqslant r\leqslant q}{\delta}_{r}^{1/2}{\Big]}^{d}.

In fact, we will apply the convex integration on the
[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d}
with zero boundary condition.

We initialize the iteration by defining

(v_{0},\rho^{i}_{0},M^{i}_{0})_{1\leqslant i\leqslant N}

through

\displaystyle\rho^{i}_{0}

\displaystyle=4^{-i}F,\ \ v_{0}=0,

\displaystyle M^{i}_{0}

\displaystyle=\beta^{\prime}(\rho_{0}^{i}+\rho^{st})\nabla(\rho_{0}^{i}+\rho^{st})-\beta^{\prime}(\rho^{st})\nabla(\rho^{st})-B(\rho_{0}^{i}+\rho^{st})+B(\rho^{st}),

where

F(x)
is a smooth mean-zero bounded function with support in
\Omega_{0}
, and satisfying
\|F\|_{C_{x}^{0}}=\frac{1}{2}c_{in},\|F\|_{L^{1}}\geqslant\frac{1}{2^{d+1}}c_{in}
by multiplying by a suitable constant. In particular, since
\rho^{st}
is a stationary solution, we know that
(v_{0},\rho^{i}_{0},M^{i}_{0})
is a solution to (
10.3
), and
\supp\rho_{0}^{i},\supp v_{0},\supp M_{0}^{i}\subset\Omega_{0}
.
Also, there exists a sufficiently large constant
C_{0}>0
such that

\displaystyle\|\rho^{i}_{0}\|_{C_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{0}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}},\ \|v_{0}\|_{C_{x}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant C_{0}^{1/d_{0}},

(10.4)

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\beta^{\prime}\|_{C^{1}}+\|E\|_{C_{x}^{1}([-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d})}\|b\|_{C^{1}}\leqslant(\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2})^{d_{0}+d_{0}^{\prime}}C_{0}.

Our main iteration procedure reads as follows:

Proposition 10.4
.

Under the assumption of Theorem
1.7
, there exists a choice of parameters
a,b,\beta
such
that the following holds: Let
(v_{q},\rho^{i}_{q},M^{i}_{q})_{1\leqslant i\leqslant N}
be a solution to the system (
10.3
) satisfying

(10.5)

\displaystyle\|v_{q}\|_{C_{x}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant C_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\lambda_{q}^{d+1},\ \|\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{C_{x}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\lambda_{q}^{-1}\|\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{C_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant C_{0}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\lambda_{q}^{d+1},

(10.6)

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{q}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant C_{0}\delta_{q+1},

(10.7)

\displaystyle\supp\rho_{q}^{i},\supp v_{q},\supp M_{q}^{i}\subset\Omega_{q}.

Then there exists

(v_{q+1},\rho^{i}_{q+1},M^{i}_{q+1})_{1\leqslant i\leqslant N}
which solves (
10.3
) and satisfies (
10.5
)-(
10.7
) at the level
q+1
and

(10.8)

\displaystyle\|v_{q+1}-v_{q}\|_{L^{d_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant C_{v}C_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}},\ \ \|\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant C_{v}C_{0}^{1/{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/{d_{0}^{\prime}}},

(10.9)

\displaystyle\|v_{q+1}-v_{q}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+4^{i}\|\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{W^{1,1+\epsilon}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}},\ \ \rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q}\geqslant-\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}.

for some universal constant
C_{v}\geqslant 1
.

By (
10.7
), we know that all the bounds on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
are in fact restricted on the domain
[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d}
, so the estimates are similar to Section
9
.
The proof of the main iteration will be given below.
Assuming Proposition
10.4
, we now complete the proof of Theorem
1.7
.

Proof of Theorem
1.7
.

We initialize the iteration from the triple

(v_{0},\rho^{i}_{0},M^{i}_{0})

defined above.
By (
10.4
), the required estimates hold at level
q=0
.
Next, applying Proposition
10.4
inductively, we construct

(v_{q},\rho^{i}_{q},M^{i}_{q})

for every

q\geqslant 1.

By
(
10.8
)–(
10.9
), the sequence

(v_{q},\rho^{i}_{q})

is Cauchy in

L^{d_{0}}\times(L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}\cap W^{1,1+\epsilon})^{N},

and we denote its limit by

(v,\rho^{i}).
We then define
\overline{\rho}^{i}:=\rho^{i}+\rho^{st}
.
By (
10.6
), it is straightforward to verify that

(\overline{\rho}^{i},v)

solves (
1.7
).

Furthermore, by (
10.2
),
(
10.8
), and
(
10.9
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|v\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{q=0}\|v_{q+1}-v_{q}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant\sum_{q=0}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}}\leqslant\epsilon_{0}.

\displaystyle\|\rho^{i}-\rho_{0}^{i}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{q=0}^{\infty}\|\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant 4^{-i}\sum_{q=0}^{\infty}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\leqslant 4^{-i}\frac{c_{in}}{2^{d+10}},

\displaystyle\overline{\rho}^{i}

\displaystyle\geqslant\rho^{st}+\rho^{i}_{0}+\sum_{q=0}^{\infty}(\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q})1_{[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d}}\geqslant 1_{[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d}}(c_{in}-\frac{1}{2}c_{in}-\sum_{q=0}^{\infty}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}})>0.

Hence, by noticing that
\|\rho^{i}_{0}-\rho_{0}^{j}\|_{L^{1}}\geqslant|4^{-i}-4^{-j}|\cdot\frac{c_{in}}{2^{d+1}}
, it holds that
\overline{\rho}^{i}
is nonnegative, and
\rho^{i}

do not coincide with each other.
Since

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{q}^{i}\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x=0,

it follows that
\overline{\rho}^{i}
is a probability density.

Since

v\in L^{d_{0}},\ \overline{\rho}^{i}\in L^{d_{0}^{\prime}},
we apply the superposition principle
[
Tre16
]

and there exists a probability measure

\mathbf{Q}^{i}

on

C([0,\infty);\mathbb{R}^{d})

which is a stationary solution and satisfies

{\mathord{{\rm d}}}\mathbf{Q}^{i}\circ\Pi_{t}^{-1}=\overline{\rho}^{i}\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x,\ t\geqslant 0.

Then, a standard result implies that there exists a
d
-dimensional Brownian motion
W_{t},t\geqslant 0
, on a stochastic basis and continuous measurable maps
X^{i}_{t},1\leqslant i\leqslant N
satisfying the corresponding (DD)SDE.
∎

10.4.
Proof of Proposition
10.4

We choose the all same parameter as in Section
9.4.1
.

First, we also replace
(v_{q},\rho^{i}_{q},M^{i}_{q})
by a space-direction mollified field

\displaystyle v_{l}=v_{q}*\phi_{l},\ \ \rho^{i}_{l}=\rho^{i}_{q}*\phi_{l},\ M^{i}_{l}=M^{i}_{q}*\phi_{l},

where we recall that
\phi_{l}
is a standard radial mollifiers supported on
[-1,1]^{d}
.
By calculation we obtain that

\displaystyle-\mathord{{\rm div}}(\beta^{\prime}(\rho^{i}_{l}+\rho^{st})\nabla(\rho^{i}_{l}+\rho^{st}))+\mathord{{\rm div}}(v_{l}(\rho^{i}_{l}+\rho^{st}))+\mathord{{\rm div}}(B(\rho^{i}_{l}+\rho^{st}))

\displaystyle=-\mathord{{\rm div}}(M^{i}_{l}+M^{i}_{com}),

\displaystyle\mathord{{\rm div}}v_{l}

\displaystyle=0,

where

\displaystyle M^{i}_{com}:=

\displaystyle-v_{l}(\rho^{i}_{l}+\rho^{st})+(v_{q}(\rho^{i}_{q}+\rho^{st}))*\phi_{l}

\displaystyle+\beta^{\prime}(\rho^{i}_{l}+\rho^{st})\nabla(\rho^{i}_{l}+\rho^{st})-(\beta^{\prime}(\rho^{i}_{q}+\rho^{st})\nabla(\rho^{i}_{q}+\rho^{st}))*_{x}\phi_{l}

\displaystyle-\beta^{\prime}(\rho^{st})\nabla\rho^{st}+(\beta^{\prime}(\rho^{st})\nabla\rho^{st})*_{x}\phi_{l}

\displaystyle-B(\rho_{l}^{i}+\rho^{st})+B(\rho_{q}^{i}+\rho^{st})*_{x}\phi_{l}+B(\rho^{st})-B(\rho^{st})*_{x}\phi_{l}.

Here we used the fact that
\rho^{st}
is a stationary solution. Since
\supp\rho_{q}^{i},\supp v_{q},\supp M_{q}^{i}\subset\Omega_{q}
by (
10.7
), we know that
\supp\rho_{l}^{i},\supp v_{l},\supp M_{l}^{i}\subset\Omega_{q+1}
, and then
\supp M_{com}^{i}\subset\Omega_{q+1}
.

Then, we define the perturbations

\displaystyle w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}:

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\tilde{\chi}(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}}W_{(\xi)},

\displaystyle w_{q+1}^{(c,i)}:

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\nabla{\Big(}\tilde{\chi}(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big)}{\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta}{\Big)}^{1/{d_{0}}}:V_{(\xi)},

and the total perturbation and new iteration

\displaystyle w_{q+1}:=\sum_{i=1}^{N}{\Big(}w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}+w_{q+1}^{(c,i)}{\Big)},\ \ v_{q+1}:=v_{l}+w_{q+1}.

Here we note that
W_{(\xi)},V_{(\xi)}
are introduced in Section
9.2
, and are seen as a periodic function on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Since
\supp M_{l}^{i}\subset\Omega_{q+1}
, we know that
\supp(w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}+w_{q+1}^{(c,i)})\subset\Omega_{q+1}
and so is
v_{q+1}
. Here we note that the building blocks
W_{(\xi)}
and
V_{(\xi)}
are not globally integrable. However, since the amplitude functions are supported in
\Omega_{q+1}
, all estimates involving the building blocks are restricted to the interval
[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d}
. On this domain, the relevant norms coincide with those on the torus, and therefore the same estimates remain valid.

We define the perturbations for the stationary densities functions as

\displaystyle\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}:

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\chi(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta}{\Big)}^{1/{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}\Theta_{(\xi)},

\displaystyle\theta_{q+1}^{(c,i)}:

\displaystyle=-\hat{\rho}^{i}_{q}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x.

and then define

\displaystyle\theta_{q+1}^{i}:=\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}+\theta_{q+1}^{(c,i)},\ \ \rho^{i}_{q+1}:=\rho^{i}_{l}+\theta^{i}_{q+1}.

Here we note that
\Theta_{(\xi)}
is seen as a periodic function on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\hat{\rho}^{i}_{q}
is a probability density with support in
\Omega_{q}
satisfying
\|\hat{\rho}^{i}_{q}\|_{C_{x}^{2}}\lesssim 1.

Since
\supp M_{l}^{i}\subset\Omega_{q+1}
, we know that
\supp(\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}+\theta_{q+1}^{(c,i)})\subset\Omega_{q+1}
and so is
\rho_{q+1}
.
By the definition, it is easy to see that
\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}(\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}+\theta_{q+1}^{(c,i)}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}x=0
, which implies that
\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rho^{i}_{q+1}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x=0
.

Now, we need to establish the corresponding estimates on the perturbations.
The definition of the perturbations are the same as before, except for
\theta_{q+1}^{(c,i)}
. However, it could be bounded as the same manner, since there is only an extra
\hat{\rho}_{q}
. For example,

\displaystyle\|w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}

\displaystyle\|_{L^{m}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\|w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|_{L^{m}([-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\left\|\tilde{\chi}(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}}\right\|_{C_{x}^{0}([-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d})}\|W_{(\xi)}\|_{L^{m}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\lesssim l^{-2d-4}r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{m}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}},

\displaystyle\|w_{q+1}^{(c,i)}

\displaystyle\|_{L^{m}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\|w_{q+1}^{(c,i)}\|_{L^{m}([-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\left\|\tilde{\chi}(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}}\right\|_{C^{1}_{x}([-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d})}\|V_{(\xi)}\|_{L^{m}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\lesssim l^{-3d-8}r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{m}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1},

which are the same as before. All estimates for the perturbations can be obtained by an almost word-for-word repetition of the arguments in Section
9.4
: for
A_{i,n,\xi}:=\chi(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}{\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}
,

\displaystyle\|w_{q+1}^{i}\|_{C_{x}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\left\|\tilde{\chi}(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}}\right\|_{C_{x}^{2}([-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d})}\|V_{(\xi)}\|_{C_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\lesssim l^{-4d-12}\lambda_{q+1}r_{\perp}^{-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}},

\displaystyle\|\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|_{L^{m}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\left\|A_{i,n,\xi}\right\|_{C_{x}^{0}([-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d})}\|\Theta_{(\xi)}\|_{L^{m}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\lesssim l^{-2d-4}r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{m}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}},

\displaystyle\|\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|_{W^{1,1+\epsilon}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\left\|A_{i,n,\xi}\right\|_{C_{x}^{1}([-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d})}\|\Theta_{(\xi)}\|_{W^{1,1+\epsilon}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\lesssim l^{-4d-12}\lambda_{q+1}r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{1+\epsilon}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}},

\displaystyle\|\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|_{C_{x}^{i}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\left\|A_{i,n,\xi}\right\|_{C_{x}^{i}([-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d})}\|\Theta_{(\xi)}\|_{C_{x}^{i}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\lesssim l^{-4d-12}\lambda_{q+1}^{i}r_{\perp}^{-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}},\ i=1,2.

Then the desired estimates on the perturbations are obtained by the same calculations.

As for the stress term, by the same calculation as Section
9.3
, we could define

-{M}^{i}_{q+1}:=M^{i}_{osc}+M^{i}_{nonlin}+M^{i}_{lin}-M^{i}_{com},

where

\displaystyle M^{i}_{nonlin}:

\displaystyle=-\beta^{\prime}(\rho^{i}_{q+1}+\rho^{st})\nabla(\rho^{i}_{q+1}+\rho^{st})+\beta^{\prime}(\rho^{i}_{l}+\rho^{st})\nabla(\rho^{i}_{l}+\rho^{st})

\displaystyle\quad+Eb(\rho_{q+1}^{i}+\rho^{st})(\rho_{q+1}^{i}+\rho^{st})-Eb(\rho_{l}^{i}+\rho^{st})(\rho_{l}^{i}+\rho^{st}),

\displaystyle M^{i}_{lin}:

\displaystyle=v_{l}\theta^{i}_{q+1}+w_{q+1}(\rho^{i}_{l}+\rho^{st}+\theta_{q+1}^{(c,i)})+w_{q+1}^{(c,i)}\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)},

and

\displaystyle\mathord{{\rm div}}M^{i}_{osc}

\displaystyle:=\mathord{{\rm div}}(w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}-M^{i}_{l})

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\nabla{\Big[}\chi(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}{\Big]}{\mathbb{P}}_{\neq 0}(W_{(\xi)}\Theta_{(\xi)})

\displaystyle\ \ +\mathord{{\rm div}}{\Big(}\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\chi(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}-M^{i}_{l}{\Big)}.

Using the inverse divergence operator
\mathcal{B}_{\hat{\rho}_{q}^{i}}
in Proposition
10.2
, we define the oscillation error
M^{i}_{osc}:=M^{i}_{osc,x}+M^{i}_{osc,c}
by

\displaystyle M^{i}_{osc,x}:

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\mathcal{B}_{\hat{\rho}_{q}^{i}}{\Big(}\nabla{\Big[}\chi(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}{\Big]},\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}(W_{(\xi)}\Theta_{(\xi)}){\Big)},

\displaystyle M^{i}_{osc,c}:

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\chi(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}-M^{i}_{l}.

Since
\supp\rho^{i}_{l},\supp\theta_{q+1}^{i},\supp M_{l}^{i}\subset\Omega_{q+1}
, we know that
\supp M_{q+1}^{i}\subset\Omega_{q+1}
.

We estimate the terms appearing in the definition of
M^{i}_{q+1}
separately. For the oscillation error
M^{i}_{{osc}}
, the operator
\mathcal{B}_{\hat{\rho}_{q}^{i}}
satisfies the same estimates as in Theorem
3.2
, so the underlying calculations remain unchanged.

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{osc,x}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n,i}}\left\|\chi(\zeta|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}\right\|_{C_{x}^{2}([-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d})}(r_{\perp}\lambda_{q+1})^{-1}\|W_{(\xi)}\Theta_{(\xi)}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-6d-20}(r_{\perp}\lambda_{q+1})^{-1}\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}\leqslant\frac{1}{5}C_{0}\delta_{q+2}.

The treatment of
M^{i}_{osc,c}
and the linear error
M^{i}_{{lin}}
is identical as before.

For the nonlinear error
M^{i}_{nonlin}
, by the same calculation as in (
5.21
), together with (
10.1
) we have

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{nonlin}\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|\beta^{\prime}\|_{C^{1}}+\|E\|_{C_{x}^{0}([-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d})}\|b\|_{C^{1}})\|\theta^{i}_{q+1}\|_{W^{1,1+\epsilon}}(1+\|\rho^{i}_{l}+\rho^{st}\|_{C_{x}^{1}})

\displaystyle\lesssim(\lambda_{q}^{d+1}+1)\lambda_{q+1}^{-2\alpha}\lesssim C_{0}\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}\leqslant\frac{1}{5}C_{0}\delta_{q+2}.

For the commutator error
M^{i}_{com}
, By the calculation as in (
5.22
), together with (
10.1
) we obtain

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{com}\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim l\|v_{q}\|_{C_{x}^{1}}(\|\rho_{q}^{i}\|_{C_{x}^{1}}+\|\rho^{st}\|_{C_{x}^{1}})

\displaystyle\quad+l(\|\beta^{\prime}\|_{C^{1}}+\|E\|_{C_{x}^{1}([-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d})}\|b\|_{C^{1}})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\times(1+\|\rho_{q}^{i}\|_{C_{x}^{2}}+\|\rho^{st}\|_{W^{2,\infty}}^{2}+\|\rho_{q}^{i}\|_{C_{x}^{1}}^{2})(\|\rho_{q}^{i}\|_{C_{x}^{1}}+\|\rho^{st}\|_{W^{1,\infty}})

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}l\lambda_{q}^{3d+4}\lesssim C_{0}\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}\leqslant\frac{1}{5}C_{0}\delta_{q+2}.

Then the proof is finished.

10.5.
The main iteration used in the proof of Theorem
1.6

Let
0<\epsilon_{0}\leqslant{\mathord{{\rm min}}}\{\frac{1}{8},\frac{1}{2^{d+10}}c_{in}\}
be given. The iteration is indexed by a parameter

q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}.

We consider sequences of parameters

\displaystyle\lambda_{q}=a^{b^{q}},\ q\geqslant 0,\ \delta_{q}=(\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2})^{d+1}\lambda_{1}^{2\beta}\lambda_{q}^{-2\beta},\ q\geqslant 1,\ \delta_{0}=1,

by (
4.1
)
we obtain
\sum_{q\geqslant 1}\delta_{q}^{1/(d+1)}<\epsilon_{0}.

At each step
q
, we construct a family

(v_{q},\rho_{q}^{i},M_{q}^{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}

solving the system

(10.10)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho_{q}^{i}-\mathord{{\rm div}}\bigl(\beta^{\prime}(\rho_{q}^{i}+\rho^{st})\nabla(\rho_{q}^{i}+\rho^{st})\bigr)+\mathord{{\rm div}}(v_{q}(\rho_{q}^{i}+\rho^{st}))+\mathord{{\rm div}}(B(\rho_{q}^{i}+\rho^{st}))

\displaystyle=-\mathord{{\rm div}}M_{q}^{i},

\displaystyle\mathord{{\rm div}}v_{q}

\displaystyle=0.

We recall the notation
T_{q}:=\frac{1}{3}-\sum_{1\leqslant r\leqslant q}\delta_{r}^{1/2}
and initialize the iteration by defining

(v_{0},\rho^{i}_{0},M^{i}_{0})

through

\displaystyle\rho^{i}_{0}(x)

\displaystyle=4^{-i}\mathord{{\rm div}}F,\ \ v_{0}(x)=0,

\displaystyle M^{i}_{0}(x)

\displaystyle=-4^{-i}\partial_{t}F+\beta^{\prime}(\rho_{0}^{i}+\rho^{st})\nabla(\rho_{0}^{i}+\rho^{st})-\beta^{\prime}(\rho^{st})\nabla\rho^{st}-B(\rho_{0}^{i}+\rho^{st})+B(\rho^{st}),

where

F(t,x)
is a smooth non-divergence-free bounded
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
-valued function with support in
[\frac{1}{3},\frac{2}{3}]\times\Omega_{0}
, and satisfying
\|\mathord{{\rm div}}F\|_{C_{t,x}^{0}}=\frac{1}{2}c_{in},\|\mathord{{\rm div}}F\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}\geqslant\frac{1}{2^{d+1}}c_{in}.
In particular,
\supp\rho_{0}^{i},\supp v_{0},\supp M_{0}^{i}\subset[T_{0},1-T_{0}]\times\Omega_{0}
.

By choosing
C_{0}>0
sufficiently large we obtain

\displaystyle\|\rho_{0}^{i}\|_{C_{t,x}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant 4^{-i}C_{0}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}},\ \ \|v_{0}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\leqslant C_{0}^{1/d_{0}},

(10.11)

\displaystyle\|M_{0}^{i}\|_{L_{t}^{1}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|\beta^{\prime}\|_{C^{1}}+\|E\|_{C_{x}^{1}([-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d})}\|b\|_{C^{1}})\|\rho_{0}^{i}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\lesssim 4^{-i}\leqslant(\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2})^{d+1}4^{-i}C_{0}.

With the above assumptions in hand, our main iteration relies on the first step of iteration and reads as follows:

Proposition 10.5
.

Under the assumption of Theorem
1.6
, there exist
d+1>d_{0}>2>d_{0}^{\prime}>1
with
\frac{1}{d_{0}}+\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}=1
and a choice of parameters
a,b,\alpha,\beta
such
that the following holds: Let
(v_{q},\rho^{i}_{q},M^{i}_{q})_{i\in{\mathbb{N}}}
be a solution to the system (
10.10
) satisfying

(10.12)

\displaystyle\|v_{q}\|_{L^{d_{0}}_{t}L^{d_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant C_{v}C_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\sum_{m=0}^{q}\delta_{m}^{1/d_{0}},

for some universal constant
C_{v}\geqslant 1
, and

(10.13)

\displaystyle\|v_{q}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant C_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\lambda_{q}^{d+4},\ 4^{i}\|\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\lambda_{q}^{-2}\|\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{C_{t,x}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant C_{0}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\lambda_{q}^{d+4},

(10.14)

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{q}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant C_{0}2^{-i}\delta_{q+1},

(10.15)

\displaystyle M^{i}_{q}=M^{i}_{0}-v_{q}\rho^{i}_{0},\ \rho_{q}^{i}=\rho_{0}^{i},\ {\rm for\ }i>N_{q},

(10.16)

\displaystyle\supp\rho_{q}^{i},\supp v_{q},\supp M_{q}^{i}\subset[T_{q},1-T_{q}]\times\Omega_{q}.

Then there exists

(v_{q+1},\rho^{i}_{q+1},M^{i}_{q+1})_{i\in{\mathbb{N}}}
which solves (
10.10
) and satisfies (
10.12
)-(
10.16
) at the level
q+1
and

(10.17)

\displaystyle\|v_{q+1}-v_{q}\|_{L^{d_{0}}_{t}L^{d_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant C_{v}C_{0}^{1/d_{0}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}},\ \ \|\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}_{t}L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant C_{v}C_{0}^{1/{d_{0}^{\prime}}}2^{-i/d_{0}^{\prime}}\delta_{q+1}^{1/{d_{0}^{\prime}}},

(10.18)

\displaystyle\|v_{q+1}-v_{q}\|_{L^{r}_{t}L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|v_{q+1}-v_{q}\|_{C_{t}L^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}},

(10.19)

\displaystyle\|\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leqslant 4^{-i}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}},\ \ \inf_{t\in[0,1]}(\rho^{i}_{q+1}-\rho^{i}_{q})\geqslant-\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}.

Proof of Theorem
1.6
.

We intend to start the iteration from

(v_{0},\rho^{i}_{0},M^{i}_{0})_{i\in{\mathbb{N}}}
which are defined as above.
Next, we use Proposition
10.5
to build inductively
(v_{q},\rho^{i}_{q},M^{i}_{q})_{i\in{\mathbb{N}}}
for every
q\geqslant 1
. By (
10.17
)-(
10.19
), the sequence
\{(v_{q},\rho^{i}_{q})_{i\in{\mathbb{N}}}\}_{q\in\mathbb{N}}
is
Cauchy in

{\Big(}L^{r}([0,1];L^{p})\cap L^{d_{0}}([0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap C([0,1];L^{s}){\Big)}\times{\Big(}L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}([0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap C([0,1];L^{1}){\Big)}^{{\mathbb{N}}}

and we denote by
(v,\rho^{i})
its limit. We then define
\overline{\rho}^{i}:=\rho^{i}+\rho^{st}
.

By (
10.17
)-(
10.19
) we have

\displaystyle\|\rho^{i}-\rho_{0}^{i}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\leqslant 4^{-i}\sum_{q=0}^{\infty}\delta_{q+1}^{1/{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\leqslant 4^{-i}\frac{c_{in}}{2^{d+10}},\ \ \|v\|_{L^{r}_{t}L^{p}}+\|v\|_{C_{t}L^{s}}\leqslant\sum_{q\geqslant 0}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}}\leqslant\epsilon_{0},

\displaystyle\inf_{t\in[0,1]}\overline{\rho}^{i}

\displaystyle\geqslant\rho^{st}+\inf_{t\in[0,1]}\rho_{0}^{i}+\sum_{q=0}^{\infty}\inf_{t\in[0,1]}(\rho_{q+1}^{i}-\rho_{q}^{i})\geqslant(c_{in}-\frac{1}{2}c_{in}-\sum_{q=0}^{\infty}\delta_{q+1}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}})1_{[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]}>0,

at which point, together with the fact that
\|\rho^{i}_{0}-\rho_{0}^{j}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}\geqslant|4^{-i}-4^{-j}|\cdot\frac{c_{in}}{2^{d+1}}
, we obtain
\overline{\rho}^{i}\to\rho^{st}
in
C_{t}L^{1}
as
i\to\infty
,

\overline{\rho}^{i}
is a probability density function, and
\overline{\rho}^{i}

do not coincide with each other.
We finish the proof of the first statement.

For the second statement,
Since
|v|\in L_{t}^{d_{0}}L^{d_{0}},\overline{\rho}^{i}\in L_{t}^{d_{0}^{\prime}}L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}
, and

t\mapsto\overline{\rho}^{i}(t)

is continuous on
[0,1]
, we finish the proof using the superposition principle.
∎

10.6.
Proof of Proposition
10.5

We choose the all same parameter as in Section
5
.

First, we mollify the first
N_{q+1}
equations by

\displaystyle v_{l}=(v_{q}*_{x}\phi_{l})*_{t}\varphi_{l},\ \ \rho_{l}^{i}=(\rho_{q}^{i}*_{x}\phi_{l})*_{t}\varphi_{l},\ \ M_{l}^{i}=(M_{q}^{i}*_{x}\phi_{l})*_{t}\varphi_{l},

where

\phi_{l}:=\frac{1}{l^{d}}\phi\bigl(\tfrac{\cdot}{l}\bigr)

is a family of standard radial mollifiers on
[-1,1]^{d}
, and

\varphi_{l}:=\frac{1}{l}\varphi\bigl(\tfrac{\cdot}{l}\bigr)

is a family of standard mollifiers supported in
(0,1)
. By straightforward calculations we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho_{l}^{i}-\mathord{{\rm div}}(\beta^{\prime}(\rho_{l}^{i}+\rho^{st})\nabla(\rho_{l}^{i}+\rho^{st}))+\mathord{{\rm div}}(v_{l}(\rho_{l}^{i}+\rho^{st}))+\mathord{{\rm div}}(B(\rho_{l}^{i}+\rho^{st}))

\displaystyle=-\mathord{{\rm div}}(M^{i}_{l}+M^{i}_{com}),

\displaystyle\mathord{{\rm div}}v_{l}

\displaystyle=0,

where

\displaystyle M^{i}_{com}:=

\displaystyle-v_{l}(\rho^{i}_{l}+\rho^{st})+(v_{q}(\rho^{i}_{q}+\rho^{st}))*\phi_{l}*_{t}\varphi_{l}

\displaystyle+\beta^{\prime}(\rho^{i}_{l}+\rho^{st})\nabla(\rho^{i}_{l}+\rho^{st})-(\beta^{\prime}(\rho^{i}_{q}+\rho^{st})\nabla(\rho^{i}_{q}+\rho^{st}))*_{x}\phi_{l}*_{t}\varphi_{l}

\displaystyle-\beta^{\prime}(\rho^{st})\nabla\rho^{st}+(\beta^{\prime}(\rho^{st})\nabla\rho^{st})*_{x}\phi_{l}*_{t}\varphi_{l}

\displaystyle-B(\rho_{l}^{i}+\rho^{st})+B(\rho_{q}^{i}+\rho^{st})*_{x}\phi_{l}*_{t}\varphi_{l}+B(\rho^{st})-B(\rho^{st})*_{x}\phi_{l}*_{t}\varphi_{l}.

Since
\supp\rho_{q}^{i},\supp v_{q},\supp M_{q}^{i}\subset[T_{q},1-T_{q}]\times\Omega_{q}
, we know that
\supp\rho_{l}^{i},\supp v_{l},\supp M_{l}^{i}\subset[T_{q+1},1-T_{q+1}]\times\Omega_{q+1}
, and then
\supp M_{com}^{i}\subset[T_{q+1},1-T_{q+1}]\times\Omega_{q+1}
.

We next define the perturbations for the drift term.
For
1\leqslant i\leqslant N_{q+1}
, let

\displaystyle w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}:=\sum_{n\geqslant 3}

\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}(\zeta^{i}|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\Bigl(\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Bigr)^{1/d_{0}}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}W_{(\xi,n,i)}g_{(\xi,i,d_{0})},

\displaystyle w_{q+1}^{(c,i)}:=\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\Bigl(

\displaystyle-\tilde{\chi}(\zeta^{i}|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\Bigl(\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Bigr)^{1/d_{0}}\frac{1}{(n_{*}\lambda_{q+1})^{2}}\nabla\Phi_{(\xi,n,i)}\,\xi\cdot\nabla\psi_{(\xi,n,i)}

\displaystyle\quad+\nabla\bigl(\tilde{\chi}(\zeta^{i}|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\bigr)\Bigl(\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Bigr)^{1/d_{0}}:V_{(\xi,n,i)}\Bigr)g_{(\xi,i,d_{0})},

and the total perturbation, the new velocity field by

\displaystyle w_{q+1}:=\sum_{i=1}^{N_{q+1}}\bigl(w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}+w_{q+1}^{(c,i)}\bigr),\qquad v_{q+1}:=v_{l}+w_{q+1}.

Here we note that
W_{(\xi,n,i)},V_{(\xi,n,i)}
are introduced in Section
5.2
, and are seen as a periodic function on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Since
\supp M_{l}^{i}\subset[T_{q+1},1-T_{q+1}]\times\Omega_{q+1}
, we know that
\supp(w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}+w_{q+1}^{(c,i)})\subset[T_{q+1},1-T_{q+1}]\times\Omega_{q+1}
and so is
v_{q+1}
.

We next define the perturbations for the density functions.
For
1\leqslant i\leqslant N_{q+1}
, we set

\displaystyle\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}:=

\displaystyle\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\chi(\zeta^{i}|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\Bigl(\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Bigr)^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\Gamma_{\xi}\Bigl(\frac{M_{l}^{i}}{|M_{l}^{i}|}\Bigr)\Theta_{(\xi,n,i)}g_{(\xi,i,d_{0}^{\prime})},

\displaystyle\theta_{q+1}^{(c,i)}:=

\displaystyle-\hat{\rho}^{i}_{q}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x,

\displaystyle\theta_{q+1}^{(o,i)}:=

\displaystyle-\sigma^{-1}\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}h_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}\mathord{{\rm div}}\Bigl(\chi(\zeta^{i}|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Gamma_{\xi}\Bigl(\frac{M_{l}^{i}}{|M_{l}^{i}|}\Bigr)\xi\Bigr).

We now define, for every
1\leqslant i\leqslant N_{q+1}
,

\displaystyle\theta_{q+1}^{i}:=\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}+\theta_{q+1}^{(c,i)}+\theta_{q+1}^{(o,i)},\qquad\rho_{q+1}^{i}:=\rho_{l}^{i}+\theta_{q+1}^{i}.

For
i>N_{q+1}
, we simply set

\rho_{q+1}^{i}:=\rho_{q}^{i}.

By construction,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{q+1}^{i}\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x=0

for every
i\in\mathbb{N}
.
Since
\supp M_{l}^{i}\subset[T_{q+1},1-T_{q+1}]\times\Omega_{q+1}
, we know that
\supp(\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}+\theta_{q+1}^{(c,i)})\subset[T_{q+1},1-T_{q+1}]\times\Omega_{q+1}
and so is
\rho_{q+1}
.

Now we establish the corresponding estimates on the perturbations.
The definition of the perturbations are also the same as before, except for
\theta_{q+1}^{(c,i)}
.
Here we note that since
\supp_{x}M_{l}^{i}\subset[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d}
, all estimates are restricted to the domain
[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d}
.
We now collect several key estimates in the sequel:

\displaystyle\|w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|_{L^{u}_{t}L^{m}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-2d-4}r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{m}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}}r_{\parallel}^{\frac{1}{m}-\frac{1}{d_{0}}}\eta^{\frac{1}{u}-\frac{1}{d_{0}}},

\displaystyle\|w_{q+1}^{(c,i)}\|_{L^{u}_{t}L^{m}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-3d-8}r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{m}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}}r_{\parallel}^{\frac{1}{m}-\frac{1}{d_{0}}}\frac{r_{\perp}}{r_{\parallel}}\eta^{\frac{1}{u}-\frac{1}{d_{0}}},

\displaystyle\|\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|_{L^{u}_{t}L^{m}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-2d-4}r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{m}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}r_{\parallel}^{\frac{1}{m}-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\eta^{\frac{1}{u}-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}},

\displaystyle\|\theta_{q+1}^{(o,i)}\|_{C_{t}C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma^{-1}l^{-6d-20},

\displaystyle\|\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\|_{L^{1}_{t}W^{1,1+\epsilon}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-4d-12}\lambda_{q+1}r_{\parallel}^{\frac{1}{1+\epsilon}-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{1+\epsilon}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\eta^{1-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}.

Then the desired estimates on the perturbations are obtained by the same calculations.

As for the stress term, by the same calculation as Section
5.4
, we could define for
1\leqslant i\leqslant N_{q+1}
,

-{M}^{i}_{q+1}:=M^{i}_{osc}+M^{i}_{nonlin}+M^{i}_{lin}-M^{i}_{com},

where

\displaystyle M^{i}_{nonlin}:

\displaystyle=-\beta^{\prime}(\rho^{i}_{q+1}+\rho^{st})\nabla(\rho^{i}_{q+1}+\rho^{st})+\beta^{\prime}(\rho^{i}_{l}+\rho^{st})\nabla(\rho^{i}_{l}+\rho^{st})

\displaystyle\quad-Eb(\rho_{q+1}^{i}+\rho^{st})(\rho_{q+1}^{i}+\rho^{st})+Eb(\rho_{l}^{i}+\rho^{st})(\rho_{l}^{i}+\rho^{st}),

\displaystyle M^{i}_{lin}:

\displaystyle=v_{l}\theta^{i}_{q+1}+w_{q+1}(\rho^{i}_{l}+\rho^{st}+\theta_{q+1}^{(c,i)}+\theta_{q+1}^{(o,i)})+w_{q+1}^{(c,i)}\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}.

To define the oscillation error, we define

\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0,\hat{\rho}^{i}_{q}}f:=f-\hat{\rho}^{i}_{q}\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x
.
In particular, for any mean-zero function
f
, we have

\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0,\hat{\rho}^{i}_{q}}f=f
. Then, we have

\displaystyle M^{i}_{osc}:=

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\theta^{i}_{q+1}+\mathord{{\rm div}}(w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}-M^{i}_{l})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0,\hat{\rho}^{i}_{q}}(\partial_{t}\theta^{(p,i)}_{q+1}+\mathord{{\rm div}}(w_{q+1}^{(p,i)}\theta_{q+1}^{(p,i)}-M^{i}_{l})+\partial_{t}\theta^{(o,i)}_{q+1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0,\hat{\rho}^{i}_{q}}\left(\partial_{t}\Bigl[\chi(\zeta^{i}|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\Bigl(\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Bigr)^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\Gamma_{\xi}\Bigl(\frac{M_{l}^{i}}{|M_{l}^{i}|}\Bigr)g_{(\xi,i,d_{0}^{\prime})}\Bigr]\Theta_{(\xi,n,i)}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0,\hat{\rho}^{i}_{q}}\left(\nabla\Bigl[\chi(\zeta^{i}|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Gamma_{\xi}\Bigl(\frac{M_{l}^{i}}{|M_{l}^{i}|}\Bigr)\Bigr]g_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}g_{(\xi,i,d_{0}^{\prime})}\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}\bigl(W_{(\xi,n,i)}\Theta_{(\xi,n,i)}\bigr)\right)

\displaystyle+\mathord{{\rm div}}\Bigl(\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\chi(\zeta^{i}|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\frac{M_{l}^{i}}{|M_{l}^{i}|}-M_{l}^{i}\Bigr)

\displaystyle-\sigma^{-1}\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}h_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}\partial_{t}\mathord{{\rm div}}\Bigl(\chi(\zeta^{i}|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Gamma_{\xi}\Bigl(\frac{M_{l}^{i}}{|M_{l}^{i}|}\Bigr)\xi\Bigr).

Now, we apply the Bogovskii operators
\mathcal{B}_{\hat{\rho}_{q}^{i}},\mathcal{R}_{\hat{\rho}_{q}^{i}}
in Section
10.1
to define
M^{i}_{osc}:=M^{i}_{osc,t}+M^{i}_{osc,x}+M^{i}_{osc,c}+M^{i}_{osc,o}
as

\displaystyle M^{i}_{osc,t}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\mathcal{B}_{\hat{\rho}_{q}^{i}}{\Big(}\partial_{t}[\chi(\zeta^{i}|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}g_{(\xi,i,d_{0}^{\prime})}]\Theta_{(\xi,n,i)}{\Big)},

\displaystyle M^{i}_{osc,x}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\mathcal{R}_{\hat{\rho}_{q}^{i}}{\Big(}\nabla[\chi(\zeta^{i}|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}],\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}(W_{(\xi,n,i)}\Theta_{(\xi,n,i)}){\Big)}g_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}g_{(\xi,i,d_{0}^{\prime})},

\displaystyle M^{i}_{osc,c}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\chi(\zeta^{i}|M^{i}_{l}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}-M^{i}_{l},

\displaystyle M^{i}_{osc,o}

\displaystyle:=-\sigma^{-1}\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}h_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}\partial_{t}{\Big(}\chi(\zeta|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M_{l}^{i}}{|M_{l}^{i}|}{\Big)}\xi{\Big)}.

Since
\supp\rho^{i}_{l},\supp\theta_{q+1}^{i},\supp M_{l}^{i}\subset[T_{q+1},1-T_{q+1}]\times\Omega_{q+1}
, we know that
\supp M_{q+1}^{i}\subset[T_{q+1},1-T_{q+1}]\times\Omega_{q+1}
.

We now estimate each term in the definition of
M_{q+1}^{i}
separately for
1\leqslant i\leqslant N_{q+1}
.

For the oscillation error
M^{i}_{osc,t}
, the operator
\mathcal{R}_{\hat{\rho}_{q}^{i}}
satisfies the same estimates as in Theorem
3.2
, so the underlying calculations remain unchanged.

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{osc,t}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\left\|\chi(\zeta^{i}|M^{i}_{l}|-n){\Big(}\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}{\Big)}^{1/d_{0}^{\prime}}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}\right\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\|g_{(\xi,i,d_{0}^{\prime})}\|_{W_{t}^{1,1}}\|\Theta_{(\xi,n,i)}\|_{C_{t}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim l^{-4d-12}r_{\perp}^{d-1-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}r_{\parallel}^{1-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\sigma\eta^{-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\lesssim 2^{-i}\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}.

By (
A.9
) and the bounds above we have

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{osc,o}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma^{-1}\sum_{n\geqslant 3}\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda^{n}}\left\|\chi(\zeta|M_{l}^{i}|-n)\frac{n}{\zeta^{i}}\Gamma_{\xi}{\Big(}\frac{M^{i}_{l}}{|M^{i}_{l}|}{\Big)}\xi\right\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}\lesssim\sigma^{-1}l^{-4d-12}\lesssim 2^{-i}\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}.

For the nonlinear error
M_{nonlin}^{i}
, similar to (
5.21
), we have

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{nonlin}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|\beta^{\prime}\|_{C^{1}}+\|E\|_{C_{x}^{0}([-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d})}\|b\|_{C^{1}})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times(\|\theta^{i}_{q+1}\|_{C_{t}L^{1+\epsilon}}+\|\theta^{i}_{q+1}\|_{L^{1}_{t}W^{1,1+\epsilon}})(1+\|\rho^{i}_{q}+\rho^{st}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}})

\displaystyle\lesssim(\lambda_{q}^{d+4}+1)2^{-i}\lambda_{q+1}^{-2\alpha}\lesssim 2^{-i}C_{0}\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha}\leqslant\frac{1}{5}C_{0}2^{-i}\delta_{q+2}.

For the commutator error
M^{i}_{com}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{com}\|_{L_{t}^{1}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim l\|v_{q}\|_{C_{x}^{1}}(\|\rho_{q}^{i}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}+\|\rho^{st}\|_{C_{x}^{1}})

\displaystyle\quad+l(\|\beta^{\prime}\|_{C^{1}}+\|E\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}([-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d})}\|b\|_{C^{1}})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\times(1+\|\rho_{q}^{i}\|_{C_{t,x}^{2}}+\|\rho_{q}^{i}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}^{2}+\|\rho^{st}\|_{W^{2,\infty}}^{2})(\|\rho_{q}^{i}\|_{C_{t,x}^{1}}+\|\rho^{st}\|_{W^{1,\infty}})

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}l\lambda_{q}^{3d+12}\lesssim 2^{-i}C_{0}\lambda_{q+1}^{-\alpha/2}\leqslant\frac{1}{5}C_{0}2^{-i}\delta_{q+2}.

For the case
i>N_{q+1}
, since
\supp_{x}v_{q+1},\supp_{x}v_{q}\subset[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d}
and
\rho^{st}=g^{-1}(-\Phi(x)+\mu^{st})
is constant on
[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d}
, we obtain that

\mathord{{\rm div}}((v_{q+1}-v_{q})\rho^{st})=0.
Together with
\rho_{q+1}^{i}=\rho_{q}^{i}=\rho_{0}^{i},

we define

\displaystyle M_{q+1}^{i}:=M_{q}^{i}-(v_{q+1}-v_{q})\rho_{0}^{i}=M_{0}^{i}-v_{q+1}\rho_{0}^{i}.

Since
\supp\rho^{i}_{0},\supp M_{0}^{i}\subset[T_{q+1},1-T_{q+1}]\times\Omega_{q+1}
, we know that
\supp M_{q+1}^{i}\subset[T_{q+1},1-T_{q+1}]\times\Omega_{q+1}
.

In the end, by (
10.11
) and (
10.12
) we have

\displaystyle\|M^{i}_{q+1}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{1}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|M^{i}_{0}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{1}}+\|v_{q+1}\|_{L^{d_{0}}_{t}L^{d_{0}}}\|\rho^{i}_{0}\|_{L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}_{t}L^{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\lesssim C_{0}C_{v}4^{-i}\leqslant C_{0}2^{-i}\delta_{q+2}.

Prof. Xiangchan Zhu for their invaluable discussions, insightful comments, and
generous support. Their suggestions have greatly improved both the mathematical
content and the presentation of this work.

Appendix A A
L^{d_{0}}
-based intermittent spatial-time jets

In this section we recall the
L^{d_{0}}
-based intermittent spatial-time jets as presented in
[
LRZ25
, Appendix C.1]
.

First we introduce the following geometrical lemma:

Lemma A.1
.

[
BCDL21
, Lemma 3.1]

Let
d\geqslant 2
. There exists a finite set
\Lambda\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\cap\mathbb{Q}^{d}
and non-negative
C^{\infty}
-function
\Gamma_{\xi}:\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\to\mathbb{R}
such
that for every
R\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}

R=\sum_{\xi\in\Lambda}\Gamma_{\xi}(R)\xi.

With Lemma
A.1
in hand, it is easy to generate
2
disjoint families
\Lambda^{1},\Lambda^{2}
, where each one enjoys the property of Lemma
A.1
by taking suitable rational rotations of one fixed set. For simplicity, we denote
\Lambda:=\Lambda^{1}\cup\Lambda^{2}
. Moreover, we know that
\{\Gamma_{\xi}\}_{\xi\in\Lambda}
are uniformly bounded.

For parameters
\lambda,r_{\perp},r_{\parallel}>0
, we assume

\lambda^{-1}\ll r_{\perp}\ll r_{\parallel}\ll 1,\ \ \lambda r_{\perp}\in\mathbb{N}.

For each
\xi\in\Lambda
let us define
A^{i}_{\xi}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\cap\mathbb{Q}^{d},\ i=1,2,...,d-1
, such that
\{\xi,A^{i}_{\xi},i=1,...,d-1\}

form an orthonormal basis in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Let
n_{*}\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that
\{n_{*}\xi,n_{*}A^{i}_{\xi},i=1,...,d-1\}\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d}

for every
\xi\in\Lambda
.
We define
\phi:\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\to\mathbb{R}
be a smooth function with support in a ball of radius 1,
\phi\equiv 1
on
B(0,\frac{1}{3})
and mean-zero. We define
\Phi
such that
\phi=-\Delta\Phi
. Let
\psi:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be a smooth, mean-zero function with
support in
B(0,1)
satisfying
\psi\equiv 1
on
B(0,\frac{1}{3})
.
Define
\phi^{\prime}:\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\to\mathbb{R}
to be a smooth non-negative function with support in
B(0,\frac{1}{3})
satisfying
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\phi^{\prime}(x_{1},x_{2},...,x_{d-1}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}x_{1}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x_{2}..{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x_{d-1}=1,

and let
\psi^{\prime}:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be a smooth non-negative function with support in
B(0,\frac{1}{3})
such that

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi^{\prime}(x_{d}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}x_{d}=1.

Let
d_{0}\geqslant 1
be fixed, we define the rescaled cut-off functions

\phi_{r_{\perp},{d_{0}}}(x_{1},x_{2},...,x_{d-1})=\frac{1}{r_{\perp}^{(d-1)/{d_{0}}}}\phi(\frac{x_{1}}{r_{\perp}},\frac{x_{2}}{r_{\perp}},...,\frac{x_{d-1}}{r_{\perp}}),

\Phi_{r_{\perp},{d_{0}}}(x_{1},x_{2},...,x_{d-1})=\frac{1}{r_{\perp}^{(d-1)/{d_{0}}}}\Phi(\frac{x_{1}}{r_{\perp}},\frac{x_{2}}{r_{\perp}},...,\frac{x_{d-1}}{r_{\perp}}),

\psi_{r_{\parallel},{d_{0}}}(x_{d})=\frac{1}{r_{\parallel}^{1/{d_{0}}}}\psi(\frac{x_{d}}{r_{\parallel}}).

Similarly, for a conjugate exponent
d_{0}^{\prime}\in[1,\infty]
, we define
\phi^{\prime}_{r_{\perp},d_{0}^{\prime}}
and
\psi^{\prime}_{r_{\parallel},d_{0}^{\prime}}
as the same manner. Then we periodize
\phi_{r_{\perp},v},\Phi_{r_{\perp},{d_{0}}},

\psi_{r_{\parallel},{d_{0}}}
,
\phi^{\prime}_{r_{\perp},d_{0}^{\prime}}
and
\psi^{\prime}_{r_{\parallel},{d_{0}}^{\prime}}
so that they can be viewed as functions on
\mathbb{T}^{d-1}
and
\mathbb{T}
respectively.
Consider a large time oscillation parameter
\mu=r_{\perp}^{-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}}r_{\parallel}^{-\frac{1}{d_{0}}}>0
. For every
\xi\in\Lambda
we introduce

\psi_{(\xi,d_{0})}(t,x):=\psi_{r_{\parallel},d_{0}}(n_{*}r_{\perp}\lambda(x\cdot\xi-\mu t)),

\Phi_{(\xi,d_{0})}(x):=\Phi_{r_{\perp},d_{0}}(n_{*}r_{\perp}\lambda x\cdot A^{1}_{\xi},...,n_{*}r_{\perp}\lambda x\cdot A^{d-1}_{\xi}),

\phi_{(\xi,d_{0})}(x):=\phi_{r_{\perp},d_{0}}(n_{*}r_{\perp}\lambda x\cdot A^{1}_{\xi},...,n_{*}r_{\perp}\lambda x\cdot A^{d-1}_{\xi}).

Similarly we define
\phi^{\prime}_{(\xi,d_{0}^{\prime})}
and
\psi^{\prime}_{(\xi,d_{0}^{\prime})}
.

The building blocks
W_{(\xi,d_{0})}:\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d},\Theta_{(\xi,d_{0}^{\prime})}:\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
are defined as

W_{(\xi,d_{0})}(t,x):=\xi\psi_{(\xi,d_{0})}(t,x)\phi_{(\xi,d_{0})}(x),

\Theta_{(\xi,d_{0}^{\prime})}(t,x):=\psi^{\prime}_{(\xi,d_{0}^{\prime})}(t,x)\phi^{\prime}_{(\xi,d_{0}^{\prime})}(x).

By the definition and the choice of
\mu
we have that

(A.1)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}W_{(\xi,d_{0})}\Theta_{(\xi,d_{0}^{\prime})}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}x=\xi,

(A.2)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Theta_{(\xi,d_{0}^{\prime})}+\mathord{{\rm div}}(W_{(\xi,d_{0})}\Theta_{(\xi,d_{0}^{\prime})})=0.

Since
W_{(\xi,d_{0})}
is not divergence-free, we
introduce the skew-symmetric corrector term

\displaystyle V_{(\xi,d_{0})}:=\frac{1}{(n_{*}\lambda)^{2}}(\xi\otimes\nabla\Phi_{(\xi,d_{0})}-\nabla\Phi_{(\xi,d_{0})}\otimes\xi)\psi_{(\xi,d_{0})}

satisfying

(A.3)

\displaystyle\mathord{{\rm div}}V_{(\xi,d_{0})}=W_{(\xi,d_{0})}-\frac{1}{(n_{*}\lambda)^{2}}\nabla\Phi_{(\xi,d_{0})}\xi\cdot\nabla\psi_{(\xi,d_{0})}.

Finally, we obtain that for
N,M\geqslant 0
and
p\in[1,\infty]
the
following holds

(A.4)

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{N}\partial_{t}^{M}\psi_{(\xi,d_{0})}\|_{C_{t}L^{p}}\lesssim r_{\parallel}^{\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{d_{0}}}(\frac{r_{\perp}\lambda}{r_{\parallel}})^{N}(\frac{r_{\perp}\lambda\mu}{r_{\parallel}})^{M},

(A.5)

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{N}\phi_{(\xi,d_{0})}\|_{L^{p}}+\|\nabla^{N}\Phi_{(\xi,d_{0})}\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{p}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}}\lambda^{N},

(A.6)

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{N}\partial_{t}^{M}W_{(\xi,d_{0})}\|_{C_{t}L^{p}}+\lambda\|\nabla^{N}\partial_{t}^{M}V_{(\xi,d_{0})}\|_{C_{t}L^{p}}\lesssim r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{p}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}}}r_{\parallel}^{\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{d_{0}}}\lambda^{N}(\frac{r_{\perp}\lambda\mu}{r_{\parallel}})^{M},

(A.7)

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{N}\partial_{t}^{M}\Theta_{(\xi,d_{0}^{\prime})}\|_{C_{t}L^{p}}\lesssim r_{\perp}^{\frac{d-1}{p}-\frac{d-1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}r_{\parallel}^{\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}}\lambda^{N}(\frac{r_{\perp}\lambda\mu}{r_{\parallel}})^{M},

where the implicit constants may depend on
p,N
and
M
, but are independent of
\lambda,r_{\perp},r_{\parallel},\mu
. These estimates can be easily deduced from the definitions.

Then let us introduce a family of temporal functions to oscillate the building blocks intermittently in time.
Let
K\in{\mathbb{N}}
be fixed, and
G\in C_{c}^{\infty}(0,1)
be non-negative and

\int_{0}^{1}G^{2}(t){\mathord{{\rm d}}}t=1.

Let
\eta>0
be a small constant satisfying
\eta K\ll 1
. For
\xi\in\Lambda
as defined above, and
1\leqslant i\leqslant K,
we define
\tilde{g}_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}:\mathbb{T}\to\mathbb{R}
as the 1-periodic extension of
\eta^{-1/d_{0}}G(\frac{t-t_{(\xi,i)}}{\eta})
, where
t_{(\xi,i)}
are chosen so that
\tilde{g}_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}
have disjoint supports for distinct
(\xi,i)
.
We will also oscillate the perturbations at a large
frequency
\sigma\in\mathbb{N}
. So, we define

g_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}(t)=\tilde{g}_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}(\sigma t).

Similarly we define
\tilde{g}_{(\xi,i,d_{0}^{\prime})}
and
g_{(\xi,i,d_{0}^{\prime})}
.

For the corrector term we define
H_{(\xi,i,d_{0})},h_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}:\mathbb{T}\to\mathbb{R}
by

(A.8)

\displaystyle H_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}(t)=\int_{0}^{t}g_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}(s){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s,\ \ h_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}(t)=\int_{0}^{\sigma t}(\tilde{g}_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}(s)\tilde{g}_{(\xi,i,d_{0}^{\prime})}(s)-1){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s,

where we recall that
\frac{1}{d_{0}^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{d_{0}}=1
, In view of the zero-mean condition for
\tilde{g}_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}(t)\tilde{g}_{(\xi,i,d_{0}^{\prime})}(t)-1
, we see that
h_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}
is
\mathbb{T}/\sigma
-periodic, and for any
N\geqslant 0,p\geqslant 1

(A.9)

\displaystyle\|g_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}\|_{W^{N,p}}\lesssim(\frac{\sigma}{\eta})^{N}\eta^{1/p-1/d_{0}},\ \ \|h_{(\xi,i,d_{0})}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leqslant 1,

where the universal constant is independent of the choices of
i
and
\xi
.
